# Build notes (binding overrides and additions to SPEC.md)

These notes override SPEC.md where they differ. Read SPEC.md first, then these.

## Versions (verified on cdnjs today)
- PDF.js 3.11.174 UMD: `https://cdnjs.cloudflare.com/ajax/libs/pdf.js/3.11.174/pdf.min.js` and `.../pdf.worker.min.js` (global `pdfjsLib`).
- KaTeX 0.16.47: `https://cdnjs.cloudflare.com/ajax/libs/KaTeX/0.16.47/katex.min.js` and `katex.min.css` (global `katex`).
- Google Fonts: `https://fonts.googleapis.com/css2?family=Outfit:wght@400;500;700&display=swap`.
- Node 23.7 runs the tests: `node --test` from the `marginalia/` folder (Node 23 rejects a bare directory argument). No npm packages anywhere.

## Vision INSTRUCTION (verbatim; use exactly this string in js/vision.js)
Transcribe every piece of mathematics in this image into LaTeX exactly as written: same symbols, order, subscripts, superscripts, primes, hats, vectors and alignment. Do not simplify, solve, complete, correct or annotate anything. Return ONLY the LaTeX source: no prose, no explanation, no code fences, and no $ / \( / \[ delimiters. Use standard amsmath notation (\frac, \sqrt, \int, \sum, \partial, \nabla, \hbar, \left( \right), \mathbf, \vec, \hat). Leave out equation numbers such as (1.12). Put independent equations on separate lines; use \begin{aligned} ... \end{aligned} with \\ and & only for a derivation that is aligned across lines in the image. If the image contains no mathematical notation (a diagram, figure, table of words or plain prose), return exactly the single token NO_MATH.

## CSS ownership (replaces the single styles.css of SPEC §10)
`styles.css` holds only: the §13 tokens (light + both dark blocks), base element styles, `.btn`, `.btn--primary`, `.btn--danger`, `.btn--ghost`, `.kbd`, `.is-hidden`, `.chip`, form controls, the screen containers (`#library`, `#reader`, `#deck`, and since D27 `#stats`, shown one at a time via `.is-hidden`; since D30 also `--tabs-h` and how the screens share the viewport with the tab strip, see "Tabs"), the reader structure of §2 (`#toolbar` height, `#scroller` flex row, gutter, `#pages`, `#margin` width) and the three breakpoint classes `.reader--wide`, `.reader--rail`, `.reader--stack` that `reader.js` sets on `#reader`; and the defaults of the shared `M.markup` output that every surface uses (`.math-display`, `.tex`, `.cloze-blank`, `.cloze-reveal`, `.cloze-cue`, `.att`), which the modules' files refine.
Every UI module owns the CSS of the DOM it creates, in `css/<module>.css`:
`css/app.css` (toasts, confirm, banner, shortcut sheet, the `#tabLock` "open in another tab" notice), `css/library.css` (home with its folders and the "Move to folder…" picker, the desktop app's welcome card `.lib-welcome`, deck page, settings panel with the Data tab's "Automatic backup" block, export/import dialog), `css/reader.css` (toolbar, page column, `.page`, `.hlLayer`, `.hl*`, `.textLayer`, `.dimLayer`, adder, find bar, page box, resume-review pill, "no text layer" chip), `css/margin.css` (cards, glyphs, chips, connectors, rail, markers, popover, bottom sheet), `css/editor.css` (editor, preview, lint line, buttons, the split note, the Angles button and its picker, the "Similar prompt" line), `css/list.css` (prompt list drawer rows, search, filter chips, the "≈" line), `css/review.css` (overlay, starburst, context line, question/answer, grading bar, Flag / Delete tools, struggling line, end screen), `css/snip.css` (snip overlay, `.snip-rect`, snip toast contents, thumbnail, mono LaTeX line), `css/stats.css` (the statistics page `#stats`: header, the two filters and the totals line, the "Coming due" columns with their hover line and list, the table of prompts; its one token `--stats-bar`), `css/tabs.css` (the tab strip `#tabs`, D30: see "Tabs"), `css/desktop.css` (`#cover`, "Marginalia is now an app": see "Desktop app (binding)", part 2).
`index.html` links, in order: Google Fonts, katex.min.css, `styles.css`, then `css/app.css`, `css/library.css`, `css/reader.css`, `css/margin.css`, `css/editor.css`, `css/list.css`, `css/review.css`, `css/snip.css`, `css/stats.css`, `css/tabs.css`, `css/desktop.css`. Each file must exist (an owner that needs no CSS still writes a comment-only file).
The PDF.js text layer needs these rules (put them in css/reader.css): `.textLayer{position:absolute;inset:0;overflow:hidden;line-height:1;text-align:initial;opacity:1;z-index:2}` `.textLayer span,.textLayer br{color:transparent;position:absolute;white-space:pre;cursor:text;transform-origin:0 0}` `.textLayer ::selection{background:rgba(47,111,224,.35)}` `.textLayer .endOfContent{display:block;position:absolute;inset:100% 0 0;z-index:-1;cursor:default;user-select:none}` `.textLayer.selecting .endOfContent{top:0}`.

## index.html skeleton (the html agent writes it; every other agent builds its DOM inside these hosts)
```
<div id="app">
  <div id="banner" class="is-hidden"></div>
  <div id="tabs" class="is-hidden"></div>     <!-- tabs.js (the tab strip, D30) -->
  <section id="library"></section>              <!-- library.js renders the home screen here -->
  <section id="deck" class="is-hidden"></section> <!-- library.js renders the deck page here -->
  <section id="stats" class="is-hidden"></section> <!-- stats.js renders the statistics page here (D27) -->
  <section id="reader" class="is-hidden" tabindex="-1">
    <div id="toolbar"></div>                    <!-- reader.js -->
    <div id="scroller">
      <div class="gutter"></div>
      <div id="pages"></div>                    <!-- pdfview.js -->
      <div id="margin"></div>                   <!-- margin.js -->
    </div>
    <div id="adder" class="is-hidden"></div>    <!-- reader.js -->
    <div id="find" class="is-hidden"></div>     <!-- reader.js -->
    <div id="snipOverlay" class="is-hidden"></div> <!-- snip.js -->
    <div id="sheet" class="is-hidden"></div>    <!-- margin.js (stack mode) -->
    <div id="list" class="is-hidden"></div>     <!-- list.js (drawer) -->
  </section>
  <div id="review" class="is-hidden" tabindex="-1"></div>  <!-- review.js -->
  <div id="settings" class="is-hidden"></div>   <!-- library.js -->
  <div id="shortcuts" class="is-hidden"></div>  <!-- app.js (the ? sheet) -->
  <div id="toasts"></div>                       <!-- app.js -->
  <div id="confirm" class="is-hidden"></div>    <!-- app.js -->
  <div id="cover" class="is-hidden"></div>      <!-- desktop.js ("Marginalia is now an app") -->
  <input id="fileInput" type="file" accept="application/pdf,.pdf" hidden>
</div>
```
Each owner fills its host with `innerHTML`/DOM calls on mount; nobody touches another owner's host.

## Decisions on the spec's open points
- `?` opens the shortcut sheet in the reader AND in review (fixed, not configurable).
- Review session: `MAX_ASKS = 3` per card per session as in §7; D9's "no cap" refers to session length.
- A `Q.`-only prompt is a note (§4); a highlight or note never enters any queue.
- Test affordance: `M.library.addPdfFromUrl(url)` fetches a PDF and runs the same path as `addPdf(file)`; at boot `M.app` reads `?pdf=<url>` from `location.search` (same-origin or relative URLs only) and calls it once. The library home also shows a small "Open sample PDF" link ONLY when `location.hostname` is `127.0.0.1` or `localhost` (it loads `test/sample.pdf`).
- `M.app.isArtifact()` is true when `<meta name="marginalia-build" content="artifact">` is present.
- Settings panel tabs: Appearance, Review shortcuts, LaTeX from snips, Obsidian (see "Obsidian" below), Data (export/import, the "Automatic backup" block (see "Automatic backup" below), storage usage; "Forget key" lives under LaTeX).

## Artifact page contract (tools/bundle.py --artifact)
The claude.ai Artifact file is NOT a full document. `--artifact` output must be a fragment: `<title>Marginalia</title>` first, then every `<style>` block, then the `#app` markup, then the `<script>` tags (cdnjs scripts kept as `<script src>`; local scripts inlined). No `<!doctype>`, `<html>`, `<head>`, `<body>`, no `<meta charset>`/viewport (the platform adds them). Keep `<meta name="marginalia-build" content="artifact">` inside the fragment (it is allowed in the body). Keep the Google Fonts `<link>`; inline katex.min.css with fonts as data URIs. Local build (`dist/marginalia.html`) stays a full document.

## Coding rules (every agent)
- Classic scripts only, `"use strict"` inside the IIFE of §17.1, ES2019 syntax is fine (no modules, no optional chaining needed but allowed, no top-level await). Chrome and Safari.
- Never call `alert`, `confirm`, `prompt`. Never use `innerHTML` with unescaped user text: pass through `M.util.escapeHtml` or `M.markup.render`.
- Every clipboard, localStorage, IndexedDB and `navigator.storage` call is wrapped in try/catch or `.catch`. The localStorage keys are `marginalia.settings`, `marginalia.apiKey`, the editor drafts `marginalia.draft.*`, the pasted-image placeholder ledger `marginalia.placeholder.*`, `marginalia.tab` ("One writing tab per origin"), `marginalia.backup` ("Automatic backup"), `marginalia.openFolders` ("Library folders") and `marginalia.welcomeDismissed` ("Desktop app (binding)", part 2).
- No external dependencies beyond pdf.js and katex. No network calls except the vision backends and the same-origin `/api/obsidian/*` and `/api/backup*` endpoints (`/api/backup`, `/api/backup/latest`, and for the PDFs `/api/backup/pdfs` and `/api/backup/pdf[/<sha256>]`) and `GET /api/app` (once at boot, only from a browser page on 127.0.0.1 or localhost) of Marginalia's own local server (never asked in the artifact build or from `file://`, and `/api/backup*` not in the store's memory mode either; see "Obsidian", "Automatic backup" and "Desktop app (binding)").
- Keep each file self-contained; talk to other modules only through the §17 signatures and §17.3 events.
- Every file ends with a short "SELF-CHECK" comment listing the §17 signatures it implements.

## Key routing (binding; adds to SPEC §3 and §17)
Bindings are strings in the canonical form of §7; a map entry's `binding` may also be an ARRAY of strings (several keys for one action). The prefix `Mod+` means `Meta+` on macOS and `Ctrl+` elsewhere; `M.keys.parse` expands it (`navigator.platform`/`userAgent` sniff, Meta when unknown). `M.keys.normalize(ev)`: single letters are lower-cased and keep `Shift+`; when `ev.altKey` and `ev.code` is `KeyA`…`KeyZ`, the letter comes from `ev.code` (macOS Option produces symbols); for other single characters (`?`, `,`, `=`, `-`, `+`) the character is used as-is and `Shift` is NOT added; `" "` → `Space`.

`M.keys.READER_MAP` (action id → {binding, inField:false}): prompt "n", cloze "c", highlight "h", obsidianNote "o" (see "Obsidian"), next "j", prev "k", edit "e", toggleAnswer "Enter", goToPassage "g", flag "f", delete ["Backspace","Delete"], undoDelete "z", hideAnswers "a", markers "m", dim "d", list "l", find "Mod+f", pageBox "p", snip "s", snipMode "Shift+s", escape "Escape", reviewBook "r", reviewAll "Shift+r", reviewToday "t", markChapter "Shift+c", zoomIn ["Mod+=","Mod++"], zoomOut "Mod+-", zoomFit "Mod+0", openPdf "Mod+o", shortcuts "?", settings ",".
`M.keys.EDITOR_MAP` (inField:true): save "Mod+Enter", saveAnother "Mod+Shift+Enter", cancel "Escape", wrapCloze "Alt+Shift+c". (Tab / Shift+Tab are handled inside editor.js, not through the map.)
`M.keys.TABS_MAP` (inField:true; D30, see "Tabs"): newTab "Mod+t", closeTab "Mod+w", reopenTab "Mod+Shift+t", nextTab ["Ctrl+Tab", "Mod+}"], prevTab ["Ctrl+Shift+Tab", "Mod+{"], tab1 … tab8 "Mod+1" … "Mod+8", lastTab "Mod+9". (`Mod+}` and `Mod+{` are Cmd+Shift+] and Cmd+Shift+[: `normalize` gives a shifted symbol as it is, without `Shift+`.)
`M.keys.UNDO_MAP` (inField:false; D33, see "Undo and redo"): undo "Mod+z", redo "Mod+Shift+z".
`M.keys.REVIEW_ACTIONS` = ["showAnswer","forgot","remembered","undo","skip","goToSource","flag","delete","exit"] (flag and delete: "Prompt craft"); `M.keys.PRESETS` as §7 plus flag "f" and delete "Backspace" in each; `settings.reviewKeys` = {actionId: binding} (Default preset by default).

`M.app` installs the ONE document keydown listener; after the lock, the ? sheet and a confirm question it asks `M.tabs.handleKey(ev)` first (D30, "Tabs"), then `M.keys.UNDO_MAP` (D33, "Undo and redo"), then routes by focus owner (while the "open in another tab" notice is up it also holds capture-phase guards on window and nothing below routes; see "One writing tab per origin"). By focus owner:
- `reader` / `card`: `M.keys.dispatch(ev, [READER_MAP])` → action id, then routed: `M.snip.run(id)` first (snip, snipMode, escape while armed), then `M.reader.run(id)` (prompt, cloze, highlight, obsidianNote, find, pageBox, zoomIn, zoomOut, zoomFit, markChapter, dim, hideAnswers, markers, list, escape), then `M.margin.run(id)` (next, prev, edit, toggleAnswer, goToPassage, flag, delete, undoDelete, escape), then app itself (reviewBook, reviewAll, reviewToday, openPdf, shortcuts, settings, escape closes the shortcut sheet/settings). Each `run(id)` returns true when it handled the action; the first true wins and `preventDefault()` is called.
- `editor`: `M.keys.dispatch(ev, [EDITOR_MAP])` → `M.margin.activeEditor().run(id)` (editor.js implements `Editor.run(id)`); in deck batch mode `M.library.activeEditor().run(id)`.
- `review`: a map built from `M.settings.get("reviewKeys")` → `M.review.reveal() | grade("forgot") | grade("remembered") | undo() | skip() | goToSource() | flag() | remove() | stop()`; plus "?" → shortcut sheet.
- `library` / `settings` / `list`: openPdf, shortcuts, settings, escape (closes list/settings/sheet), reviewAll "Shift+r", reviewToday "t"; in `list`, ↑↓/Enter are handled inside list.js.
Extra signatures this implies: `M.reader.run(id) → boolean`, `M.margin.run(id) → boolean`, `M.snip.run(id) → boolean`, `M.margin.closePopover()`, `M.list.isOpen() → boolean`, `M.library.closeSettings()`, `M.library.activeEditor() → Editor|null`, `M.app.startReview(scope, collectionId|null)`, `M.app.openShortcuts()`, `Editor.run(id) → boolean`, `M.margin.focusedId() → id|null`.
`Mod+V` is never a keydown action: `paste` events do the work (editor.js inside the editor via `M.attachments.handlePaste(ev, editor)`; reader.js at document level when the reader is open, the focus owner is `reader` or `card` and `M.margin.activeEditor()` is null → `M.reader.createFreeNote(pageIndex, yPdf, text)` with the clipboard content per SPEC §15).

## pdfview text index (binding)
`M.pdfview.textIndex(i)` resolves `{text, starts, items}`: `items[k] = {str, x, y, width, height, hasEOL, divIndex}` with `x = transform[4]`, `y = transform[5]` (PDF user space), `height = Math.hypot(transform[2], transform[3])` (fallback `item.height`), `divIndex` = index into `pageView(i).textDivs`; `text` = every `str` joined with `" "` (plus `"\n"` after an item with `hasEOL`); `starts[k]` = offset of item k in `text`. `anchors.refind` and `reader` find use this shape only. `page:textready` fires after both the text layer and the index exist. `M.anchors.hitTest(rectsByPrompt, x, y)` takes `[{id, rects: [[x1,y1,x2,y2], …]}]` in PDF space (one page).

## Routing and review triggers
`M.app.route(screen, params)` (since D30 it emits `app:routed` each time it settles on a screen, and a reader route may carry `position`, a tab's own place: "Tabs"): "library" | "deck" {collectionId} | "reader" {collectionId, promptId?, pageIndex?} | "stats" (the statistics page, D27: no collection, hash `#stats`; `route` calls `M.stats.open()`, and `M.stats.close()` when another screen takes over; Esc there routes to the library). Only `M.app` calls `M.reader.open/close` and `M.review.start`. Library/deck buttons call `M.app.startReview(scope, collectionId)` and `M.app.route(...)`. The review overlay sits above whatever screen is open; "Go to source" emits `review:gotoSource` and `M.app` routes to the reader. In a tab that stepped aside for a newer one ("One writing tab per origin"), `route()` resolves false and does nothing.

## Post-review decisions (binding; added after the code review of 2026-11-04)
- PDF.js loader: `M.pdfview.open` always passes `isEvalSupported: false` (CVE-2024-4367, PDF.js < 4.2.67). Never forward caller options to `getDocument`.
- Worker: `workerSrc` is a same-origin bootstrap (blob:, or data: on opaque origins) that fetches `pdf.worker.min.js` from cdnjs with its SHA-384 integrity and runs only matching bytes. When no worker can start, PDF.js's main-thread fallback loads the same file through a `<script integrity>` element (`PDFWorker._setupFakeWorkerGlobal` is overridden in index.html). Every cdnjs `<script>`/`<link>` carries `integrity` + `crossorigin="anonymous"`.
- Passwords: pdfview remembers a password that opened a document for the current page only (in memory, keyed by byte length and a hash of the first and last 64 KiB), so the reader does not ask again right after Add PDF. Nothing is stored.
- `contentUpdatedAt` on collections moves only when name, description or sections change (`M.cache.saveCollection` compares a signature; `{content: false}` keeps the record's stamp, used by imports). For prompts, the automatic context snapshot and lost/re-found anchor positions are derived and do not move `contentUpdatedAt`; `importJSON` rebuilds the context line of merged prompts from this device's collections.
- Imported attachments: only `image/png|jpeg|gif|webp` are stored; object URLs always carry one of those types.
- Review never queues prompts of a tombstoned collection. Review buttons are never disabled: an empty scope opens the overlay in its "All caught up" / "No prompts written today" / "No reviewable prompts yet" state.
- Z (undo delete) is routed for the reader, library, settings and list owners while an undo toast is on offer.
- Reader breakpoints (replace the SPEC §2 table's widths; measured on `#reader`): wide ≥ 680, rail 600–679, stack < 600. Wide below 1000 adds `.reader--compact` on `#reader`: `--margin-w: 224px`, `--card-w: 192px` (fit width = W − 224 − 48); from 1000 up the margin stays 288 / 256. `M.reader.mode()` still returns "wide" when compact. `reader:mode` carries `{mode, compact}` and fires whenever either changes. The zoom follows every width change inside the same ResizeObserver callback (pdfview's fit reads `#margin.offsetWidth`, so no frame shows a fit for the old width or margin, and no margin pins over a page that should fit during a window drag): a breakpoint re-applies it with an immediate re-render, any other width change (at fit width, or stack's clamp) is `M.pdfview.setScale(…, {gesture: true})` (placeholders resize now, pages re-render once the drag has been idle 150 ms); there is no refit timer. A manual zoom outside stack stays as it is. Margin card estimates use 38 · cardWidth / 256 characters per line (`M.margin.estimateHeight(text, prompt, cardWidth)`); on a card-width change the margin re-estimates every card, re-measures the rendered ones and the card with the open editor (a new card's too: its saved text says nothing about the editor's height) and re-runs the cascade inside the event, and it runs the cascade at once on `scale:changed`.
- Pinned margin: `#margin` is `position: sticky; right: 0; z-index: 5` (styles.css) in the horizontally scrolling `#scroller`, so a page zoomed wider than the room beside the margin scrolls under it; scrolled to the far right, the margin is back in its own place and the page's right edge is reachable. z-index 5 is above the pages (each `.page` isolates its layers) and below the `#list` drawer (25), the snip overlay and `#sheet` (40), the adder and find bar (50), toolbar menus (60), dialogs and toasts; popovers and card menus live inside the margin. `.margin` is opaque (`--bg`, except a 4-px strip on its left that keeps the page's right shadow when the page fits); margin.js adds `.margin--pinned` (fully opaque, inset 1-px `--line` rule on the left) while `scrollLeft < scrollWidth − clientWidth`, checked on scroll, scroller resize and every layout, and redraws the connector on horizontal scroll. The snip overlay stops at the margin's left edge while the margin overlaps the page, and snip.js places it again on `scale:changed` and `reader:mode` (a zoom or a breakpoint moves the page column without scrolling).
- Navigation beside the pinned margin: in wide and rail mode the sticky margin covers the scroller's right `#margin.offsetWidth` px whether the page fits or not, so what a reader sees of the page is the band [scrollLeft + 24, scrollLeft + clientWidth − margin width − 24]. Find (the current match), G / the card menu's Go to passage, review's Go to source (all through `goToAnchor`) and J / K bring the target's first rect into that band when either end is outside it: about a third into the band (its start first when it is wider than the band), clamped to the row. J / K ask `M.reader.scrollLeftFor(anchor)` and make the sideways move part of their one smooth scroll. Stack mode never scrolls sideways (`overflow-x: hidden`).
- Toolbar: the zoom group (−, readout, +, ⊡) shows in wide, compact and rail. When the bar is short of room, reader.js adds `.tb--fit-1/2/3` to `#toolbar`, measured with every item rigid (`.tb--measure`, the title capped at 160 px): 1 hides Image|LaTeX and shows ⋯ (which carries it), 2 hides the text labels (Library, Snip, ✎), 3 hides Review ▾ and ? (both in ⋯). ⊡ gets `.is-manual` (accent) while the zoom is manual, as a "back to fit" control. Stack keeps its fixed collapse; its ⋯ menu offers Zoom in, Zoom out and Fit width.
- Pinch zoom: a non-passive `wheel` listener on `#scroller` (and `#snipOverlay`) takes `ctrlKey` wheels (Chrome / Brave trackpad pinch): `preventDefault()`, factor exp(−deltaY · 0.01) with line deltas × 16, page deltas × 400 and |deltaY| capped at 50 per event (a ctrl+mouse-wheel notch zooms 1.65×). Safari: `gesturestart` / `gesturechange` / `gestureend` (all prevented), scale = start scale · `ev.scale`; ctrl wheels are ignored during a gesture. Steps are merged and applied at most once per animation frame, clamped to 0.2–8, anchored at the pointer; the reader zoom becomes manual and the readout updates as with − / +. A step that changes nothing (Safari scale 1 or a rotation, a ctrl+wheel with deltaY 0, a pinch held at a bound) is dropped (still prevented): at fit width the zoom stays "fit" and keeps following the window.
- Stack clamp: in stack mode the zoom (pinch, Cmd+= / ⋯ Zoom in, a manual zoom carried over from a wider window or saved in `lastPosition`) is clamped to the fit width of the column as it is now, `M.pdfview.fitScale()`, computed on demand (never a fit remembered from another width or mode); a Zoom in whose next rung is past fit goes to fit width. A manual zoom wider than fit (`st.zoom`) is shown at fit width in stack and applies again in wide or rail, unless the zoom is changed in between.
- The anchor holds for the whole gesture: pdfview keeps the document point found at the gesture's first step (page and unscaled offset) while the pointer and the scroll offsets stay where the last step left them, so the whole-pixel rounding of each step never adds up. Where scrolling alone cannot keep the point (the page still fits and the flex gutter shrinks as it grows, or a zoom out below fit leaves too little row to scroll), the step holds `#pages` (pdfview's own host) with an inline margin: on the left, so the page grows to the right under the sticky margin, or on the right. Once the gesture has been idle 150 ms the page eases (150 ms, cubic out; at once under `prefers-reduced-motion`, and a scroll by the reader ends the easing) into the layout's own place, keeping its place on screen as far as scrolling allows; `scale:changed` is emitted again when it gets there. Any call that is not an anchored gesture step ends a hold at once.
- Dimmed pages stay dimmed through a zoom: a stale page keeps its `.hlLayer` and `.dimLayer` (both `inset: 0`, scale-free); only the text layer waits for the re-render.
- §17 extension `M.pdfview.setScale(scale|"fit", opts)`: `opts.anchor = {clientX, clientY}` keeps the document point under that client point fixed (`helpers.anchorOffsets` / `anchorScroll` / `zoomHold`, pure); `opts.gesture = true` resizes the placeholders and stretches the current canvases at once but starts no render until no gesture step has come for `GESTURE_IDLE_MS` (150 ms); a call without it re-renders at once. Also exported: `MIN_SCALE`, `MAX_SCALE`, `GESTURE_IDLE_MS`, `fitScale()` (the fit-width scale for the column now; null while no document is open). A scroller with `overflow-x: hidden` (stack) is never scrolled sideways and takes no hold.
- Cmd/Ctrl+0 at fit width already: `M.reader.run("zoomFit")` returns false, so `M.app` does not `preventDefault()` and the browser resets its own page zoom (first press fits the PDF, second resets the browser zoom). Cmd/Ctrl+= and Cmd/Ctrl+− stay consumed. In the desktop app nothing takes that second press (its View › Actual Size has no shortcut: "Desktop app (binding)", Menus), so there it only fits the PDF and View › Actual Size resets the page zoom.
- Extensions recorded in the SELF-CHECKs: `M.reader.MODE_WIDE`, `MODE_RAIL`, `COMPACT_BELOW`, `REVEAL_PAD`, `scrollLeftFor(anchor) → number|null`; `M.margin.cardWidth()`, `isPinned()`, `CARD_W`, `estimateHeight(text, prompt, cardWidth)`; `M.pdfview.fitScale()`, `helpers.anchorOffsets`, `helpers.zoomHold`; snip.js listens to `scale:changed` and `reader:mode`.
- Kind icons (the user's choice, 2026-11-09; replaces SPEC §6's text glyphs ✦ `{}` ▭ ·): the card glyph column, the rail and stack markers and the prompt-list rows draw a 14-px line icon from `M.util.kindIcon(kind)`: a pencil for notes, a question mark in a circle for Q./A. prompts, brackets for cloze prompts, a marker pen for highlights (constant SVG, `stroke="currentColor"`, so `.card--flagged` and `.card--temp` still colour it). An unknown kind draws the pencil. The card redraws its icon only when its kind changes (`data-kind` on `.card__glyph`).

## Obsidian (binding; added 2026-11-06)
The user asked for a Note option that adds the note to Obsidian. Settled with the user: one Obsidian note per book (per deck for decks); a silent write through the local server with an `obsidian://` link fallback; the option in the selection popup AND in every card menu; the note stays in the Marginalia margin too (Obsidian gets a copy at send time; sending again updates that copy).
- **Where it goes**: `<settings.obsidianFolder>/<name>.md` inside the vault, name = the book nickname (deck name), with `\ / : * ? " < > | # ^ [ ]` and control characters dropped, whitespace collapsed, spaces and dots trimmed, runs of dots inside turned into "…" (the server refuses any `..` in a path), at most 120 characters, "Untitled" when empty. The folder is cleaned segment by segment the same way (empty, `.` and `..` segments disappear; "" = the vault root). A new note starts with `# <name>` and a blank line. Sending again updates the copy where it lives: a prompt whose last send recorded a note (`prompt.obsidianFile`) is sent to that note, also after a rename, a folder change or a move to a deck; else a prompt whose server send did not finish goes to the note that send was writing to (`prompt.obsidianPendingFile`, the pending marker, see Feedback), which may hold its copy. The recorded path, and that one, are reused only when `notePath` could have produced them (each segment unchanged by `cleanSegment`, ending in ".md", at most 1024 characters); any other prompt goes to `<obsidianFolder>/<current nickname>.md`, so new prompts go to the new name. The vault is the one chosen now, since the record holds none. The server's title for a new note, and the link-mode `# <name>`, is the target note's own name, and the toast names the note that was written.
- **One block per prompt**, upserted by its id: `<!-- marginalia:<promptId> -->`, `### <heading>` (books: section label and page, "Ch. 1 · The Wave Function · p. 12", or the page alone; decks: the section, else the deck name), the anchor quote with every line prefixed `> ` and escaped so the PDF's text is never Obsidian syntax (left out without a quote), a blank line, the body (`M.markup.toObsidian`, left out for a highlight or empty text), a blank line, `[Open in Marginalia](<location.origin>/#reader/<collectionId>/<promptId>) · YYYY-MM-DD` (local date of the send; the link only when `location.hostname` is 127.0.0.1 or localhost, else just the date), `<!-- /marginalia -->`. Quote escaping (`quoteText`): a backslash and `# $ ^ [ ]` are backslash-escaped, as is every `=` and `%` in a run of two or more; a `<` before a letter, `!`, `/` or `?` is written `&lt;`; a leading `>` or a line of only `-` or `=` is escaped. Obsidian shows each of these characters unchanged. The typed body is not escaped, and the Markdown export (`exporter.toMarkdown`) still writes the quote verbatim. The block always has the shape serve.py can find again (see tools/serve.py below): typed text that would read as a marker (`<!--`, optional spaces and "/", then "marginalia", any case; matched as `<!--\s*(?:/\s*)?marginalia`, serve.py's pattern, in linear time) is written as `&lt;!--`; a body line outside code fences that would read as the date line gets its date's first "-" escaped (`2026\-11-06`, which Obsidian shows as 2026-11-06), and so does a body line anywhere, fenced or not, that starts (after spaces) with the block's own link and a date, `[Open in Marginalia](…#reader/<any collection id>/<promptId>) · <date>` (serve.py's `own_link_re`), for example a date line pasted back from Obsidian (serve.py reads such a line as the block's own date line and would refuse the block; inside code the `\` shows); a code fence (CommonMark: up to three spaces, then ``` or ~~~) the body leaves open is closed after it. The block is always well-formed text: a lone surrogate (a quote cut inside an astral letter such as 𝑥, which pdf.js keeps) becomes U+FFFD, in `blockFor`, `fileName` and both URI builders; `M.anchors.fromSelection` cuts quotes at 500 characters (code points), never inside a surrogate pair.
- **`M.markup.toObsidian(text, {images})`** (pure): the text as typed (Q./A. lines, the #tags line, `\$`, `\{`), except a cloze prompt's deletions `{x}` → `==x==` and `{x::cue}` → `==x==` (the cue dropped: "Prompt craft") (the render() scan: math inside a deletion stays math, `\{` literal, whitespace inside the braces moves outside), `\(…\)` → `$…$` and `\[…\]` → `$$…$$` on math tokens only, image tokens → `![[marginalia-<id>.png]]` when `images` is true (`![[<name>]]` when it is a function id → name), else `[image]`.
- **`M.obsidian`** (`js/obsidian.js`, loaded after exporter.js and before js/backup.js and cache.js; 31 namespaces in all, in the order "Automatic backup" lists; tools/smoke.js and xref check them): pure `fileName(name)`, `notePath(folder, name)`, `blockFor(prompt, collection, {images, now, origin, hostname})`, `uriFor(vault, file, content)`, `openUriFor(vault, file)`; `detect({force})` → `Promise<{mode: "server"|"link", vaults, server, outdated}>`, cached; the cache is forced each time Settings opens, and also by a send while the cached answer is "link" on a page served from 127.0.0.1 or localhost (one GET before the send, inside the gesture), so a restarted server is used without a reload. `outdated` is true when such a page's `GET /api/obsidian/vaults` answers 404, 405 or 501, meaning an older serve.py; it is false for `file://`, the artifact, a network error, a 500 and any other host. `replaceOnly` is in the answer, as true, only when `GET /api/obsidian/vaults` answers `"replaceOnly": true` (exactly true): that serve.py's `/notes` honours a block's `replaceOnly` (FEATURES A8, "Send all, server mode"). `send(promptId, {fromGesture})` → `Promise<{ok, mode, file, created?, replaced?, error?}>` (never rejects; toasts through `M.app.toast`); in a tab that stepped aside for a newer Marginalia tab (`M.store.locked()`, see "One writing tab per origin") it sends nothing, neither a POST nor a link, and answers `{ok: false, error: "Marginalia is open in another tab"}`. Extensions: `state()`, `headingFor()`, `copyNote(block, file, many, detail)` (`many`: several blocks, so the texts say "notes" and the button "Copy notes"; `detail`, optional: a line that both of its toasts carry, "… copied — paste … into <file>" and "The browser blocked the clipboard — …", as do those of the retry from its button; without it the toasts are as before), `URI_LIMIT`, `DEFAULT_FOLDER`, `METHOD_SERVER`, `METHOD_LINK`, `METHOD_RESTART`, and for "Send all to Obsidian" (below) `sendAll`, `planAll`, `isSendingAll`, `readingOrder`, `sendState`, `SPLIT_BYTES`, `NOTES_MAX_BODY`, `NOTES_MAX_BLOCKS`; test hooks `_setOpener(fn)`, `_setFetch(fn)`, `_setApiBase(base)`, `_setSplit(bytes, blocks)` (smaller request limits; no argument: the real ones), `_reset()` (also forgets running send-alls and restores the limits).
- **Server mode** (detect: `GET /api/obsidian/vaults` answers with at least one vault): `POST /api/obsidian/note` with `{vault, file, title, blockId, markdown, images: [{name: "marginalia-<id>.<png|jpg|gif|webp>", data: base64}]}` — the vault is `settings.obsidianVault` (compared exactly with the server's names, and the server's name is sent), or the only vault found when that is ""; with several vaults and none chosen nothing is written and the toast names Settings › Obsidian. Images come from `M.store.attachments` (extension by signature); a missing one is `[image]`.
- **Link mode** (404, a network error, an empty vault list, the artifact build, `file://`): `obsidian://new?vault=<enc>&file=<enc path without .md>&content=<enc block>&append=true&silent` (`encodeURIComponent`, so "/" is %2F and " " is %20; the vault name as stored, spaces kept; no vault name → the parameter is left out and Obsidian takes the vault it has open), opened by a temporary `<a href>` click inside the user's gesture (the opener is the only thing that ever opens an `obsidian://` URI, and tests replace it); a tab that stepped aside meanwhile (during the send's check of the server, or before the toast's "Add to Obsidian" was clicked) opens nothing (repair D6, 2026-11-10: `M.store.locked()` is checked again right before the link opens). A card with the pending marker (Feedback) never goes through a link (FEATURES A9.1). Images are `[image]`; a re-send goes to the note that holds the copy and appends a second copy there, and the toast says so (a send from before `obsidianFile` counts as that note's); when the recorded copy is in another note (a recorded path that cannot be reused), the detail is "The earlier copy stays in <file>." `obsidian://new` creates a missing note with exactly the content, so the content starts with `# <name>` and a blank line when no prompt records a send to that note (`prompt.obsidianFile`, deleted prompts included; sends from before that field existed count for their own book's note only while no send of that book has a recorded file): the first send into a note (a book's first prompt, or a prompt never sent, after a rename or into another folder) makes a titled note, later ones append bare blocks; a hand-made note of that name gets the title line appended once. Above `URI_LIMIT` (60 000 characters, title included) nothing opens: the block is copied ("Note copied — paste it into <file>"). Without a gesture (`fromGesture` false) the toast's "Add to Obsidian" button opens it. On a 127.0.0.1 or localhost page every link-mode send asks `GET /api/obsidian/vaults` once before it opens the link (the re-check above), as the first send always did, so Safari's activation window (still unverified) applies to every such send, not just the first.
- **Feedback**: "Added to Obsidian · <file>" / "Updated in Obsidian · <file>" with "Open in Obsidian" (`openUriFor` through the same opener, on the user's click); a failure names the reason (the server's own words for its refusals, such as a read-only or hard-linked note or a vault name two vaults share) and offers "Copy note" (`writeText` inside the click, else a selected textarea + `execCommand("copy")`); `send()` builds the block before anything else, so every later failure, an unexpected one too ("unexpected error (<message>)"), offers it. Every failure's "Copy note" offers the block with images as "[image]": the version with embeds is only POSTed, since a failed send wrote no image. **A card that may be in its note already is never offered to copy** (repair D2 of the A9 review, 2026-11-10: a copy invites the second copy, as for A9.1; the card menu's own copy items stay): a server send that left no telling — no answer, a 5xx (an older serve.py's 404, 405 or 501 excepted), a 2xx whose body was cut off or is not JSON ("the server's answer could not be read"), an unexpected error once the request left — toasts "May already be in Obsidian — <reason>" with the detail "Send it again once Marginalia's server answers: it checks <file> and never adds a second copy." (12 s, no action; result `{ok: false, mode: "server", file, error: <reason>}`), and every other failure of a card that holds the pending marker (below: an earlier send did not finish, or another request of it is on its way) keeps its "Could not add to Obsidian — <reason>" text without "Copy note" (`markedNow`, `failServer`, `maybeThere` in js/obsidian.js). A copy the clipboard refuses toasts "The browser blocked the clipboard — the note was not copied" with "Copy note" again (12 s), until one goes through; this also applies to the copy above 60 000 characters. After an older server is detected (`outdated`), the first link-send detail reads "Sent through an obsidian:// link. Restart Marginalia (marginalia restart in Terminal) to write silently and update in place." (otherwise still "Start Marginalia with the marginalia command …"), and "this browser cannot open obsidian:// links" ends with " — restart Marginalia (marginalia restart in Terminal) to write silently". A server write refused with 404, 405 or 501 says "Marginalia's server has no Obsidian support — restart Marginalia (marginalia restart in Terminal)". On success `prompt.obsidianSentAt` = the time of the answer and `prompt.obsidianFile` = the note's vault-relative path, not a content edit. If the prompt's content stamp (`contentUpdatedAt`, read when the block was built) moved while the block was on its way — an edit, including one that changes the cached record in place, such as a move to a deck — the time is one millisecond before that edit instead, so the next Send all still updates the edit. This applies to single sends and send-alls. Every change to the send record (`obsidianSentAt`, `obsidianFile`, `obsidianPendingFile`) goes through `M.cache.patchPrompts` (FEATURES A9, added 2026-11-10; js/obsidian.js `writeRecords`): the fields are set on the cached records in place, all the records of one request in ONE store write, with no `prompt:saved` and never as a content edit (`contentUpdatedAt` and `dueAt` stay), so a send-all of a few hundred cards re-renders no list row, margin card or page per card; a cache without `patchPrompts` (a test double) gets one `M.cache.savePrompt(copy, {content: false})` per record that changes. **The pending marker** (`prompt.obsidianPendingFile`, written ahead since FEATURES A9, 2026-11-10): a request that may write blocks can write them and never be answered — the server stopped or restarted mid-request (`marginalia restart` included), the connection dropped, the tab was closed or reloaded, or it stepped aside for a newer tab — and the page cannot tell. So before every `POST /api/obsidian/note` or `/notes` leaves (single sends, each Send all request, its per-block /note fallback), every card in it gets `obsidianPendingFile` = the request's note (`writeAhead`), unless it already holds that value, and that write is stored before the request goes. When the store refuses it (a full disk: `M.cache.patchPrompts` rejects, `storeRecords`), nothing goes (repair A9-R3 of the A9 review, 2026-11-10): the markers go back as they were, a single send fails with "Could not add to Obsidian — Marginalia could not save, so nothing was sent" (with "Copy note", nothing having been sent), and a Send all fails that request's cards with that reason and sends nothing more. The answer then settles each card (`settle`): a block the server wrote records the send as above and removes the marker; a block it definitely did not write — a 4xx answer for the request whose body is JSON (serve.py's `{ok: false, error}`: 400, 403, 404, 409, 413 …), or a per-block `ok: false` result, `notFound` included — gets the marker back as it was before this request (removed when there was none); anything else leaves the marker: a 5xx (its "unexpected error" may come after a write), a network error (no answer), a 2xx whose body is cut or not JSON, a block missing from the results, an unexpected error while reading the answer. While several requests of one card are on their way at once (a single send during a send-all), a definite "not written" puts the marker back only once the last of them is answered, and one with no telling keeps it for good. `obsidianSentAt` and `obsidianFile` stay as they were meanwhile: a card never sent stays fresh and its menu still reads "Send to Obsidian". `targetFile` is the recorded note, else `obsidianPendingFile` (when `notePath` could have produced it, as for `obsidianFile`), else `<folder>/<nickname>.md`, so a marked card's next send goes to that note: the server replaces the copy the lost request wrote, else adds it, one copy, also after a rename, a folder change or a move to a deck (a single send as new through /note; Send all, see "Send all, server mode", also sends a marked card whose stamps say unchanged, with `replaceOnly`, FEATURES A9.3). A tab closed or reloaded mid-request leaves the marker in the store for the next page that reads it; a tab that stepped aside mid-request sends nothing more and its writes are refused, so the markers it wrote while it was the active tab stay in the store for the active tab to settle (FEATURES A9.4; that tab loads them when it opens). **Link mode never sends a marked card** (FEATURES A9.1): no single send ("Send to Obsidian", "Update in Obsidian", O's save) and no Send all link or copy carries a card whose `obsidianPendingFile` is a path Marginalia could have written (one it could not have written is ignored, as by `targetFile`), since an `obsidian://` link can only add and the note may hold the card already. The card stays in the margin; nothing is offered to copy (a copy invites the second copy; the card menu's own copy items stay); a single send toasts "1 card may already be in Obsidian: a send to Marginalia's server did not finish. Start Marginalia with the marginalia command and send again: it checks the note and never adds a second copy." (12 s; after an older serve.py answered, "Restart Marginalia (marginalia restart in Terminal) and send again: …"; result `{ok: false, mode: "link", file, error: "a send to Marginalia's server did not finish"}`), and Send all names them the same way ("<N> card(s) may already be in Obsidian: …", see the Send all bullets). Trade-offs: a request that never reached the server leaves the marker too, so after a rename that card is added to the old note, once, and a link holds it back until Marginalia's server sends it; a 5xx or a cut answer from a server that wrote nothing does the same. The JSON backup carries `obsidianSentAt`, `obsidianFile` and `obsidianPendingFile` with the record (FEATURES A9.5). `merge()` keeps the later send of both sides with its file (the other side's file when the later send recorded none), and the marker of either side, the local record's first (FEATURES A9.5's "local first"), else the backup's (repair D1 of the A9 review, 2026-11-10: a claude.ai copy or plain file that already held the card took no marker from a backup made after the marginalia library's interrupted send, so its `obsidian://` link added a second copy). A marker goes only when the other side shows the doubt settled: it holds no marker and recorded a send into that very note later than the marker's side did (`mergeSendRecord` in js/exporter.js), so a copy that took the marker from one backup drops it again with a backup made after the marginalia library settled the card. `importJSON` merges each prompt's send record once more with the record as it is right before saving it (repair D4: a send that left before the import began and is answered while it saves keeps its write-ahead marker, or its record; since FEATURES A10.1 no send leaves while an import saves, next bullets).
- **Entry points**: the adder's fourth action "Note → Obsidian" (key cap O; the "Note → " part hides in stack mode) and `READER_MAP.obsidianNote` "o" → `M.reader.run("obsidianNote")` → `createFromSelection("obsidian")`: `M.margin.openEditor({promptId: null, anchor, initialText: "", obsidian: true})`, whose editor's Save reads "Save + Obsidian ⌘↩" (`M.editor.create({saveLabel})`); the save stores the record exactly like any margin note (kind from `M.prompts.parse`), then calls `M.obsidian.send(id, {fromGesture: true})`. Without a selection O makes a free note at the middle of the visible page (`M.reader.createFreeNote(pageIndex, yPdf, "", {obsidian: true})`). A new free note (no rects, no quote) saved with nothing in it (`M.prompts.parse` kind "highlight": blank or tags only), from O or N, is neither stored nor sent: the editor stays open and its lint line says "Type a note first" (`Editor.hint(msg)`, an M.editor extension: the host's message until the next edit). The card ⋯ menu (after the copy items) and the prompt-list row ⋯ menu (before Delete; deck prompts have no card) offer "Send to Obsidian", or "Update in Obsidian" once `obsidianSentAt` is set, for every kind. The ? sheet lists O.
- **Send all to Obsidian** (added 2026-11-09: the user asked for a faster way to send highlights and chose only "send a whole book"; no one-key send, no always-send switch). The library's book row ⋯ menu, deck row ⋯ menu and deck page ⋯ menu (js/library.js) have "Send all to Obsidian" right after "Export…". It is left out when `M.obsidian` has no `sendAll`, disabled (title "Nothing in it to send yet") while the book or deck has no live prompt of any kind (a book with only highlights is enabled), and reads "Sending to Obsidian…" (disabled) while that collection's send-all runs. Its action, `M.library.sendAllToObsidian(collectionId)` → `Promise<sendAll's result | null>`, runs `M.obsidian.planAll(id)`, then `M.app.confirm` {title "Send N cards to Obsidian?" ("1 card"; N = new + edited + rechecked in server mode, new without the pending marker in link mode); body, in server mode (FEATURES A8), "A new ones are added and U edited ones are updated in place, in <file>. K already there are skipped." (a part with no card is left out; the singulars are "1 new one is", "1 edited one is" and "1 already there is"; <file> is "<A>", "<A> and <B>" or "<A> and N other notes"; FEATURES A9.3 adds the part "R whose last send did not finish are updated in place", "1 … is", for planAll's `rechecked`, and three parts read "A, B and C"), in link mode "They go to <file>." ("It goes to" for one card), "K sent before are skipped: links can only add." and, above `URI_LIMIT`, "Too long for an obsidian:// link: they are copied for you to paste into the note.", then, for planAll's `held` cards (FEATURES A9.1), "<N> card(s) may already be in Obsidian: a send to Marginalia's server did not finish. Start Marginalia with the marginalia command and send again: it checks the note and never adds a second copy." ("Restart Marginalia (marginalia restart in Terminal) and send again: …" after an older serve.py answered; `_pure` `heldText` in js/library.js, `heldLine` in js/obsidian.js); OK "Send", Cancel "Cancel"}, then `M.obsidian.sendAll(id, {fromGesture: true})`: the click that confirms is the user gesture a link needs. Instead of a confirm it toasts (id "obsidian") when planAll fails ("Could not send to Obsidian — <reason>", for example "choose a vault in Settings › Obsidian"), when that collection's send-all still runs ("Already sending “<name>” to Obsidian"), or when there is nothing to send (planAll's count 0; no confirm and no request at all): "Everything in “<name>” is already in Obsidian" (detail "Every card was sent before and none was edited since.") when no card is new or edited; in link mode with no new card but some edited ones, "Nothing new to send — every card of “<name>” was sent before, and an obsidian:// link can only add" (detail "M edited card(s) was/were not updated." and "Start Marginalia with the marginalia command" or, after an older server answered, "Restart Marginalia (marginalia restart in Terminal)", then " to write silently and update in place."); "Nothing in “<name>” to send to Obsidian" when the book or deck holds no card; in link mode with `held` cards (FEATURES A9.1), the held line above is the text, with "M edited card(s) was/were not updated: links can only add." as its detail when there are any; that toast stays 12 s, as the held line does everywhere (repair A9-R7, 2026-11-10; the other nothing-to-send toasts 8 s). A second click while the first confirm is on its way is ignored. `M.library._pure.sendAllConfirm(plan)` builds the confirm and `_pure.sendAllNothing(plan, name)` → `{text, detail}` the nothing-to-send toast; `toast()` in js/library.js takes an optional sixth argument, the detail.
- **`sendAll` and `planAll`**: `M.obsidian.sendAll(collectionId, {fromGesture})` → `Promise<{ok, mode ("server"|"link"|"copy"), sent, added, updated, unchanged, skipped, notFound: [{promptId, error}], notUpdated: [{promptId, error}], held: [{promptId, error}], failed: [{promptId, error}], files, file, error?, uri?}>` (never rejects; toasts through `M.app.toast`; `held`, FEATURES A9.1: the cards with the pending marker a link held back, error "a send to Marginalia's server did not finish", counted in `skipped`) sends the live prompts of the book or deck, each kind (qa, cloze, note, highlight); a prompt with neither text nor a quote is skipped (counted in `skipped`). Send all never sends a card twice (FEATURES A8, added 2026-11-09: the user chose "Update edited ones"). `sendState(prompt)` (pure) returns "fresh" (no `obsidianSentAt`), "changed" (`contentUpdatedAt` > `obsidianSentAt`, or an `obsidianSentAt` that is not a number) or "unchanged" (anything else, including a missing stamp). The content stamp is `contentUpdatedAt` as `M.cache.savePrompt(p, {content: true})` moves it ("Post-review decisions"; restores move it too); a send's own `{content: false}` record, context snapshots and re-found anchors never move it. Fresh cards are added, changed ones replace their own block (`replaceOnly`, "Send all, server mode") and are never added again, and unchanged cards go in no request; groups, splitting and `files`/`file` cover only the cards that go (server mode: the fresh, changed and rechecked ones, below; link mode: the fresh ones without the marker). A card with the pending marker (Feedback) whose stamps say "unchanged" is not taken for one already there (FEATURES A9.3, `plan.recheck`): the server sends it with `replaceOnly` into its `targetFile`, so the server settles the doubt (a marked card that is fresh or changed goes as such: added to the marker's note, or replacing its block); a link holds every marked card back (`plan.marked`). When nothing is fresh, changed or marked, planAll (count 0) and sendAll (a toast only) answer without `detect()`, so no request is made at all, not even `GET /api/obsidian/vaults`. In the result, `unchanged` counts the unchanged cards, `notFound` lists the edited and rechecked cards whose earlier block the server no longer found and `notUpdated` the edited and rechecked cards no request carried (a link, or a serve.py that cannot replace only), and `skipped` counts every card no request carried (empty, unchanged, `notUpdated` and `held` ones). Reading order, `readingOrder(prompts, collection)` (pure): books by `anchor.pageIndex` (else `context.pageIndex`, else 0), then from the top of the page down (`anchor.y` descending, PDF space; no y = the page's top), then `createdAt`, then id; decks by `context.sectionLabel`, in the order the sections first appear by creation, then `createdAt`, then id. The blocks are `blockFor`'s, exactly as a single send writes them, grouped by the single send's `targetFile` rule (a copy sent before is updated in the note that holds it); groups go in the order of their first card. `planAll(collectionId)` → `Promise<{ok, mode, count, fresh, changed, unchanged, rechecked, held, sentBefore, total, skipped, file, files, tooLong, outdated, error?, busy?}>` (FEATURES A9: `rechecked`, server mode, the marked cards whose stamps say unchanged, which go too and count in `count` and `sentBefore`; `held`, link mode, every marked card, which a link holds back: there `fresh` and `changed` leave them out, `sentBefore` is changed + unchanged, and `skipped` counts them) answers what `sendAll` would do now (`count`: fresh + changed + rechecked in server mode, only the fresh cards without the marker in link mode; `fresh`, `changed`, `unchanged`: the cards by `sendState`, marked cards aside (server mode: a marked unchanged card counts as rechecked; link mode: every marked card counts as held), `sentBefore` = changed + unchanged + rechecked; `total`: every card with something to send; `skipped`: the empty and unchanged cards, in link mode the changed and held ones too; `file`: the note named first, the one that gets the most cards; `files`: every target note; `outdated`: detect()'s; with count 0 and no check, `mode` and `outdated` are the last check's, null and false before any). planAll checks `detect()` as a single send does (a cached "link" on a 127.0.0.1 or localhost page is checked again, and so is a cached server answer without `replaceOnly`, see "Send all, server mode"); `sendAll` does not check again when that answer is under 10 s old, so the link opens right after the confirming click.
- **Send all, server mode** (`GET /api/obsidian/vaults` lists a vault): one `POST /api/obsidian/notes` {vault, file, title, blocks: [{blockId, markdown, replaceOnly?}], images} per target note (see "Send all (server)" below), split into consecutive requests of at most `SPLIT_BYTES` (20 MB, counted in UTF-8, images included) and `NOTES_MAX_BLOCKS` (2000) blocks; a block bigger than 20 MB goes alone, and one whose request would pass `NOTES_MAX_BODY` (100 MB) fails without a request. Only the fresh, changed and rechecked cards go (rechecked: marked, otherwise unchanged, FEATURES A9.3); an edited or rechecked card's block carries `"replaceOnly": true` (the key only then, never on /note). Images are collected as in a single send (a missing one becomes `[image]`), each once per request. Before each request (and each /note of the fallback below) its cards' pending markers are written ahead, in one `M.cache.patchPrompts` write, and its answer settles them (Feedback). Every block the answer's `results` mark `ok: true` records `obsidianSentAt` and `obsidianFile` (the answer's `file`) and drops its marker, the request's records in one `M.cache.patchPrompts` write, all saved before the toast; `replaced` counts as updated, otherwise added. A result `{blockId, ok: false, notFound: true, error}` (`notFound` exactly true), in a 2xx answer or in a refused answer's results, records nothing: the card keeps its earlier `obsidianSentAt` and `obsidianFile` and stays "changed" (a rechecked card stays "unchanged" and keeps its marker, FEATURES A9.2); it goes to `notFound` (its `error`, else "its earlier copy is no longer in the note"), and its block (images as "[image]") to the toast's "Copy notes". Any other result with `ok: false` fails with its own error ("the server did not write it" when it names none); a block missing from `results` fails with "the server's answer left it out" and keeps its marker (no telling: repair D2, see the feedback bullet). A refused request fails its blocks with the per-block errors its answer lists, otherwise with the request's error (413: "the notes and their images are larger than 100 MB"), and the next request still goes. No answer fails that request and every later block ("Marginalia's server did not answer"), sends nothing more, and the next send checks the server again; the blocks of that request are named apart as "may already be in Obsidian" (below), the later ones, which no request carried, as "could not be sent". The blocks of that request may have been written, as may those of a 2xx answer whose body never arrives: their cards keep the markers written ahead (`obsidianPendingFile`, see Feedback), so their next send updates those copies there instead of adding second ones, also after a rename, a folder change or a move to a deck; a refused request (a 4xx JSON answer) and a block whose result is `ok: false` (`notFound` included) get their markers back as they were. `/notes` answering 404, 405 or 501 means a serve.py from before it (still running after an update): from then on one `POST /api/obsidian/note` per never-sent card, with the single send's 25 MB limit and restart wording; /note adds a block it does not find, so an edited card never goes there: it is listed in `notUpdated` ("restart Marginalia (marginalia restart in Terminal) to update it in place") and counted in `skipped`. A serve.py from after /notes but before A8 ignores unknown block keys, so its /notes would add an edited card whose block is gone (a second copy): the page tells it apart by `GET /api/obsidian/vaults`, which answers `"replaceOnly": true` from A8 on (detect()'s `replaceOnly`). Without it, `allViaServer` holds the edited cards back before any request (`notUpdated` with the same restart advice, counted in `skipped`) and sends only the never-sent ones; the confirm, built before, still counts them as updated in place, as with a serve.py from before /notes. A cached detect() answer without `replaceOnly` is checked again by the next planAll (`modeFor`), so the first Send all after `marginalia restart` updates them. With several vaults and none chosen nothing is written (only the cards that would go fail). A sticky "Sending N cards to Obsidian…" toast (N: the cards that go) shows while it runs; the result replaces it (same toast id).
- **Send all, link mode** (detect() says "link": the artifact, `file://`, a server without the Obsidian API or one that stopped answering, an empty vault list): only the prompts never sent (no `obsidianSentAt`) and without the pending marker go (FEATURES A9.1: every card with the marker, never sent or not, is held back, listed in `held` and counted in `skipped`, and named by the held line, "<N> card(s) may already be in Obsidian: …", in the toast, in the copy's toasts too, and in the confirm), their blocks joined by a blank line in ONE `obsidian://new` link to `<obsidianFolder>/<nickname>.md`, through the opener, with "# <name>" and a blank line first under the single link send's title rule (`knownFile`); each records `obsidianSentAt` and `obsidianFile`. Prompts sent before are skipped (a link can only add) and counted in `skipped`; the edited ones among them are also listed in `notUpdated` ("links can only add") and named in the toast, with nothing offered to copy, since their earlier copy is still in the note. Above `URI_LIMIT` nothing opens: the joined blocks (no title) are copied ("Notes copied — paste them into <file>"; a refused copy: "The browser blocked the clipboard — the notes were not copied" with "Copy notes"), nothing is recorded, and the result has mode "copy" and error "too_long". The edited cards a link cannot update and the held-back ones are not in the copy, and both toasts, as well as those of the retry from its "Copy notes", carry their line as the detail: "M edited card(s) was/were not updated: links can only add.", then "Start Marginalia with the marginalia command" (or, after an older server answered, "Restart Marginalia (marginalia restart in Terminal)") and " to write silently and update in place." (with held-back cards the held line takes the advice's place); with no edited or held-back card there is no detail. With `fromGesture` false the toast "Ready for Obsidian · N cards · <file>" offers "Add to Obsidian". A browser that cannot open the link fails every card.
- **Send all, feedback and refusals** (toast id "obsidian"): "Sent to Obsidian · <file>: A added, U updated, K already there" (K: the unchanged cards) with "Open in Obsidian"; <file> is the note that got the most cards. Server detail: "M edited card(s) was/were not updated: its/their earlier copy is no longer in the note." (the `notFound` cards; the toast then also offers "Copy notes" for their blocks and stays 12 s) and/or "M edited card(s) was/were not updated: restart Marginalia (marginalia restart in Terminal) to update it/them in place." (the `notUpdated` ones), then "Also in <others>."; a rechecked card (sent before, not edited since, its last send did not finish) is never called edited: "M card(s) whose last send did not finish was/were not updated: …", with the same reasons, on a line of its own after the edited cards' (repair D5 of the A9 review, 2026-11-10). Link detail: "Sent through an obsidian:// link.", then "M edited card(s) was/were not updated: links can only add." when there are any, then the held line for held-back cards (FEATURES A9.1; the toast then stays 12 s), else the start or restart advice. When a link held back every card it could send, the held line is the toast's text (the not-updated line its detail), with nothing to copy. When nothing was written but some edited cards were not updated, that line is the toast's text (the next one, if any, its detail), with "Copy notes" for the not-found cards. With failures: "N sent, F could not be sent — <first reason>", the not-updated lines as its detail, "Copy notes" (the failed blocks, then the not-found ones, images as "[image]", for the first such block's note) and, when some were sent, "Open in Obsidian". The cards a request may have written all the same (no answer, a 5xx, a 2xx that could not be read, a block left out of the results, an unexpected error once the request left; they keep the marker) are counted apart (repair D2, 2026-11-10): "N sent, U may already be in Obsidian — <their first reason>" with the detail "Send it/them again once Marginalia's server answers: it checks the note and never adds a second copy." when they are all the failures, else "… could not be sent — <the first other reason>" with "U may already be in Obsidian (<their reason>). Send …" in its detail; their blocks are never in "Copy notes", and neither is the block of any failed card that holds the marker (an earlier send of it did not finish). `failed` still lists them all. The toasts when there is nothing to send are those of the library bullet above (sendAll shows the same ones when called with nothing to send). While `M.store.locked()` planAll and sendAll send nothing (no request, no link, no record; "Marginalia is open in another tab"), and a tab that steps aside during a run sends no further request (the remaining cards fail with that reason). While a send-all of a collection runs (`isSendingAll(id)`), a second one of it is refused (toast "Already sending “<name>” to Obsidian", error "already sending"; planAll answers `busy`); other collections may send at the same time.
- **No send while an import saves** (FEATURES A10.1, added 2026-11-11, part of the user's "Yes, close it" of 2026-11-09 for the two timing gaps left after A9): `M.exporter.importJSON` merges from the library as it is when the import begins and then saves its records one by one, so until its last save a card may still lack the marker its backup carries (`obsidianPendingFile`); a link sent then would add a copy the note may hold already (and the D4 re-merge would then drop the backup's marker, since the link recorded a later send into that note), and a server send could write it into another note. So while an import runs no Obsidian send leaves, in either mode: a single send ("Send to Obsidian", "Update in Obsidian", O's save), Send all (planAll answers `{ok: false, error: "an import is still saving", importing: true}` and the library's ⋯ item toasts instead of a confirm), and a send-all's link waiting for its toast's "Add to Obsidian". The toast (id "obsidian") says "Wait until the import finishes, then send again." with no actions (`M.obsidian.WAIT_IMPORT`), the result's error is "an import is still saving", and the card stays as it is: no marker written, no record, nothing offered to copy. The state is `M.exporter.isImporting()` (an M.exporter extension): a count raised synchronously when `importJSON` is called, before its first write (an async function runs up to its first await at once), and lowered in a `finally`, so a refusal, an early failure (not JSON, not a backup) or a failure in the middle of its saves never leaves it set; two imports at once keep it until both end. js/obsidian.js checks it when a send starts, again once `detect()` has answered, right before each request's write-ahead (in the same task, so nothing can start an import between the check and the marker on the cached record; from then on the send is on its way, which the D4 re-merge keeps) and right before a link opens. A Send all already running stops before its next request (`ctl.stop`): the cards left fail with that reason, are never offered to copy, and the toast reads "N sent, F could not be sent — an import is still saving" with the wait line in its detail, or just the wait toast when nothing had gone. When the import ends (success or failure) `importJSON` emits `import:finished`, which M.backup uses ("Automatic backup", PDFs, restore).
- **A Send all link opened from its toast looks again** (FEATURES A10.2): the no-gesture Send all link path (the "Ready for Obsidian · N cards · <file>" toast, then "Add to Obsidian", up to 20 s later; no UI path reaches it today, every caller passes `fromGesture: true`) checks at the click, as the single send's `go()` does: a tab that stepped aside opens nothing and records nothing (repair D6), an import that is saving opens nothing (the wait toast, A10.1), and every planned card is read again from the cache: one that holds the marker now (a server send of it began meanwhile, or an import brought it) is held back, counted in `held` and `skipped` and named by the A9.1 line ("1 card may already be in Obsidian: …", the toast then 12 s; with every card held, that line is the toast's text and nothing opens); one sent meanwhile (`obsidianSentAt` set, say by its own link) is "already there" (`unchanged`), never added twice; one deleted meanwhile is left out. The link carries the rest, its blocks as planned, its "# <name>" title decided again by `knownFile` at the click (a note written meanwhile gets no second title line), and each of those cards records its send; above `URI_LIMIT` it is copied instead, as before.
- **Send all and an open prompt list** (js/list.js): each record is its own synchronous `prompt:saved`, and `M.list` used to render every row again for each one, so a send-all from the deck page (whose list is open) built N·N rows in one run and a deck of a few hundred cards froze the tab for seconds. Now a `prompt:saved` whose prompt keeps its place replaces only that prompt's row (`updateRow`): the prompt is still this list's and live, the search still shows it, it still sorts between its neighbours, and no row editor is open; focus and the selection stay on it. Every other case (a delete, a restore, a move, a re-context, a save that changes the order or what the search shows, a save under an open row editor) re-renders the list as before. N records cost N row builds. No SPEC §17 signature or event changes. Since FEATURES A9 (2026-11-10) the send record no longer saves through `savePrompt` at all: `M.cache.patchPrompts` changes the cached records in place and emits no `prompt:saved`, so a send-all's markers and records build no row (a 200-card send-all: none; test/deck-sendall-render.test.js), and the rows, which hold the cache's own records, show "Update in Obsidian" when their menu opens.
- **`M.cache.patchPrompts(changes, {now})`** → `Promise<number>` (an M.cache extension, FEATURES A9, 2026-11-10; js/cache.js): the quiet batch save of fields that are neither content nor derived, which only the Obsidian send record uses. `changes` = `[{id, fields: {name: value}}]`, a value of `undefined` removing that field. Each cached record (a tombstone too; an unknown id is skipped) gets the fields in place, so it keeps its identity and whatever holds it (a margin card, a list row and its menu, an open editor, which saves a copy of it) sees them; no event is emitted. A record that really changes gets `updatedAt` = now and nothing else: `contentUpdatedAt`, the content key and `dueAt` stay. The changed records go to the store in ONE `prompts.putMany` (one transaction), and it resolves with the number of records changed once they are stored. A failed write rejects, as `savePrompt`'s does (repair A9-R3 of the A9 review, 2026-11-10; before, it resolved anyway, so the write-ahead sent its request on a marker the store never took): code "store" (`err.cause` = the store's error) after the toast "Could not save", or code "locked" when the lock refused it (no toast); the changed records keep their in-memory state, as an edit does. It rejects, changing nothing, with code "bad_patch" for an entry without an id or fields or for a field name that is content, derived or not a plain name (`id`, `collectionId`, `kind`, `text`, `tags`, `flagged`, `anchor`, `context`, `cards`, `dueAt`, `createdAt`, `updatedAt`, `contentUpdatedAt`, `deletedAt`, `v`; names must match `[A-Za-z][A-Za-z0-9]*`), and with code "locked" in a tab that stepped aside. No SPEC §17 signature changes; no new event.
- **Send all (server)**: `POST /api/obsidian/notes` (tools/serve.py `write_notes`), same guards as /note, at most 100 MB (413 "the notes are larger than 100 MB", before the body is read) and 2000 blocks (400). Request-level checks refuse the whole request and write nothing: the Unicode of vault, file, title and image names, the vault (400 unknown, 409 a name two vaults share), the file (400/403), the images (400/403), a missing, empty or non-list `blocks`, and a blockId given twice (400 "blockId X is in the request twice; nothing was written"). Each block is then checked like a single send (blockId pattern, valid Unicode, `check_block`) and, when it carries `"replaceOnly"` (FEATURES A8), that must be JSON true or false: any other value, null included, fails the block with "replaceOnly must be true or false" (never read as "add it"). A bad block is reported and skipped, and with no valid block the answer is 400 "no block could be written: <first reason>", carrying the same `results`. The note is read once (again only when another app saved it meanwhile, see Writes), the blocks are upserted in their order in memory with exactly the text of one /note request after another (`upsert_blocks`), and the note is written once under `NOTE_LOCK` (atomic; CRLF, byte order mark, mode and extended attributes kept; read-only or hard-linked 409; a new note starts with `# <title>`; images as /note, except those of a block left out, below). A `replaceOnly` block (an edited card sent before, or a rechecked one) only ever replaces: it replaces the span `find_block` claims for it in the note as the blocks before it left it, exactly like any other block; when there is no such span it is neither added nor written, and its result is exactly `{blockId, ok: false, notFound: true, error: "its earlier copy is no longer in the note"}` (`EARLIER_COPY_GONE`). That covers a copy deleted, a copy whose markers or date line were edited by hand, two blocks merged, a copy only inside a code fence, and a date-only copy sent through an `obsidian://` link, which the app's linked block cannot claim (see Upsert). When every valid block of a request is such a one, nothing at all is written (no note, folder or image; an existing note is not even rewritten: same inode and mtime) and the answer is 200 `{ok: true, file, created: false, results}`; in a mixed request the note ends exactly as single /note sends of only the blocks that went would leave it. The images of a block left out are not written either, also in a mixed request (added 2026-11-09; before, such an image landed in the vault although no block of the note embeds it): an image whose name (`IMAGE_NAME_RE`, found in the block's Markdown as `![[name]]`) only left-out blocks embed is held back; one that a block going in also embeds is written, and so is one that no block of the request embeds, as /note writes it. `rewrite_note(vault_real, target, make, img_dir, img_targets, images_for=None)` picks the images again for each text it makes (`images_for(result)`; /note passes none and writes them all), so a copy that a save in Obsidian puts back while the send runs gets its image. An image a first try wrote stays when a save then leaves its block out of the next try, since nothing in the vault is ever removed. The page still sends every image of its cards, each once per request; any later embed form must keep the file name literally in the block's text, or the image of a block left out would be written again. A read-only, hard-linked or non-UTF-8 note still refuses the whole request with 409 before any block is looked at (so that answer carries no `notFound` result). → `{ok: true, file, created, results: [{blockId, ok: true, replaced} | {blockId, ok: false, error} | {blockId, ok: false, notFound: true, error}]}`, one per request block in order (blockId null for a block without a string blockId); a found `replaceOnly` block answers `replaced: true`. `upsert_blocks()` gives the result of sequential `upsert_text` calls in time linear in the note and the blocks. `find_span()` looks for a block's start-marker line (`str.find`, or the offsets it is given) and reads only that block's lines, and a `FenceIndex` holds the fence intervals and dangling openers. `upsert_blocks` items are `(block, id)` or `(block, id, replace_only)`, and each answer is True (replaced), False (added) or None (a `replace_only` block left out; `is_linked(block, id)` tells whether it carries its own link, find_block's `linked`). The `replace_only` blocks left out before the first block that goes in are looked up in the unchanged note (`_left_out`: one `START_ANY_RE` pass, at most one `FenceIndex`). That first block goes in on its own (`_upsert_in_order`, the earlier algorithm: the index kept up to date per edit, rebuilt when a new block's closing fence could pair with a dangling opener above it or when the old text ended in a lone CR), which leaves the note ending in a line end. The rest go in batches (`_upsert_batch`): `START_ANY_RE` finds every start-marker text of the request's ids in one pass, each span is looked up in the batch's base (`find_span(text, id, linked, fences, places)`), and the edits are spliced in once (`_spliced`). A block whose closing fence could pair with a dangling opener above it (where `FenceIndex.replaced` or `appended` would answer None) ends its batch, and the next batch starts from the note as it is then; a left-out block makes no edit and never ends a batch, so it stays linear; two blocks with one id, or an id that is not a blockId, go one at a time. `rewrite_note`'s `make()` may answer the text None, meaning nothing to write: then nothing is made and it answers `(False, result)`. The note's folder is resolved and checked by `make_dirs_inside` before the read when it exists, and made only once there is something to write (so a 400 for text that is not valid Unicode no longer leaves an empty folder behind, on either route). 2000 blocks of about 5 KB holding 𝑥 (10.9 MB) take about 0.2 s to add and 0.3 s to update (about 10 s each before the batches), and 2000 short blocks into a 2000-block note about 0.35 s (about 30 s one `upsert_text` at a time). Generated-note tests compare both with `upsert_text` / `find_block`, with the self-checks on (`_check_index`: every FenceIndex kept up to date against one built afresh, and every batch span against `find_span` on the note as it is then, `_check_batch`). The single /note endpoint behaves as before (it never reads `replaceOnly` and adds a block it does not find); its code was split into helpers `pick_vault`, `read_for_update`, `image_plan` and `write_images`, which /notes shares. The page reads 404, 405 or 501 from /notes as an older server, so /notes never refuses with one of those (it answers 400, 403, 409, 411, 413, 415 or 500, as /note does).
- **Prompt links**: `#reader/<collectionId>/<promptId>` (at boot and on hashchange, even with the book open already) routes to the reader with that promptId, which scrolls to the passage and focuses the card (the "Go to source" path); the written hash drops the promptId again. A deck id in such a link opens the deck page. A promptId that is not a live prompt of that book (deleted, unknown, moved to another collection) toasts "That note is no longer in this book": with the book open already the reader stays where it is, a fresh open goes to the last position. An "Open in Marginalia" link opens a new tab. That tab becomes the origin's one writing tab, and the tab that was already open shows "Marginalia is open in another tab" (see "One writing tab per origin"), so an older tab can never write back an older copy of the prompt, its `obsidianSentAt` / `obsidianFile`, the book or the settings, and it sends nothing to Obsidian.
- **Settings** › Obsidian: one status line naming the method ("Writes straight into the vault" or "Opens obsidian:// links — start Marginalia with the marginalia command to write silently"; a third form when `detect()` says `outdated`: "Opens obsidian:// links — restart Marginalia (marginalia restart in Terminal) to write silently", `METHOD_RESTART`), the vault (a select of the server's vaults, else a "Vault name" field), the folder (default "Notes/Marginalia"; its hint ends "Sending a note again updates its copy in the note that holds it, also after a rename or a folder change."), "Check again". `settings.obsidianVault` ("" default) is a vault's name exactly as Obsidian lists it, at most 200 characters: a control character (C0, DEL or C1) makes the value invalid, so a vault whose folder name holds a tab cannot be chosen; doubled, leading, trailing and no-break spaces stay, and only blanks become "". The name is compared exactly with the server's names and goes into `obsidian://` URIs as is. `settings.obsidianFolder` ("Notes/Marginalia" default) stays a one-line string of at most 300 characters, without leading, trailing or doubled "/". Both ride in the JSON backup settings.
