- **tools/serve.py** keeps serving the app folder (files with `Cache-Control: no-cache`) and adds the Obsidian endpoints, answered with `Cache-Control: no-store` (the bulk `POST /api/obsidian/notes` is in "Send all (server)" above; `GET` and `POST /api/backup` in "Automatic backup"): `GET /api/obsidian/vaults` → `{"vaults": [{"id", "name", "open", "attachmentFolder"}], "replaceOnly": true}` (the flag, from FEATURES A8 on, says this server's `/notes` honours a block's `replaceOnly`; a `/notes` from before it ignores the field and adds a block it does not find, so the page sends it no edited card, see "Send all, server mode") from obsidian.json (`--obsidian-config <path>`, else `MARGINALIA_OBSIDIAN_CONFIG`, else `~/Library/Application Support/obsidian/obsidian.json`; a vault whose folder is missing is skipped; one entry per name, the open one first; never an absolute path); `POST /api/obsidian/note` → upsert (next bullet) → `{"ok": true, "file", "created", "replaced"}`; errors `{"ok": false, "error"}` with 4xx/5xx: every string field must be valid Unicode (JSON can carry lone surrogates; 400 "the note holds text that is not valid Unicode", checked before anything touches the disk, and the note's bytes are ready before any image is written), a body nested too deep is "not JSON" (400), and anything unexpected is a 500 `{"ok": false, "error": "unexpected error"}` with the traceback only in the server log. A POST naming a vault whose name two vault folders share gets 409 ("two Obsidian vaults are named “X”, so it is not clear which one to write into; rename one of their folders"), so an update never follows Obsidian's open flag into the other vault; one folder listed twice still counts as one vault. `make_server(directory, bind, port, obsidian_config=None, quiet=False, backup_dir=None, app_version=None)` is importable for tests (`backup_dir` None: no backup API at all, see "Automatic backup"; `app_version`: the desktop app's `--app`, see "Desktop app (binding)", part 2).
- **Upsert** (`find_block`): the block replaces a span only where the note provably holds that one block: the exact start-marker line of that id outside code fences; then, before any other line that reads like a marker (`<!--\s*(?:/\s*)?marginalia`, any case, ASCII rules, fenced or not, indented or with text around it; the same lines as `<!--\s*/?\s*marginalia`, in linear time on long runs of spaces), the exact end-marker line outside fences; between them exactly one date line outside fences (`[Open in Marginalia](…) · YYYY-MM-DD` or the date alone), the line right before the end marker, and that date line is the block's own. When the POSTed block carries its link (every block the app sends to the server does: the API answers only on 127.0.0.1 and localhost, where `blockFor` adds the link), the span's date line must link to `#reader/<collectionId>/<blockId>` (any collection id, so a prompt moved to a deck still finds its block), and no other line of the span (fenced or not, edited or not) may start with that link; a date-alone span is matched only by a date-alone block, so the app's updates append a fresh linked copy once rather than replace a block written through `obsidian://` links away from 127.0.0.1 (later updates replace that copy); a Send all `replaceOnly` block appends nothing and answers `notFound` instead ("Send all (server)"), so only the card's own Send to / Update in Obsidian adds that linked copy. The first such span wins; in every other case the block is appended after one blank line, so a hand edit that deletes or changes marker lines (an end marker, the next block's start, the two comment lines between blocks), or merges two blocks by hand (A's date line, A's end marker and B's start marker deleted or typed over, or A's date line edited with the comment lines deleted), never lets a replacement take the user's text or the next blocks, and a copy of a block inside a code fence is never the one replaced. Code fences in the existing note are read as CommonMark, except that an opening fence with no closing fence anywhere below it is not a fence (Obsidian ends such a fence with its list item, or never reads it as one inside an HTML comment, an HTML block or `$$` math), so a stray fence above the blocks does not turn every update into a copy. The trade-off: a complete copy of a block inside a truly unclosed fence can be the one replaced, and two stray fences that pair with each other still mark the lines between them as code. Two complete copies of one block merged into one span by hand (the first copy's date line and end marker and the second copy's start marker deleted) still read as that one block and are replaced, like text typed inside a block's own markers. A POSTed block must itself have that shape (`check_block`, with the strict CommonMark fence reading): one start line, one end line, no other marker-like line, its only date line right before the end marker and that date line its own (its own link, or the date alone; a link to another blockId, or a second line starting with its own link, is a 400), and no code fence left open, else 400. A byte order mark before the first line stays.
- **Writes**: missing folders and the note are created; images are written to the vault's attachment folder (`attachmentFolderPath` of `<vault>/.obsidian/app.json`: "/" or none = the vault root, "./x" = next to the note) only when no file of that name exists; writes are atomic (temp file in the same folder + `os.replace`), UTF-8, with a trailing newline, CRLF notes keep CRLF, and nothing else in a note is removed or changed. The note's mode is kept, and on macOS its extended attributes (Finder tags among them) are copied onto the new file with copyfile(3) (`COPYFILE_XATTR` only, best effort). Its ACL is not copied: a note whose ACL denies delete cannot be replaced, and a temp file carrying that entry could not be removed either, so it would stay in the vault as an undeletable hidden file; without the copy, such an update fails with an error and leaves nothing behind. The creation date and file flags (such as Finder's hidden flag) are not kept either, because the note becomes a new file. A note that is read-only (chmod, a Finder lock, a deny-write ACL) or hard-linked (`st_nlink > 1`) is not rewritten: 409, nothing written. Both /note and /notes (`rewrite_note`) replace the note only while it is still exactly what the server read: right before `os.replace`, `note_unchanged` compares the same file (device, inode), size, `mtime_ns`, mode and flags, and the same bytes (a note that was missing must still be missing); otherwise `atomic_write(..., unchanged=)` raises `NoteChanged` and removes its temp file (`read_for_update` returns (text, mode, seen) for this). A save that lands while the new text is made (an edit in Obsidian, a sync, the note created meanwhile) is read in and the blocks upserted on top of it again, at most `NOTE_TRIES` (3) times in all; then 409 "the note kept changing while Marginalia was updating it (an edit in Obsidian, or a sync), so it was left as it is; send again" (`NOTE_CHANGED`), which the page reads as an ordinary refusal (a send-all fails that request's blocks with it and offers "Copy notes"). A note locked or made read-only meanwhile gets the read-only 409 on the next try. Images written by an earlier try stay, and later tries and sends reuse them (an image is written only when missing). Only the moment between the last check and `os.replace` stays unguarded. A `kill -9` or a power cut during a write can still leave one hidden `.marginalia-*.tmp` beside the note; nothing sweeps them.
- **Security**: the API answers loopback clients only (127.0.0.0/8, ::1, ::ffff:127.x; others get 403 "forbidden client", so `--bind 0.0.0.0` exposes the app, never the vault or the backups); only its ten routes, `GET /api/obsidian/vaults`, `POST /api/obsidian/note`, `POST /api/obsidian/notes`, `GET` and `POST /api/backup`, for the PDFs (FEATURES B9, "Automatic backup") `GET /api/backup/pdfs`, `POST /api/backup/pdf` and `GET /api/backup/pdf/<sha256>`, and (FEATURES E2.1, "Desktop app (binding)", part 2) `GET /api/backup/latest` and `GET /api/app` (a 404 on a server started without `--app`) (anything else under /api/ is 404, HEAD included; no OPTIONS handling, no CORS headers); 403 unless Host is `127.0.0.1:<port>` or `localhost:<port>`; a POST also needs Origin exactly `http://127.0.0.1:<port>` or `http://localhost:<port>`, `X-Marginalia: 1` and `Content-Type: application/json` (`application/pdf` for `POST /api/backup/pdf`), and at most 25 MB of body for /note (100 MB for /notes, 512 MB for /api/backup, 300 MB for a PDF), each refused (413) before the body is read; on a server started with `--app` (the desktop app's) every POST also needs `X-Marginalia-Client` (409 "app_only", checked after these and before the body is read; part 2); `GET /api/backup/pdf/<sha256>` and `GET /api/backup/latest` also refuse (403 "forbidden site") a request whose `Sec-Fetch-Site` is anything but `same-origin` or `none`, so no other web page can learn whether a PDF or a backup is saved; the vault must be one obsidian.json names; the file must be relative, end in `.md` and contain no `..`, backslash, control character or leading "/", and its real path (symlinks resolved) must stay inside the vault's real path; image names must be exactly `marginalia-[A-Za-z0-9_-]{1,80}\.(png|jpe?g|gif|webp)` and blockIds exactly `[A-Za-z0-9_-]{1,128}` (`fullmatch`: no trailing newline), and images must land inside the vault.
- **Options and start**: `--obsidian-config <obsidian.json>` (wins over the environment variable) and, for test servers, `--obsidian-sandbox <folder>` (next bullet); the two exclude each other. A blank `--obsidian-config`, `--obsidian-sandbox` or `MARGINALIA_OBSIDIAN_CONFIG` stops the server (exit 1, "Obsidian: …"), and a request never treats a blank `MARGINALIA_OBSIDIAN_CONFIG` as unset: it lists no vault, never Obsidian's own list. A file named by `--obsidian-config` or `MARGINALIA_OBSIDIAN_CONFIG` that lists no vault whose folder exists also stops the server, with the reason (no such file, a folder, unreadable, not JSON, no "vaults" list, none of its vault folders exists). Obsidian's own list may be empty, which means link mode. At start the server prints the address (the real port, so `--port 0` works), "Obsidian: vaults from <file>: <names>", naming a vault name two folders share, and "Backups: once a day into <folder> (the newest 30 are kept)" (see "Automatic backup"; that folder is checked first, so a refused start writes nothing, not even a sandbox). SIGTERM (`marginalia stop`, `marginalia restart`) waits at most `STOP_WAIT` (2 s in all; bin/marginalia waits 3 s) for a note or image write in progress (every vault write runs under `NOTE_LOCK`) and a backup write (`BACKUP_LOCK`), then ends as SIGTERM would, so a stop never leaves a partial `.marginalia-*.tmp` in the vault or the backup folder. A page whose send was cut by the stop sees a network error although the note was written (its toast says the card may already be in Obsidian and offers nothing to copy); sending again updates it in place, in that note also after a rename, a folder change or a move to a deck (the card remembers it, `obsidianPendingFile`, written before the request left; see Feedback), and an `obsidian://` link leaves the card alone until then (FEATURES A9.1).
- **Sandbox** (`--obsidian-sandbox <folder>`, for test servers): a throwaway vault `<folder>/Sandbox Vault` (with `.obsidian/app.json`, created when missing) and `<folder>/obsidian.json` naming only it. That obsidian.json is written at every start only when it is missing or is the sandbox's own listing (only "vaults", holding only "marginalia-sandbox" at `<folder>/Sandbox Vault`); any other file there (Obsidian's list, a test list, an edited sandbox list, a symlink, a folder) is refused and left as it is. Also refused, before anything is written: a folder inside a vault, Obsidian's own settings folder (the folder of `default_config_path()`) or a folder inside it (symlinks and other letter case count), and a symlinked `Sandbox Vault`. Sandbox folders written by the earlier code are recognised and keep working. The sandbox folder also holds `Backups`, the test server's backup folder unless `--backup-dir` or `MARGINALIA_BACKUP_DIR` names another one (made by the first backup; "Automatic backup"). A sandbox page stays in server mode only while its server answers and `Sandbox Vault` exists; nothing in the page knows it is a sandbox. Once the server stops, the next send fails ("Marginalia's server did not answer") and every later send while it stays down opens an `obsidian://` link, as on any other page (a page that opens Settings while the server is down links on its very first send). When `Sandbox Vault` is removed while the server runs, sends fail with "unknown vault" until the page checks again (Settings, a reload), and from then on the empty vault list means links as well. While the cached answer is "link", a send on the 127.0.0.1 or localhost page checks again first, so silent writes resume as soon as the server answers again. Those links would reach the real Obsidian, which writes into the vault it has open (no vault name is in the link while `settings.obsidianVault` is blank), so a test always replaces the opener (next bullet).
- **Testing a send in a browser**: only on a test server of your own with a throwaway vault and a throwaway backup folder (`python3 -I tools/serve.py --port 8791 --dir <app> --obsidian-sandbox <scratch folder> --backup-dir <scratch folder>/backups`, or `--obsidian-config` / `MARGINALIA_OBSIDIAN_CONFIG` naming a throwaway obsidian.json and `MARGINALIA_BACKUP_DIR` a throwaway folder), never on 8765: the user's `marginalia` server writes into the real vault and the real backup folder (the `marginalia` launch configuration only attaches to it, as `marginalia-test` attaches to 8791; neither starts a server). A sandbox test server keeps its page's backups in `<sandbox folder>/Backups` when neither `--backup-dir` nor `MARGINALIA_BACKUP_DIR` names a folder, so the sandbox command in CLAUDE.md is safe as written. So does a test server started with `--obsidian-config` or `MARGINALIA_OBSIDIAN_CONFIG` naming a vault list other than Obsidian's own: its page's backups go to a `Backups` folder beside that obsidian.json (FEATURES B8; "Automatic backup", Folder), never into `~/Documents/Marginalia Backups`, where a test library's file would take the day's name (the user's automatic backup of that day would then be skipped) and its "Back up now" would replace the user's file of the day. Giving every test server `--backup-dir` as well stays the rule. Nothing in a test opens an `obsidian://` URI (the Claude app would hand it to the real Obsidian): replace the opener with `M.obsidian._setOpener(fn)` and assert on the URI, also on a sandbox page (above: it falls back to links when its server stops).
- **The switch** (DECISIONS D32, added 2026-11-17: the user asked that "the obsidian connection be an option in settings", so that other people can use the app): `settings.obsidian` is `true`, `false` or `null` (never chosen; the default; any other stored value loads as `null`). `M.obsidian.enabled()` → the stored choice; never chosen: `true` only while a record of the library holds a send record (`obsidianSentAt` above 0, or the marker `obsidianPendingFile`; deleted records count), so a library that used Obsidian before the switch existed keeps the connection, and a new library starts without it. Settings › Obsidian starts with the switch "Connect Marginalia to Obsidian" (`input.settings__obsidian-switch`, role switch, in a `label.settings__check`). Below it, only while the connection is on, `.settings__obsidian-body` holds the method line, the vault, the folder and "Check again"; while it is off, the line `.settings__obsidian-off` says what the switch does. A change stores `true` or `false` (never `null`) and, when it turns the connection on, runs `detect({force: true})`. While the connection is off: (1) nothing is asked: `openSettings` does not call `detect`, so no page code calls `GET /api/obsidian/vaults`; (2) the pages offer no Obsidian command: the adder's "Note → Obsidian" button is hidden (`is-hidden`; set when the adder is built, each time it shows, and on `settings:changed` "obsidian"), `M.reader.run("obsidianNote")` and `createFromSelection("obsidian")` return false, the ? sheet has no row for O, the card menu and the list row menu have no "Send to Obsidian" / "Update in Obsidian", the book, deck and deck page menus have no "Send all to Obsidian", and `M.margin.openEditor({obsidian: true})` opens the plain editor (a tab that brings an O editor back, D30); (3) no send leaves: `send` and `sendAll` toast `M.obsidian.OFF` "The Obsidian connection is off — turn it on in Settings › Obsidian." and answer `{ok: false, error: OFF}` (no request, no link, no marker, nothing copied), `planAll` answers `{ok: false, error: OFF, off: true}` and the library toasts OFF. The switch is looked at after the tab lock and the desktop app's cover (their words come first) and before the import check, when a send starts: a send that is under way when the switch goes off finishes, a send-all's remaining requests too (a note is never left half sent). A toast's button that is clicked after the switch went off opens no link, records nothing and toasts OFF: "Open in Obsidian" (`openNote`), and "Add to Obsidian" of a send without a gesture, single or send-all. Settings › Obsidian follows a change made elsewhere (`settings:changed` "obsidian": another window of the Mac app through `M.settings.reload()`, an import that restores settings; `st.settings.paintObsidian`), and its "Check again" asks nothing while the connection is off. The adder takes its place again when the switch changes while it shows. Known edges: an O editor that is open when the switch goes off keeps the words "Save + Obsidian"; its save stores the note, and the send then says OFF. An import without "Also restore settings" brings the cards' send records and not the backup's choice, so in a library that never chose, the connection goes on with the first imported card that was sent (README "Obsidian" says so). A pane that is open at that moment (no `settings:changed` comes) shows the old state until Settings opens again; nothing leaves meanwhile. `detect()` itself is not gated. Each UI module asks through its own `obsidianOn()`: no `M.obsidian` → off; an `M.obsidian` without `enabled` → on. The JSON backup's settings carry `obsidian` (`M.exporter.SETTINGS_KEYS`); an import with "Also restore settings" sets it only when the backup holds `true` or `false`. "Open in Marginalia" links, the cards' send records and tools/serve.py do not change with the switch: the server's Obsidian endpoints stay, and no page calls them while the connection is off. Tests: `test/settings.test.js` (the three values, a stored other value), `test/obsidian.test.js` and `test/obsidian-sendall.test.js` ("the switch": enabled(), nothing leaves while off, the lock and the cover first, the toast buttons after the switch went off), `test/exporter.test.js` (the backup setting), `test/library-dom.test.js` (the pane, a change made elsewhere, "Check again" while off, the menus with the deck page, the toast), `test/reader-dom.test.js` (the adder when built, each time it shows and when the switch changes; O), `test/margin-dom.test.js` and `test/list-dom.test.js` (the menus, the plain editor), `test/app.test.js` (the ? sheet with the real modules). Every other Obsidian test switches the connection on first.
- **Tests**: `test/obsidian.test.js` (helpers, toObsidian, URIs, the 60 000 limit, the opener, detect and send in both modes with stubbed fetch, well-formed text, the body guards in serve.py's block shape (the own date line included), the link-mode title, "Copy note" after an unexpected failure; re-send targets after a rename, a folder change or a move, an older server and the restart wording, the cached-link re-check, the toast's word on earlier copies, "Copy note" with "[image]" and the refused-copy retry, quote escaping, verbatim vault names, the linear marker pattern, no send from a tab that stepped aside; 74 tests, among them, for FEATURES A8, the single send dated before an edit made during its request and detect()'s `replaceOnly` only for an exact `true`, and, for a lost answer, a single send whose answer never came (no answer, or a 200 whose body never came) followed by a rename: the next send goes to that note and updates the copy; a request that never reached the server is added there once; a refusal is written ahead and put back, and an update of a card already recorded in that note whose answer never came keeps its marker (A9.2); a remembered path Marginalia could not have written is ignored, and a recorded send wins; for FEATURES A9, a marked card never goes through a link, as "Send to" or "Update in Obsidian", above URI_LIMIT (nothing copied), without a gesture or after an older server answered (the restart wording), while a card without a marker, or with one Marginalia could not have written, still goes; the marker in place when /note leaves and settled by 200, 409, 413, 500 and no answer; a card that holds it already is not written ahead again; two sends of one card at once; for the repairs of 2026-11-10, a send that left no telling (no answer, 500, a 502 that is not JSON, a cut body) says "May already be in Obsidian" with nothing to copy, a definite refusal still offers "Copy note" unless the card holds the marker, an unexpected failure before the request offers it and one after does not, and a tab that steps aside while a link send checks the server, or before "Add to Obsidian" is clicked, opens no link; 78 tests in all), `test/obsidian-writeahead.test.js` (7 tests, FEATURES A9.2 with the real `M.cache` and `M.store`, memory backend: the marker in the store when the request leaves and after a tab that never sees the answer, which a reload reads and a link then holds back; a send-all whose answer never came, a rename, then the copies updated in that note, one each; for the repairs of 2026-11-10, with the real `M.exporter.importJSON` too: a claude.ai copy that already holds the cards takes the marker from a backup made since and holds them back from every link, then drops it with a backup made after they were settled (D1); an import still saving its merged records while a send is on its way keeps that send's marker, or its record when the answer came first (D4); a write-ahead the store refuses sends nothing, single or Send all, and the next send goes once the store takes writes again (A9-R3)), `test/obsidian-import.test.js` (10 tests, FEATURES A10, the real exporter, cache and store: in server and link mode a single send, O's save and Send all while an import saves are refused with the wait toast, no request, no link, the card unchanged, and go once it finished; nothing stored while locked), wiring in reader-dom (O, the adder, prompt links to missing notes) / app / margin-dom (the blank free note) / list-dom / library-dom (also the restart line and vault names with spaces in the select) / exporter (obsidianSentAt and obsidianFile) / editor (hint) / anchors (the quote cut) tests, `test/tabs.test.js` (see "One writing tab per origin"), `test/obsidian-sendall.test.js` (61 tests, Send all: reading order, grouping by target note, splitting by size and block count, the records, images once per request, the per-block /note fallback for an older server, per-block and per-request failures, no answer, the 413 wording, several vaults with none chosen, link mode with the title rule and the skipped cards, the copy above URI_LIMIT and the refused copy, no gesture, a browser that cannot open links, a locked tab before and during a run, no double run of one collection; for A8: `sendState`, a second send-all right after the first sends nothing and makes no request at all, editing one card sends exactly that card with `replaceOnly`, `notFound` records nothing, is listed and offers "Copy notes", every edited card not found (answers 200, 400 and 409), a failure plus a not-found card, an edit while the block is on its way (as a new record and as an in-place change), without a chosen vault only the cards that would go fail, deck naming; an older serve.py: edited cards never go to /note, and a serve.py whose GET /vaults lacks `replaceOnly` gets no edited card until it is restarted, when the next send-all checks again and updates them; the images are picked again when a save puts a copy back meanwhile), the value must be a boolean, the single endpoint never reads it, a copy deleted while its update is sent is not added again, and a property test through the endpoint on 150 random hand-edited notes; a `replace_only` block never adds a block of its id (a property test on 2300 generated notes against the `one_by_one` oracle, `_check_index` on), the left-out blocks before the first block that goes in (no final newline, a lone CR, a byte order mark, an empty note, CRLF), and linear time with 2000 left-out or replace-only blocks; the backup routes, see "Automatic backup"; the few tests that run tools/serve.py itself use port 0, are killed if they ever serve, set HOME to a fake home whose Obsidian list names only a decoy vault, and get a temp backup folder). `marginalia test` runs `python3 -I -m unittest discover -s test -p 'test_*.py'` after the node checks.

## One writing tab per origin (binding; added 2026-11-07)
(A "tab" in this section is a whole page: a browser tab, or the Mac app's window. The tabs of D30, "Tabs" below, are places inside one page; they change nothing here: a page is still one writer.)
(Since D31 the pages of the Mac app do not use this rule: its windows hand the writer role to each other, "Windows" below. A browser page keeps the rule as it is written here, and so does the app's page when Web Locks are missing.)
Every Marginalia tab of an origin shares one IndexedDB database, but each keeps its own in-memory copy (`M.cache`) and writes whole records from it, and an "Open in Marginalia" link from Obsidian (or running `marginalia` again) opens a second tab while the first stays open. The older tab's next save (a flag, a grade, the reading position at a tab switch) silently undid what the newer one had stored. Now the newest tab writes and every older tab steps aside.
- **Protocol** (js/store.js): once the database is open in idb mode (after `dataMigrate`, before `M.cache.load`), a tab sends `{type: "open", id, at}` on BroadcastChannel "marginalia" (`TAB_CHANNEL`) and writes localStorage "marginalia.tab" (`TAB_KEY`) = `{id, at}`. The storage event is the fallback; `pageshow` (persisted) and `visibilitychange` to visible re-read the key, for a tab that missed the message (frozen, back-forward cache). Newer means a later `at`, then the larger id; `at` = max(now, key.at + 1), so a later tab is always newer. A tab that hears an older announcement announces again, so exactly one of two tabs keeps writing; a tab that has not opened yet ignores announcements. The memory backend never takes part (its data lives only in its own tab).
- **Store**: a tab that hears a newer announcement locks at once. Extensions: `M.store.lock(info)` → info, `locked()` → `{reason, by, at}`|null, `tab()` → `{id, at}`|null, `checkTabs()` → boolean, `TAB_CHANNEL`, `TAB_KEY`, test hook `_setTabChannel(factory|null)`. New error code "locked": from then on every backend put, del and non-readonly tx rejects with it, and a write inside a transaction that is already running aborts the whole transaction; reads keep working. `docs.saveDocument` passes "locked" through as is (no stub record). `store:mode` gains reason "othertab", payload `{mode, reason: "othertab", message: "Marginalia is open in another tab", by}`, emitted once.
- **Cache**: while `M.store.locked()`, every writer leaves the maps, events and store alone. `savePrompt` rejects with code "locked" (`err.prompt` = the record); the other writers resolve with the record unchanged (`recontext` resolves 0), except `patchPrompts` (FEATURES A9) and the seven folder writers `createFolder`, `renameFolder`, `moveFolder`, `moveCollection`, `removeFolder`, `restoreFolder` and `saveFolders` (FEATURES L1.8, "Library folders"), which reject with code "locked" and change nothing. A refusal by the lock is never toasted "Could not save". In such a tab `M.library.attachPdf` refuses ("locked") and M.backup neither uploads nor restores a PDF ("Automatic backup"). New event (an addition to the SPEC §17.3 table): `prompt:refused {prompt, reason: "locked"}`, cache → app, emitted when the refused text is not blank and differs from this tab's copy.
- **Settings and Obsidian**: while locked, `M.settings.set` returns false and `M.settings.reset()` does nothing (`persist()` writes the whole settings object, which may be older than the newer tab's), and `M.obsidian.send` sends nothing: no POST, no link, `{ok: false, error: "Marginalia is open in another tab"}`, also when the tab steps aside while the send checks the server or before the toast's "Add to Obsidian" is clicked (the lock is checked again right before a link opens; repair D6, 2026-11-10); neither do `M.obsidian.planAll` and `sendAll` (a send-all stops sending once its tab steps aside). A request on its way when the tab steps aside keeps its cards' pending markers, written ahead while the tab was the active one: the answer's records are refused like any write, so the markers stay in the store for the active tab, which loads them, to settle (FEATURES A9.4). `M.backup` neither asks the server nor backs up in such a tab: boot does not start it, `runNow()` is refused, and `store:mode` "othertab" stops its hourly timer and visibility listener ("Automatic backup"). Their callers are user actions that the notice blocks anyway; the guards hold for any other caller. The API key (its own localStorage entry) is not guarded.
- **M.app**: on `store:mode` "othertab" a blocking `#tabLock` notice is appended to `#app`: role alertdialog, aria-modal, labelled and described, title "Marginalia is open in another tab", body text, and one primary button "Use this tab" that calls `location.reload()` (the reloaded tab announces itself and becomes the newest, so the other tab steps aside in turn). Unsaved text is shown in a read-only textarea through `M.util.escapeHtml`, with "Copy text" (`writeText` inside the click, else select + `execCommand("copy")`, and a status line). It comes from `M.margin.activeEditor()` and `M.library.activeEditor()` (the deck add-box and the list editor), counting only text that differs from the stored prompt and never a bare "Q.", and from `prompt:refused`. Focus starts on Copy text when there is text, otherwise on Use this tab. Lockout: the other `#app` children get `inert`; capture-phase keydown, paste and drop guards on window (Tab cycles inside the notice, Escape is swallowed, Cmd/Ctrl browser shortcuts still work) and a focusin guard keep focus in the notice; `route()`, `confirm()` and `toast()` do nothing, and boot stops before routing once locked. Extensions: `tabLock()` → `{texts, by}`|null and `_setReload(fn)` (test hook). The notice's CSS is in css/app.css: `#tabLock` (fixed, inset 0, z-index 2000, above the toasts at 1600, the confirm dialog at 1500 and the ? sheet at 1400) and `.tablock__panel` / `__title` / `__body` / `__unsaved` / `__label` / `__text` / `__actions` / `__status`, theme tokens only.
- **Limits**: a write the older tab issues in the few milliseconds before the new tab's announcement reaches it can be missing from the new tab's copy (the new tab reads in the same task that announces). In practice nothing writes then: the reading-position save on `visibilitychange` runs at the tab switch, well before the new page's scripts load; closing the window fully would need a handshake that delays every boot. A tab still running code from before this change does not step aside, so after updating Marginalia reload any tab that was already open.
- **Tests**: `test/tabs.test.js` drives two or more tabs of one origin in Node: each tab is a vm context running the real modules on one shared fake IndexedDB, with a BroadcastChannel hub and a shared localStorage that fires storage events. It covers the store and cache guard (every write path, a write queued before the lock, a running transaction rolled back whole, memory mode left alone), the announcement protocol (the storage-event fallback, the back-forward-cache and visibility re-check, the same-millisecond tie, a reload through "Use this tab"), the older tab's settings and Obsidian sends, a send under way when a newer tab opens (FEATURES A9.4: its marker in the store when the request left, the older tab's record of the answer refused, the newer tab holding the card back from a link and settling it through the server), the `#tabLock` notice in the real app on the tools/smoke.js fake DOM, and the notice's CSS; its L1.8 block checks that every folder writer of the older tab is refused ("locked"), the store untouched, while the newer tab's folder writes land.

## Automatic backup (binding; added 2026-11-09)
The user chose "option 2": Marginalia's own server saves the same file as the JSON backup once a day into a folder in Documents and keeps the newest 30 (no clicks, 30 days of history, and Time Machine copies `~/Documents`). It works only when Marginalia is started with the `marginalia` command (tools/serve.py), not in the claude.ai copy or a saved file.
- **Folder** (tools/serve.py `startup_backup`, `check_backup_dir`): `--backup-dir <absolute folder>`, else `MARGINALIA_BACKUP_DIR`, else, for a test server started with `--obsidian-sandbox <folder>`, `<folder>/Backups` (the sandbox folder's real path; `SANDBOX_BACKUPS`), else, for a server that reads a vault list other than Obsidian's own (FEATURES B8, added 2026-11-09: a test library's backup in the user's folder could become the day's file and make the user's own automatic backup skip that day), `<real path of that list's folder>/Backups`, else `~/Documents/Marginalia Backups`. That list comes from `--obsidian-config`, else `MARGINALIA_OBSIDIAN_CONFIG` (the option wins over the variable, and a sandbox wins over the variable too; `own_vault_list`). Obsidian's own list (`is_obsidians_own_list`) is the same file as `~/Library/Application Support/obsidian/obsidian.json` (symlinks resolved), or, while either file is missing, the same path in either letter case. So the user's own server, with no Obsidian option or one that names Obsidian's own list, keeps `~/Documents/Marginalia Backups`. A blank or relative value, the served app folder (`--dir`) or a folder inside it (symlinks resolved, either letter case) stops the server (exit 1, "Backups: …"): the static server must never serve a backup. A sandbox, or a vault list, inside the app folder is refused the same way ("Backups: --obsidian-sandbox's backup folder: …", "--obsidian-config's backup folder", "MARGINALIA_OBSIDIAN_CONFIG's backup folder"), before anything is written, and so is a vault list's `Backups` inside Obsidian's own settings folder, its real path checked so a symlinked `Backups` counts ("Backups: --obsidian-config's backup folder: … is inside Obsidian's own settings folder …, which Marginalia never writes into; give --backup-dir"); a blank `--obsidian-sandbox`, `--obsidian-config` or `MARGINALIA_OBSIDIAN_CONFIG` is left to the Obsidian check, which refuses it. This is checked before the Obsidian options, so a refused start writes nothing, not even a sandbox. Nothing is created at start; the first backup makes the folder (mode 0700). The third start line reads "Backups: once a day into <folder> (the newest 30 are kept)". `make_server(directory, bind, port, obsidian_config=None, quiet=False, backup_dir=None)`: `backup_dir` None means no backup API at all (`GET` and `POST /api/backup` answer 404 `{ok: false, error: "not found"}`, like a serve.py from before backups), so a test or another embedder never reaches the real folder unless told to; a bad folder raises ValueError. Only `main()` resolves the default. bin/marginalia is unchanged: it passes none of `--backup-dir`, `--obsidian-sandbox` and `--obsidian-config`, so the default applies.
- **`GET /api/backup`** → `{ok: true, dir (a display path, "~" for the home folder), today (the server's local date, YYYY-MM-DD), files: [{name, date, bytes}]}`, newest first. Only regular files (not symlinks, not folders) whose whole name is `marginalia-backup-YYYY-MM-DD.json` (`BACKUP_NAME_RE`, ASCII digits) are listed; a missing folder lists none. A folder that cannot be read, or a path that is not a folder, gives 500 `{ok: false, error}`. `GET /api/backup/latest` sends the first file of that listing as it is on disk (FEATURES E2.1d, for the desktop app's move; "Desktop app (binding)", part 2).
- **`POST /api/backup`** `{"mode": "auto"|"manual", "backup": <the JSON backup>}`: the same guards as the Obsidian POSTs (loopback client, Host, Origin, `X-Marginalia: 1`, `Content-Type: application/json`; see "Obsidian", Security), at most 512 MB (413 "the backup is larger than 512 MB", before the body is read). The body must be one JSON object (NaN, Infinity, trailing data, invalid UTF-8 and deep nesting get 400), mode exactly "auto" or "manual", and `backup` must look like the exporter's backup: format "marginalia", an integer version ≥ 1, `collections` and `prompts` lists, `attachments` a list when present (else 400). The server names the file, `marginalia-backup-<today>.json`, and writes the backup's own JSON text exactly as the request carries it (`json_object_spans` finds its span), so the file holds exactly the text Export would save (never re-serialized). "auto" never replaces today's file: it answers `skipped: true` (with that file's size and `pruned: []`), also for a file that appears during the write (the new file is put in place with a hard link, or with `os.replace` only while the name is free on a disk without hard links); "manual" replaces it (`os.replace`). Writes go through a temp `.marginalia-*.tmp` in the folder, fsync, mode 0600. A folder or symlink holding today's name is left alone (409). So is a file whose name is today's in other letters (such as `Marginalia-Backup-<today>.json`), which a disk that ignores letter case (macOS's default) reaches through today's name although GET never lists it and the prune never counts it (`check_todays_name`: the exact name must be in `os.listdir`): both modes answer 409 "<that name> in the backup folder has today's backup name in other letters, so it is left as it is; nothing was written", also when it appears during an automatic write, and the page records that as the day's failed attempt (one toast, no retry loop). Then the prune keeps the 30 newest matching regular files (`BACKUP_KEEP`, by the date in their names), never the file just written, and deletes the older ones permanently (not to the Trash); no other backup-folder file is ever touched, except the saved PDFs of the PDFs folder, by their own prune ("PDFs, the server" below), which runs right after. → `{ok: true, file, bytes, skipped, pruned: [names]}` (`pruned`: JSON backups only). One backup runs at a time (`BACKUP_LOCK`); SIGTERM waits for it ("Obsidian", Options and start). A `kill -9` or a power cut during a backup write can leave one hidden `.marginalia-*.tmp` in the backup folder (or, during a PDF upload, in its PDFs folder); nothing sweeps it, because the prunes only touch backup names and saved-PDF names.
- **`M.backup`** (`js/backup.js`, a module beside SPEC §17.2) loads after js/exporter.js and js/obsidian.js and before js/cache.js. The script order of index.html and tools/smoke.js `ORDER` (replacing SPEC §18's list and its "24 namespaces"): util, bus, settings, store, scheduler, prompts, similar, context, keys, layout, anchors, markup, exporter, obsidian, backup, cache, undo, attachments, pdfview, reader, editor, margin, list, snip, vision, review, stats, library, tabs, desktop, app: 31 namespaces (undo since D33, "Undo and redo"; tabs since D30, "Tabs"; desktop since the desktop app's part 2, "Desktop app (binding)"; similar since FEATURES P2.4, "Prompt craft"; stats since D27, "Scheduling and statistics"). tools/xref.py also checks that index.html loads every `js/*.js` exactly once, in smoke's `ORDER`; tools/xref-rt.js checks that every js file loads as a namespace; tools/bundle.py follows index.html.
- **API**: `start()` → `Promise<state>`; M.app's boot calls it once the store is open, the library loaded and the first screen routed, only in the tab that writes, and does not wait for it. `state()` → `{status: "checking"|"on"|"off"|"outdated"|"unavailable", dir, today, last: {name, date, bytes}|null, error, running, reason, enabled}`; `reason` is null, "artifact", "file", "memory", "no_fetch", "no_server", "server" or "outdated"; `error` says why the server cannot back up, or why today's automatic backup failed. "outdated": the page's own server on 127.0.0.1 or localhost answers 404, 405 or 501 (a serve.py from before backups, still running after an update; `marginalia restart` replaces it); on any other host that answer means "unavailable". Until start() has an answer the status is "checking". `runNow()` → `Promise<{ok, file, bytes, skipped, error}>`: a "manual" backup now (today's file replaced); it never toasts (its caller does) and is refused while `M.store.locked()` or with an empty library. `setEnabled(bool)` → boolean stores `settings.autoBackup` through `M.settings.set` (so nothing while locked); turned on, it checks at once. Extension `refresh()` → `Promise<state>`: a GET without backing up (Settings › Data), then, while the server answers, the PDF listing. FEATURES B9 extensions: `state().pdfs` (`{saved, bytes, dir, running, left, error, outdated}` or null; "PDFs, the page"), `savePdfs()` → `Promise<{ok, sent, failed: [{collectionId, name, error}], error}>` and `restorePdfs()` → `Promise<{ok, restored, failed, error}>` (one PDF run now, queued; "PDFs, the page" and "PDFs, restore"). Constants `STATUSES`, `HOUR_MS`, `VISIBLE_GAP_MS`, `MEMO_KEY`; test hooks `_setFetch(fn)`, `_setNow(fn)`, `_setTimers({setInterval, clearInterval})`, `_idle()` (a promise for the moment no PDF run is queued or running), `_reset()`. Every promise resolves, never rejects. New event (an addition to the SPEC §17.3 table): `backup:state` (payload: the state), backup → library, on every change. M.backup listens to `settings:changed` (key autoBackup), `store:mode` and `import:finished` (FEATURES B9.5, M.exporter → backup: the restore after an Import). The desktop app's part 2 (FEATURES E2.2) extends it: the client header on both POSTs, `runNow(opts)` and `savePdfs(opts)` with a `client`, `latest()`, `idle()` (the old `_idle()`), `libraryPdfs()`, `hasToday()` (repair SEC-1), and the refusals in a page the app replaced; see "Desktop app (binding)", part 2.
- **The automatic rule**: `GET /api/backup` at start, every hour, and when the tab becomes visible (at most once a minute; one check at a time). An automatic backup (mode "auto") goes only when the status is "on", `settings.autoBackup` is not false, the tab is not locked, the library holds at least one live (not deleted) collection or prompt, the server lists no file for its today, and no automatic attempt failed today. The body is exactly `{"mode":"auto"|"manual","backup":<text>}`, where text is `M.exporter.prepare("json", {scope: "all", collectionId: null, includeImages: true}).text` verbatim, the very call the manual JSON backup makes (prompts, review history, images and settings; never PDF bytes, never the API key). Headers `Content-Type: application/json` and `X-Marginalia: 1`, cache no-store, credentials same-origin. Refused in the page, without a request: above 512 MB ("the backup is larger than 512 MB"), and a body that holds the stored API key (16 characters or more, raw or JSON-escaped, because a prompt holds it: "a prompt holds your Anthropic API key — remove it from the prompt, then back up again").
- **Failures**: a failed automatic attempt (an HTTP refusal, no answer, a refusal in the page) is recorded in localStorage `marginalia.backup` = `{day, error}`, so it is not tried again that day, not even after a reload: there is no retry loop. When storage refuses the record (a full or blocked localStorage), the page keeps it in memory, and that in-memory record of today wins over whatever storage still holds (an older day's record, or a value that is not a record), so that page neither tries nor toasts again that day; a reload of such a page starts with empty memory and may try once more (still once). It toasts once (toast id "backup"): "Automatic backup failed — <reason>" with "Try again" (runNow: "Backing up…", then "Backed up · <dir>/<file> (<size>)" or "Backup failed — <reason>"). Any backup that succeeds clears the record. A GET without an answer makes the status "unavailable" (no_server) until a later check gets one; other error answers make it "unavailable" (server) with the server's own words (cut at 300 characters).
- **Nothing elsewhere**: no request (no PDF upload or restore either), no timer and no visibility listener in the store's memory mode, on a page not served over http(s) (`file://`, a saved `dist/marginalia.html`) or in the artifact build; state() says "unavailable" and why. A tab that steps aside (`store:mode` "othertab") stops its timer and listener for good, and boot never starts M.backup in a locked tab.
- **PDFs, the server** (FEATURES B9, added 2026-11-11; the user chose "Each PDF saved once": each PDF goes once into the backup folder's `PDFs` folder as a normal PDF file, the daily backup stays small and lists which PDFs belong to it, a PDF stays as long as any of the 30 kept backups still lists it, and a restore reconnects the PDFs from that folder by itself; not chosen: the PDFs inside every daily backup). tools/serve.py keeps them in `<backup folder>/PDFs` (`PDF_FOLDER`; the folder of "Folder" above), made with mode 0700 by the first upload that passes the header checks (so it can stay empty when that body is then refused); each saved PDF is a regular file, mode 0600, named `<book name> [<first 12 hex digits of its SHA-256>].pdf` (`pdf_file_name`). The book name is cleaned exactly as js/obsidian.js `cleanSegment`/`fileName` cleans an Obsidian note name (see "Obsidian", Where it goes: `\ / : * ? " < > | # ^ [ ]` and control characters dropped, whitespace collapsed, spaces and dots trimmed, `..` inside → "…", at most 120 characters), and the server adds two rules: at most 150 bytes of UTF-8 (so the whole name stays far below the 255-byte limit of macOS disks, also for titles in Chinese, Japanese or Korean) and one trailing ".pdf" dropped (a PDF's own file name sent as the book name does not give `Book.pdf [sha12].pdf`); "Untitled" when nothing is left. The name never holds a path separator or starts with a dot, so the file always lands directly in `PDFs/`. A **saved PDF** is a regular file (lstat: never a symlink or a folder) directly in `PDFs/` whose whole name matches `(?!\.).* \[([0-9a-f]{12})\]\.pdf` (`PDF_FILE_RE`); any such file that still holds that content counts as that content saved, whatever its book name (a renamed book, or a copy renamed by hand, makes no second copy), and nothing else in `PDFs/` is ever listed, sent, replaced or deleted. A saved PDF **changed after it was saved** (repair B9-R2 of 2026-11-11: opened and saved in another app, such as Preview saving highlights into it) keeps its name but no longer holds what its name says: the listing, which goes by name and hashes nothing, still lists it, but its bytes are never sent for that PDF, and an upload of that PDF or the prune (below) hashes it and renames it aside (`set_aside_pdf`) to `<book name> [<sha12>] (changed).pdf` (`(changed 2)` and so on while a name is taken, up to `PDF_ASIDE_TRIES`; the book name cut when the whole name would pass 255 bytes, `aside_name`), a name that never matches `PDF_FILE_RE`, so from then on that file and the user's changes in it are the user's: never listed, sent, replaced or deleted. The rename never replaces anything (a hard link, then the old name removed; `os.rename` while the new name is free on a disk without hard links), and the server log names both names. A `PDFs` that is a symlink or not a folder is never used (every PDF route answers 500, the prune deletes nothing). A server made with `make_server(backup_dir=None)` has no PDF routes either (404, like `/api/backup`). Routes, with the guards of `/api/backup` (loopback client, Host; POST: Origin, `X-Marginalia: 1`), no CORS, `Cache-Control: no-store`: **`GET /api/backup/pdfs`** → `{ok: true, dir, files: [{sha12, name, bytes}]}` sorted by name (`dir`: the backup folder as `GET /api/backup` shows it, the PDFs being in `<dir>/PDFs`; a missing folder lists `[]` and a GET never makes anything; two files may carry one sha12, both listed); **`POST /api/backup/pdf`**, body = the PDF's bytes, `Content-Type: application/pdf` (else 415), `Content-Length` required (411) and at most 300 MB, the app's `MAX_DOC_BYTES` (413 "the PDF is larger than 300 MB"), `X-Marginalia-Sha256` = the body's SHA-256 in 64 hex digits (400), all checked before the body is read, and `X-Marginalia-Name` = the book name percent-encoded (`encodeURIComponent`; optional, "Untitled"): the body is streamed in 1 MiB pieces into a temp file in `PDFs/` while it is hashed (never whole in memory), must hash to the header's SHA-256 and hold `%PDF-` within its first 1 KB, as js/library.js `looksLikePdf` accepts a short preamble (400), is fsync'ed and made 0600; then, under `BACKUP_LOCK`, every saved PDF with that sha12 is hashed (`verified_pdf`): one that holds this very PDF means nothing is written (`skipped: true`, with that file's name and size), one changed after it was saved is set aside first (500 "… was changed after it was saved (it is not that PDF any more), and it could not be renamed (…); nothing was written" when the rename fails); else the file is put in place with a hard link that never replaces anything (`os.replace` only on a disk without hard links, and only while the name is free), and a file that took that name meanwhile is hashed in turn (this PDF: skipped; anything else: set aside and the link tried once more); a name held by a folder, a symlink or a file that differs only in letter case gets 409 and nothing is written; every failure removes the temp file, and two uploads of one content at once leave one file → `{ok: true, file, bytes, skipped}`, plus `setAside: [{name, as}]` (the old and the new name of each saved PDF set aside on the way) when there are some; **`GET /api/backup/pdf/<sha256>`** (64 hex digits, either case) → the saved PDF whose content has exactly that SHA-256 (opened without following symlinks, a regular file only, hashed whole before the answer; a file whose content does not match is logged and never sent; none: 404 "no saved PDF has that SHA-256", with `changed: [names]` when saved PDFs with that sha12 hold something else, so the page can say which file was changed; a GET renames nothing), `application/pdf` with `Content-Length`, `nosniff` and `no-store`, streamed and hashed again with its last piece held back until that hash matches, so a file that changes mid-send never arrives whole (the page sees a cut body); a request whose `Sec-Fetch-Site` is anything but `same-origin` or `none` gets 403 "forbidden site" (else any web page could time an `<img>` of the address of a known PDF's hash and learn whether this user has that PDF; the page's own fetch is same-origin). **The prune** (B9.3, `prune_pdfs`) runs after every JSON backup actually written (automatic or manual, never after a skip), after the JSON prune and still under `BACKUP_LOCK`: it reads every backup the JSON prune leaves (normally the 30 newest; the one just written from the parsed request), collects the `sha256` of 64 hex digits of every live collection and of every tombstone deleted less than `DELETED_PDF_DAYS` (30) days ago (`collection_sha12s`; a tombstone is a `deletedAt` that js/exporter.js `stampOf` reads as one, a number that is finite and above 0: `is_tombstone`; its age counts from `now_ms()`, the Mac's clock in milliseconds as `Date.now()` counts, through `tombstone_cutoff()`; an older tombstone lists nothing; D23 of 2026-11-11), and deletes permanently only the saved PDFs whose sha12 none of them lists, each hashed first and deleted only when its content still has its name's sha12 (one changed after it was saved holds the user's own work: it is set aside instead and logged; one that cannot be read stays, logged), and lstat'ed again right before the unlink (never a symlink). It deletes nothing at all, and logs why, when any kept backup cannot be read or parsed or is not a Marginalia backup, when the backup just written is not among them, or when `PDFs` is not a folder; it never fails the backup or changes its answer, and logs what it deleted ("PDFs: deleted N that no kept backup lists: …"). A deleted book's PDF therefore stays 30 days after the deletion, also when "Back up now" replaced the only backup that listed the book alive (review safety-1 of 2026-11-11), and after that while a kept backup still lists the book alive; it goes with the first backup written once neither holds (when that is the backup whose JSON prune removes the last one that lists the book alive, in that same request: `prune_backups` runs before `prune_pdfs`). The user's choice of 2026-11-11, D23; before, every tombstone counted, so such a PDF stayed for good. Time Machine keeps older copies. A replaced file (Replace file keeps the book's id with the new file's `sha256`) stays while a kept backup lists the old `sha256` for a live book; D23's 30 days do not apply to it, so it normally stays about 30 daily backups, but goes at once when "Back up now" replaces the only backup that listed it (a file replaced on the day its book was first backed up; review9 safety-2 of 2026-11-11). The 30 days go by the Mac's clock (`now_ms()` against the page's `Date.now()`): a clock 30 or more days wrong, ahead at a backup or behind at the deletion, shortens them for a book that no kept backup lists alive (review9 safety-1; a known limit). SIGTERM (`marginalia stop`/`restart`) also removes the temp files of uploads under way (`PDF_TEMPS`, within `STOP_WAIT`). The tools/serve.py module docstring ("PDFs") describes the same contract; its code is the section "PDFs (FEATURES B9)" after `write_backup` (`pdf_book_name`, `pdf_file_name`, `pdf_files`, `save_pdf`, `place_pdf`, `open_pdf`, `prune_pdfs`, …).
- **PDFs, the page** (js/backup.js `savePdfs()`, FEATURES B9.4): after each backup written (an automatic one the server wrote, not one it skipped; and "Back up now", also while the switch is off) the page sends the PDFs the folder lacks, in the background, while `runNow()` has already answered. With no live book whose bytes are in this browser (`hasBytes` not false) and that has a SHA-256 (64 hex digits; `hashAlgo` "sha-256" or none: a book hashed with the fnv1a fallback has no SHA-256 to send) nothing is asked at all; else `GET /api/backup/pdfs`, then, one book at a time in the library's order, every such book whose sha12 is not listed, or is listed only with another size than the record's `byteLength` (repair B9-R2: a saved PDF changed after it was saved keeps its name, seldom its size; a record without `byteLength` goes by its sha12 alone; `savedCopy`): its bytes read from the store (`M.store.docs.loadBytes`, which joins its 8 MiB chunks), the book read again (deleted, given other bytes or no bytes meanwhile: skipped), then `POST /api/backup/pdf` with the bytes as the body and `Content-Type: application/pdf`, `X-Marginalia: 1`, `X-Marginalia-Sha256` (the book's sha256) and `X-Marginalia-Name` (`encodeURIComponent` of the book's name, a lone surrogate made U+FFFD first), cache no-store, credentials same-origin. A 200 (`skipped` or not) counts as saved; the files its `setAside` names leave the page's listing (so `state().pdfs` counts the PDF, not the changed file). A refusal (400, 409, 413, 500 …) is noted with the server's own words and the next book goes; no answer ends the run; an older serve.py (404, 405 or 501 on 127.0.0.1 or localhost) ends it quietly (`state().pdfs.outdated`). A run that could not save every PDF says so once: one toast (id "backup-pdfs", no action) "Could not save the PDF of “<name>” to the backup folder — <reason>" ("Could not save N PDFs …" for several; "Could not save the PDFs to the backup folder — <reason>" when the listing failed) and the same text in `state().pdfs.error`, which a run that saves everything clears. Nothing is tried again before the next run (the next backup written, or "Back up now"), so there is no retry loop. A tab that stepped aside sends nothing (checked before the listing, before each book and again once its bytes are read, right before the POST) and a run stops, saying nothing, when the tab steps aside (`store:mode` "othertab"); nothing in the claude.ai copy, off http(s) or in the store's memory mode. Uploads and restores (next bullet) share one queue, so at most one PDF is in memory; a run of a kind asked while one of that kind waits to start is that run. `state().pdfs` (in every `backup:state`) = `{saved: the files listed, bytes: their total size, dir: the backup folder, running, left: the books an upload run still has to send, error, outdated}`, or null before anything is known; `refresh()` (Settings › Data) lists the PDFs too, and each saved PDF updates it. B9.7: every book of the JSON backup carries its `sha256` and `hashAlgo` (`toJSON` leaves out only `chunkCount` and `chunkSize`), which the prune reads.
- **PDFs, restore** (js/backup.js `restorePdfs()`, js/library.js `attachPdf`, FEATURES B9.5): at start (once the server first answers a check after `start()`) and after every Import (`M.exporter.importJSON` emits `import:finished` `{report, error}` when it ends, success or failure, an addition to the SPEC §17.3 table, exporter → backup; M.backup listens while started and the status is on or off), every live book whose bytes are not in this browser (`hasBytes` false: an Import made it, or its bytes were lost) and that has a SHA-256 is looked up: with none (or without `M.library.attachPdf`, an older bundle) nothing is asked; else `GET /api/backup/pdfs`, then for each such book whose sha12 is listed, one at a time, `GET /api/backup/pdf/<sha256>` (404: a failure, named in the toast like any other (repair B9-R2; it was skipped without a word before): "its saved copy “<file>” was changed after it was saved, so it is not this book's file any more" when the 404's `changed` names that file, else "its saved copy is no longer in the backup folder"; above 300 MB by `Content-Length` or size: refused; a cut body: "the PDF did not arrive whole …"), then `M.library.attachPdf(collectionId, bytes)` (an M.library extension): it joins the bytes to the book as "Choose file again" joins the same file. Its checks: a live book with `hasBytes` false, a tab that writes, no Add PDF under way (Add PDF and Choose file again hold the library's busy flag, and so does attachPdf while it hashes and stores; "busy" is skipped quietly and tried again at the next start or Import, and Add PDF meanwhile says "Another PDF is still being added"), bytes that look like a PDF (`%PDF-` in the first 1 KB), at most 300 MB, whose SHA-256 (`M.util.sha256`) equals the book's `sha256` (else code "mismatch"). The PDF is not opened with PDF.js: `M.pdfview` holds one document, so opening it would close the book being read (and a password-protected PDF would ask for its password in the middle of a background task); the same SHA-256 means the very same bytes, so the same PDF.js fingerprint (the §11 duplicate check's other key) and the page count, labels and outline the book recorded when its file was added. So the record keeps everything (id, name, sections and marks, position, `lastOpenedAt`; no recontext, since nothing it derives from changed), is read again after the hash (deleted, given its bytes or another sha256 meanwhile: "changed", nothing written), and gets `byteLength`, `chunkCount`, `chunkSize` and `hasBytes: true` from `M.store.docs.saveDocument` (record and chunks in one transaction), then `M.cache.saveCollection`, so the row reads "Continue reading" and the book opens normally; a store failure that leaves a stub is mirrored as Add PDF does. The library stays usable meanwhile (no overlay), and a rename, Move to folder… (or a drag), Delete and its Undo, or an Import, made while the PDF is stored, changes M.cache's record at once and queues its own store write behind that transaction (IndexedDB runs overlapping transactions in the order they were made). So, once the PDF is stored, the record is read a third time (repair B9-R1 / LIB-1 of 2026-11-11): only those four storage fields join the record M.cache holds then, in place, and that record is saved (never the copy handed to the store), so the rename, the move or the import stays; a delete of the book seen while it was stored (bus `collection:deleted`, whether its Undo came too or not) writes nothing more (its transaction removes the chunks this one wrote, and the Undo brings the book back without them, so it keeps "Choose file again" and the next restore attaches its PDF), and so does any other change that makes it no longer a live book waiting for this PDF; then the chunks under that id are deleted too unless the record now says it has its bytes, and attachPdf rejects "changed" (skipped quietly). After a store failure the book as M.cache holds it then goes over the store's stub (a tombstone stays one). "Choose file again" and "Replace file" (js/library.js `storeBook`) follow the same rule: the record saved once the PDF is stored is `bookRecord(the new file, the record as it is then)`, so a rename, move or chapter-mark change made while "Storing …" shows stays, and a book deleted before or while its PDF is stored is not brought back (code "changed", toast "The book was deleted while its PDF was being stored, so the PDF was not added", nothing opens). Then one toast (id "backup-restore"): "Restored N PDF(s) from your backup folder" ("1 PDF", "2 PDFs"), with the ones that could not be restored in its detail ("“<name>” could not be restored — it is not this book's file (its SHA-256 differs). Choose file again in the library adds it by hand."), or, with none restored, "Could not restore the PDF of “<name>” from your backup folder — <reason>"; a server that cannot list the folder, or has no PDF backups, restores nothing and says nothing. "Choose file again" stays for every book the folder does not hold. Nothing in a tab that stepped aside, the claude.ai copy, off http(s) or in memory mode.
- **Settings › Data** (js/library.js; css/library.css `.settings__backup`, `.settings__backup-controls`): an "Automatic backup" block between the JSON backup hint and Storage, only when M.backup is loaded. Status line: on "Every day to <dir> · last: <date> (<size>)" ("· no backup yet" without one); off "Off"; outdated "Restart Marginalia (marginalia restart in Terminal) to turn on automatic backups"; unavailable "Needs the marginalia command — not available in the claude.ai copy or a saved file"; checking "Checking for Marginalia’s server…". A red second line: on or off with an error, "Today’s automatic backup failed — <error>"; unavailable for a reason other than artifact or file, the error itself. Under them (FEATURES B9.6) the PDFs line `.settings__backup-pdfs`, while the status is on or off and the server has listed its PDFs: "PDFs: <N> saved in <dir>/PDFs (<total size>)" (N: the files listed; the size: their sum, `M.util.fmtBytes`), with " · saving <k> more…" while an upload run has books left, or "PDFs: restart Marginalia (marginalia restart in Terminal) to back them up too" for a serve.py from before PDF backups; and a red line `.settings__backup-pdf-error` with `state().pdfs.error` (why the last upload run could not save them all). Both hide otherwise, and both use the `.settings__status` styles. A switch (checkbox, role switch) "Back up automatically every day" calls `M.backup.setEnabled(checked)`; it shows `state().enabled` and is disabled when unavailable. "Back up now" calls `runNow()` and toasts the result (id "backup": "Backed up · <dir>/<file> (<size>)" or "Backup failed — <error>"); it is enabled in on and off while no backup runs ("Backing up…"); the backup it makes also sends the missing PDFs, in the background (the PDFs line shows them going). Hint: "Each backup also saves the PDFs that are not in the folder yet. To restore: Import a file from that folder; Marginalia then takes each book’s PDF from its PDFs folder by itself (a book whose PDF is not there says Choose file again)." Opening the tab calls `M.backup.refresh()`, and `backup:state` repaints the block while the Data tab shows. Pure helpers: `M.library._pure.backupLine(state)`, `backupProblem(state)`, `pdfLine(state)`, `pdfProblem(state)`.
- **settings.autoBackup**: default true; `M.settings.set` accepts only true or false (anything else returns false), and a stored non-boolean loads as true. It is not among the exporter's `SETTINGS_KEYS`, so a JSON backup neither carries nor restores it (each browser keeps its own).
- **Limits and restore**: one file per server per day, so two browsers, profiles or addresses on the same server share the day's name (the first automatic backup of the day wins; "Back up now" replaces it). macOS sleep pauses the hourly timer, so after waking the next check comes within an hour, or sooner when the tab is shown again. A restore is an Import of a file from the folder (Settings › Data; it merges by prompt id); the books it brings back take their PDFs from the folder's `PDFs` by themselves ("PDFs, restore"), and a book whose PDF is not there keeps "Choose file again" (Add PDF with the same file works too). The PDFs folder belongs to the server, not to one library: one backup folder per server, so two browsers, profiles or addresses on one server share it, and a library whose backups never become the day's file is listed by no kept backup, so the PDFs only it uploaded are pruned at the next backup written (the same limit as the JSON backups); a PDF uploaded for a book added after the backup just written is safe until the next backup written, which lists it while the book exists. A saved PDF changed after it was saved (repair B9-R2) is set aside and saved again at the next backup written only while this browser still holds the book's PDF and the change altered the file's size (as an editor's save does); a change that keeps the size exactly is not seen by the page's size check (the listing hashes nothing, so that it stays fast however large the folder), and only a restore that needs that PDF says so (its 404 names the file). D23's 30 days for a deleted book's PDF go by the Mac's clock: a clock 30 or more days wrong shortens them ("PDFs, the server"). Opening a saved PDF to read it changes nothing; the README asks the user to annotate a copy instead. After updating Marginalia, `marginalia restart` once gives the running server the backup API (and, since FEATURES B9, the PDF routes); until then Settings › Data says so. A test server keeps its backups in a scratch folder: `--backup-dir` (or `MARGINALIA_BACKUP_DIR`), or a sandbox server's own `<sandbox>/Backups`, or the `Backups` folder beside the test obsidian.json that `--obsidian-config` or `MARGINALIA_OBSIDIAN_CONFIG` names ("Obsidian", Testing a send in a browser).
- **Tests**: `test/backup.test.js` (30 tests; each a vm page with the real util, bus, settings, scheduler, prompts, context, exporter and backup modules, a stand-in server through `_setFetch` (with tools/serve.py's PDF routes: the SHA-256 and `%PDF-` checks, one file per content), an injected clock and timers; nothing reaches a network or a folder; among them a full localStorage that refuses today's record while it holds nothing, an older day's record or a value that is not JSON: one attempt and one toast that day, one more the next day, and a success clears the stored value; for FEATURES B9: after the daily backup only the PDFs the folder lacks go (one already there under an older name does not), their bytes read through the real js/store.js (memory backend, a 9 MB PDF in two chunks), one at a time, with the headers; no PDF after a backup the server skipped, and Back up now sends them also while off; a tab that stepped aside sends none, also when it steps aside between two, and the claude.ai copy, file:// and memory mode never ask; a refusal noted once (one toast, `pdfs.error`) while the next book goes, no answer ends the run, nothing retried before the next backup written, whose success clears the note; an older serve.py: nothing sent or toasted, `pdfs.outdated`; the restore at start through `M.library.attachPdf` (only the live books the folder holds, one toast, no request with nothing to restore), a refused restore named in the toast, a busy library or a server error quiet, no request without `attachPdf`; the restore after the real `importJSON`, and none in a tab that stepped aside; the daily backup lists every book's sha256 (B9.7); repair B9-R2: a saved PDF changed after it was saved (another size than the book's) is sent again, the stand-in server sets it aside and the page's listing follows, while a record without `byteLength` goes by its sha12 alone; a restore whose saved copy was changed fails out loud, naming the file, and so does one gone between the listing and its GET), `test/settings.test.js` (autoBackup), `test/app.test.js` (the boot hook, on the real modules in the tools/smoke.js window), `test/smoke.test.js` (28 modules since FEATURES P2.4, 27 since the desktop app's part 2, the order equals index.html, "Backup after boot: unavailable (memory)"), library-dom (the Settings block in each status, the switch, Back up now and its toasts; for FEATURES B9 the PDFs line in each state and its red line, `attachPdf` joining a PDF with the real hashing (the record kept, nothing opened, the row then "Continue reading") and refusing another file, a non-PDF, a book with its bytes or gone, an fnv1a book, a tab that stepped aside, an Add PDF under way (both ways) and a record changed meanwhile, the real js/backup.js restoring a book end to end, and Choose file again with a rename, a move and a delete made while the PDF is stored), `test/pdf-restore-race.test.js` (repair B9-R1 / LIB-1, 8 tests: the real util, bus, settings, store (memory backend), scheduler, prompts, context, markup, cache, library and backup modules, the person's action run after the real saveDocument committed and before attachPdf goes on, the order IndexedDB gives it: a rename and a move kept in the cache and the store, a delete kept with no chunks left and its Undo bringing the prompts back, a delete and its Undo both meanwhile, a store failure with a move or a delete, the real `restorePdfs` while the book is deleted, and the untouched case) and `test/test_serve.py`'s `BackupApiTest` and `ServerOptionsTest` (the folder from the option, the variable, a sandbox or Documents, and a sandbox server started as CLAUDE.md starts it keeping its page's backups in the sandbox; B8, `test_a_server_with_a_vault_list_of_its_own_keeps_its_backups_beside_it`: `--obsidian-config`, the variable, the option over the variable, a sandbox over the variable, `--backup-dir` and `MARGINALIA_BACKUP_DIR` winning, no option, Obsidian's own list named (as a path, through `..`, in the variable, through a symlink, in other letters) keeping the user's folder, a relative path, refusals inside Obsidian's settings folder (also through a symlinked `Backups`) and inside an app folder (also through the CLI), blank values, and real serve.py runs under `--obsidian-config` and the variable whose page backup lands beside the list with nothing in the home's Documents; the start-line test, `test_the_server_names_its_backup_folder_and_makes_nothing_at_start`, expects `<list folder>/Backups` for `--obsidian-config` and the variable, the option's folder for `--backup-dir`, and the (fake) home's folder for Obsidian's own list and for no option; refused starts, a sandbox inside the app folder among them; a file with today's name in other letters left alone (409), also when it appears during a write; nothing made at start; auto once a day and manual replaces; the file is the backup's own text; the prune with decoys; refusals; a failed write leaves no temp file; a file that appears meanwhile, also without hard links; 12 MB; SIGTERM during a backup; and `PdfBackupApiTest` for FEATURES B9, 24 tests: a PDF saved once as a normal file (0600 in a 0700 folder; the same content under another name skipped; a hand-renamed copy counts), the name rule (a table, 2000 fuzzed names, linear time on long headers), bodies that are not the PDF they claim (SHA-256, `%PDF-` past 1 KB, empty, bad headers), the guards before anything is written (Origin, `X-Marginalia`, Host, Content-Type, 411, 413, no CORS, other machines on all three routes), the bytes sent only when their content is what was asked (Sec-Fetch-Site, a decoy with the same sha12, a symlink never followed), a PDF that changes while it is sent never arriving whole, two uploads of one PDF at once leaving one file, a failed save leaving no temp file, a name held by a folder or a link, a `PDFs` that is a link or a file, a server without a backup folder, the prune (kept by any kept backup that lists the PDF for a live book: the oldest kept, upper-case hex, a PDF that an old tombstone shares with a live book in either order, a book deleted since; an old tombstone lists nothing; deleted only when listed by none; decoys untouched; nothing deleted while a kept backup cannot be read or parsed; only after a backup written; the PDFs a test expects deleted hold what their names say), D23 (`test_a_deleted_books_pdf_stays_30_days_after_the_deletion`: a book added and deleted on one day with Back up now after each, kept 1 and 29 days later, also after another library's backup replaced the day's file, gone 30 days after; `test_a_pdf_deleted_over_30_days_ago_stays_while_a_kept_backup_lists_the_book_alive`; `test_a_tombstone_is_read_as_the_exporter_reads_deletedAt`: stampOf's rule on every kind of value, the cutoff's bounds, a clock that was ahead, the real clock in milliseconds, numbers too large for a float through POST; from review9: `test_older_kept_backups_keep_a_tombstones_pdf_the_whole_30_days`, `test_the_backup_just_written_keeps_a_tombstones_pdf_the_whole_30_days` (29 days kept, 30 days and 1 ms gone) and `test_a_pdf_uploaded_while_a_backup_ran_stays_after_its_books_deletion`), repair B9-R2 (`test_a_saved_pdf_changed_after_it_was_saved_is_set_aside_and_saved_again`: an edit in place and a safe save that keeps the size, listed by name but never sent (the 404's `changed`), set aside as "(changed)" and "(changed 2)" with the user's changes while the PDF goes in its place or a good copy renamed by hand counts, a rename that fails (500, nothing written), names near 255 bytes; a file that takes the name meanwhile, holding the PDF or not; `test_the_prune_never_deletes_a_saved_pdf_changed_after_it_was_saved`: set aside, not deleted, and never touched again; an unreadable one stays), and `ServerOptionsTest.test_marginalia_stop_during_a_pdf_upload_leaves_no_temp_file`). Every server a test makes or starts gets a temp backup folder and a throwaway Obsidian config (the one test of a server without backups gets no backup folder at all), and the tests that need the default folder use a fake HOME, so no test ever reads or writes the real `~/Documents/Marginalia Backups`.

## Library folders (binding; added 2026-11-10, FEATURES L1)
The user asked for "a folder option" and chose "Folders in the library": books and decks grouped into folders on the home screen, folders that open and close, "Move to folder…" in each ⋯ menu. Settled with the user: folders inside folders at any depth (like Finder); a folder's Review button reviews everything in it, sub-folders included, interleaved like "Review all"; Obsidian keeps its note paths (`<obsidianFolder>/<book>.md` whatever the folder); moving by the menu AND by dragging a row onto a folder.
- **Data**: a folder is `{id: "folder_<uuid>", name, parentId: <folder id>|null, createdAt, updatedAt, deletedAt: null|<ms>}`; a deleted folder stays as a tombstone (as collections do) and keeps its `parentId`. The name is the typed text with whitespace collapsed, trimmed, at most 80 characters (`M.cache.FOLDER_NAME_MAX`, never half a surrogate pair); a blank name is refused (code "bad_name"). Names need not be unique. A book or deck gets `folderId` (missing or null: the top level) and `folderUpdatedAt` (when its `folderId` last changed; the import merge compares it). Nothing else of a book, deck, prompt or review record knows about folders.
- **Storage (the choice and why)**: ONE record in the existing `meta` store, key `"folders"` (`M.cache.FOLDERS_KEY`), value = the array of every folder record, tombstones included. No IndexedDB version bump (`DB_VERSION` stays 1, `MIGRATIONS` keeps only `1`): a bump is a one-way door (an older copy of the app could no longer open the database: `VersionError`). A new object store needs that bump. Records of a new kind in `collections` would need every walker of collections to skip them (the home lists, Review all, counts, the exports, Obsidian's Send all, the JSON backup, js/backup.js's "library not empty" test) and an older copy would show them as decks. An older copy ignores the meta record; it keeps `folderId` / `folderUpdatedAt` when it writes a collection (records are written whole), its import carries them with the collection, and it ignores a backup's `folders`. Each folder write stores the whole array (a few KB for 50 folders). The value stays an array of folder records: a later shape needs a new key. Nothing is written at load, so an existing library opens unchanged, every book and deck at the top level.
- **Reading** (`M.cache`, never trusting a stored reference): a `folderId` or `parentId` naming a live folder is that folder; one naming a deleted folder means the nearest live folder up that tombstone's parent chain (where the folder's delete moved its contents); one naming no folder at all means the top level; a loop in the stored parents is cut at its folder with the newest `updatedAt` (then the larger id), which sits at the top level. Readers: `folders({live})`, `folder(id)` (tombstones too), `folderParent(id)` → id|null, `folderChildren(parentId|null)` → Folder[] (stored order), `folderPath(id)` → [ids from the top level down to the folder], `folderOf(collection|id)` → folderId|null, `folderMembers(id)` → `{folders, collections}` (live, any depth). The derived index is rebuilt after each write and load.
