- **Writing** (`M.cache`): `createFolder(name, parentId|null, {now})` → Folder; `renameFolder(id, name)` (the same name writes nothing); `moveFolder(id, parentId|null)` → `{folder, from, to, moved}` (rejects "cycle" when the target is the folder or any of its sub-folders, at any depth); `moveCollection(id, folderId|null)` → `{collection, from, to, moved}` (sets `folderId`, `folderUpdatedAt` and `updatedAt`, never `contentUpdatedAt`); `removeFolder(id)` → `{folder, to, folders, collections}`: the folder becomes a tombstone and what was directly in it (sub-folders, books, decks) moves up one level, to `to` (its parent, or null); nothing is deleted and no prompt, review history or other field changes; `restoreFolder(id, {folders, collections})` (the Undo): the tombstone comes back where it was, with each listed child that still carries the delete's stamp (nothing moved or renamed it since) and does not sit above the folder by then; `saveFolders(records)` (the import): upserts by id with their own stamps, a parent that would close a loop or names no live folder stored as the readers see it; a loop that the records close with folders already in the library is cut where the readers cut it, and the folder cut (its parent stored as null, its stamps kept, named in the event) is stored even when it is not one of the records (repair LIB-2 of 2026-11-11). So no loop is ever stored, through the UI, the API or an import. "not_found" for a folder or collection that is not live. Every write is ONE readwrite transaction over `meta` and `collections` and ONE `folders:changed` event (below), emitted before the store write as the other writers do; a store failure toasts "Could not save" and keeps the in-memory state, as `saveCollection` does. While `M.store.locked()` every folder writer rejects code "locked" and changes nothing (the one-writing-tab rule, FEATURES L1.8); the library never toasts that refusal. `countsBy(now)` → `Map<collectionId, counts>` gives `counts(id, now)` for every collection in one pass over the prompts.
- **Event** (an addition to the SPEC §17.3 table): `folders:changed` `{folderIds, collectionIds, reason: "create"|"rename"|"move"|"delete"|"restore"|"import"}`, cache → library; one per write, however many books it moves (no `collection:changed` for a move).
- **Home screen** (js/library.js, css/library.css): "New folder" after "New deck" in the header (an inline field at the end of FOLDERS, or alone above BOOKS while there is no folder; Enter or Create makes it, Esc or Cancel drops it, a blank name toasts "Give the folder a name first"; the new folder opens and takes focus). An inline New folder or New deck field holds nothing back (repair UI-1 of 2026-11-11): a re-render (a chevron, a move, a rename, a data change, the 60 s refresh, coming back to the home screen) puts the same field back in its place, with its text, and gives focus back to it when it had it; only an IME composition in it holds a re-render until it ends. "New folder" or "New folder inside" with the field already up moves that field (with its text) there; a blank New folder field gives way to New deck and the other way round, one with a name stays; a "New folder inside" field whose folder is no longer open on screen (closed, moved into a closed folder, deleted) goes, as Cancel would. Focus after a book's or deck's delete inside a folder stays in that folder (repair UI-4): the next row of that kind, else the previous one, else the folder's other rows (the decks below a deleted book, the books above a deleted deck), else the folder's chevron (or the nearest chevron above it on screen); only at the top level does it go to "Add PDF" / "New deck" when none of that kind is left. A FOLDERS section (label "Folders") above BOOKS and DECKS holds the top-level folders; without any folder there is no such section and the screen is as before. A folder row: a button with the chevron (▸ / ▾, `aria-expanded`), a folder icon and the name (it opens and closes the folder, and keeps focus); the counts of its whole subtree, "<n> folders · <n> books · <n> decks · <n> due" (folders only when there are some; due = cards due, as the book rows count them); "Review · N due" (never disabled; the quiet look and the title "Nothing is due" at 0, as a book row's Review; calls `M.app.startReview("folder", id)`); ⋯ with Rename (inline, as a book's), New folder inside (opens the folder, the field at the top of its contents), Move to folder…, Delete. Folders sort by name with `Intl.Collator(undefined, {numeric: true, sensitivity: "base"})` (case-insensitive, "Ch 2" before "Ch 10"), then by id; books and decks keep the order of today (last opened first). An open folder shows its sub-folders, then its books, then its decks (the usual rows), indented beside a guide line; an open folder with nothing in it says "Empty — drag books here or use Move to folder…". Open folders are remembered in this browser: localStorage `marginalia.openFolders` = JSON array of folder ids (only live folders are kept; a broken value opens nothing; nothing is written in a tab that stepped aside). BOOKS / DECKS stay the top level's; when every book (deck) is in a folder they say "No books (decks) outside folders — drag one here to take it out of its folder."
- **Delete**: `M.app.confirm` title "Delete folder “<name>”?", body "Its books, decks and folders move to “<parent name>”. Nothing else is deleted." (or "… move to the top level. …"), OK "Delete folder" (danger); then `removeFolder`, focus on the parent's chevron (or "New folder"), and the toast "Deleted the folder “<name>” · Undo (Z)" (8 s; its Undo and Z on #library run `restoreFolder`, "Restored the folder “<name>”").
- **Move to folder…**: in the ⋯ menus of every book row, deck row, the deck page and every folder row, after "Send all to Obsidian" (after Export… without it), before "Replace file" / "Delete"; disabled with nowhere else to go (title "No folders yet — make one with New folder", or "There is no other folder to move it into" for a folder). It opens a picker: a `.dialog` frame (`.lib-picker`) appended to `document.body`, so no re-render of the home screen takes it away; a `role="dialog"` panel titled "Move “<name>” to…", a `role="menu"` list of `menuitemradio` buttons: "No folder (top level)", then every live folder as an indented tree by name (padding-left 12 + 20·depth px); the current place has `aria-checked="true"` and a "current" tag and takes focus; the folder itself and its sub-folders are disabled ("A folder cannot go inside itself"). ↑ ↓ (wrapping), Home, End and Tab stay inside; Esc, ✕ or a click outside close it and focus goes back to ⋯; no key reaches M.app's listener while it is up. Choosing the current place just closes it; another place moves the item: toast "Moved “<name>” to “<folder>”" (or "to the top level") with Undo ("Moved “<name>” back to …"); focus lands on the moved row's ⋯ if it is on screen, else on the chevron of the folder it went into or of the nearest folder above that is on screen. The picker remembers the ⋯ that opened it by its `data-focus` key and its screen, so when a re-render replaced that button while the picker was up, Esc, ✕, a click outside or the current place give focus to the new ⋯ with that key (repair UI-3). Opened from the deck page (its ⋯ carries `data-focus` "deck:more"), choosing a place closes the picker back to that ⋯ and the move sets no landing, so the home screen later keeps focus and scroll where they were (repair UI-2). A move or its Undo that M.cache refuses with "not_found" says what is gone, never a record id (repair UI-5): "Could not move “<name>” — the folder “<folder>” was deleted", "Could not undo the move — the folder “<folder>” was deleted" (the place it came from), or "… — “<name>” was deleted"; other refusals keep their own words.
- **Drag and drop**: with folders in the cache, book, deck and folder rows are `draggable` and carry `data-kind` (book, deck, folder) and `data-id`; the drop targets carry `data-drop`: a folder's group (its row and its open contents) = that folder, the FOLDERS, BOOKS and DECKS sections = "" (the top level). HTML5 drag and drop, every listener bound once on `#library` in `init()` (dragstart, dragover, dragleave, drop, dragend, keydown for Esc, a capture mousedown that ends a drag whose dragend never came); `dataTransfer` gets `application/x-marginalia-item` = "collection:<id>" | "folder:<id>" (and the name as text/plain), effectAllowed "move". The same rules as the picker: a folder over itself or its own sub-folders, or an item over the place it already is, finds no target (dragover not prevented, dropEffect "none"); a valid target gets `.lib-drop--over`; the drop moves it with the toast and its Undo. Esc cancels (the browser's own Esc ends the drag with dragend; a keydown that reaches `#library` while a drag runs ends it too and stops there). The home screen does not re-render while a row is dragged (it marks itself dirty and renders when the drag ends). A file drop is unchanged (Add PDF). Keyboard users use the menu.
- **Review a folder** (js/review.js, js/app.js): `M.app.startReview("folder", folderId)` (rejects "no_collection" without an id) → `M.review.start({scope: "folder", collectionId: folderId, now})`: the live books and decks in the folder at any depth (`M.cache.folderMembers`, read once at start), their prompts queued by `M.scheduler.buildQueue` with scope "all" (exactly the "Review all" interleave); the context line and grading are unchanged; the summary carries `folderName`; the end screen and the empty screen show `.review__scope` "Folder “<name>”" (css/review.css). M.review rejects "bad_scope" for a folder scope without an id.
- **Backup and import** (js/exporter.js): the JSON backup (manual and daily: `prepare("json", {scope: "all"})`) has `folders` (every folder, tombstones included, by createdAt) after `settings`, and every collection carries its `folderId` / `folderUpdatedAt`; a one-collection export has `folders: []`. The format version stays 1 (an older Marginalia imports the file and ignores the folders); `MAX_IMPORT_RECORDS` counts folders too. `merge` merges folders first, by id: the later `updatedAt` wins for the name and the parent (every field but the stamps); a tombstone wins over the other side's record when its `deletedAt` is newer than that record's `updatedAt` (as for collections); then every live folder's parent must name a live folder: a parent missing after the merge → the top level, a deleted one → the nearest live folder above it; a loop is cut by moving its newest folder (`updatedAt`, then id) to the top level; the stamps stay (an import is not an edit). A collection's `folderId` follows the newer `folderUpdatedAt` (neither a scroll elsewhere, `updatedAt`, nor a rename, `contentUpdatedAt`, outranks a move); then every live collection's `folderId` must name a live folder (missing → top level, deleted → nearest live folder above it). A backup without `folders` changes no folder. `merge` returns `state.folders` and `report.folders` = `{added, updated, unchanged, skipped, removed}` with `report.changed.folders`; `importJSON` stores those with `M.cache.saveFolders` before the collections, and its toast adds " · N new folder(s)" and " · N folder(s) deleted, as in the backup". An import never deletes a book or deck for a folder: a folder the backup deleted moves its contents up. Anki TSV and Markdown are unchanged. Obsidian is unchanged (js/obsidian.js never reads the folders).
- **Fast with 300 books and 50 folders**: one `folders:changed` per write and the library's coalesced re-render (one per move, also for a delete that moves 100 books); one `countsBy` pass per render (no `counts(id)` per row; with a cache that lacks it, `counts(id)` once per row); a closed folder renders nothing inside; every per-row listener belongs to the row (dropped with it), the drag listeners are bound once, the picker listens on its own element and goes when it closes.
- **SPEC §17 extensions**: M.cache `countsBy`, `folders`, `folder`, `folderParent`, `folderChildren`, `folderPath`, `folderOf`, `folderMembers`, `createFolder`, `renameFolder`, `moveFolder`, `moveCollection`, `removeFolder`, `restoreFolder`, `saveFolders`, `FOLDERS_KEY`, `FOLDER_NAME_MAX`; M.library `createFolder`, `renameFolder`, `deleteFolder`, `moveTo(kind "collection"|"folder", id, folderId|null, {focus})`, `openPicker(kind, id, invoker)`, `closePicker()`, `toggleFolder(id)`, `showNewFolderField(parentId|null)`, `OPEN_KEY`, `_pure.folderCompare`, `folderTree`, `folderTotals`, `folderMeta` (a ⋯ menu item's `run` now gets the menu's ⋯ button); M.review and M.app.startReview scope "folder"; M.exporter `toJSON` option `folders`, `merge` state/report `folders`; the event `folders:changed`. CSS: css/library.css `.lib__section--folders`, `.lib-folder`, `--open`, `__body`, `__empty`, `__toggle`, `__chevron`, `__glyph`, `.lib-row--folder`, `.lib-row--dragging`, `.lib-drop--over`, `.lib__newfolder*`, `.lib-picker*`; css/review.css `.review__scope`.
- **Tests**: `test/folders.test.js` (the real modules on the memory store: storage and an existing library unchanged, create / rename / nest / move, cycles refused, delete and Undo, readers, one event for a 100-book delete, countsBy, review of nested folders in the Review all order, the backup round trip, every merge rule, the import, Obsidian paths, Anki and Markdown unchanged), `test/folders-dom.test.js` (the home screen on the fake DOM of library-dom: sections and rows, counts, open state remembered, sorting, New folder / inside / Rename / Delete with Undo and Z, the menus and the picker, drag and drop onto a folder and the top level, the refused cycle, Esc, the review end and empty screens, a locked tab, one re-render per move, no listener left behind, one counts pass for 300 books and 50 folders; the repairs of 2026-11-11: with a New folder or New deck field up the chevron, a drag, the picker, a folder's rename and a data change show at once and the field keeps its text and focus, an IME composition holds a re-render, "New folder inside" moves the open field, closing its folder drops it (UI-1); the deck page's picker gives focus back to its ⋯ and leaves no landing (UI-2); the picker finds its ⋯ again after a re-render, for Esc, ✕, a click outside and the current place (UI-3); a delete in a folder lands in that folder (UI-4); a refused Undo of a move names what was deleted (UI-5)), `test/folders.test.js` also stores a loop that one saved record closes with an existing folder cut (LIB-2), `test/tabs.test.js` (L1.8: every folder writer of the older tab refused, the store untouched, the newer tab's writes land) and `test/app.test.js` (startReview "folder").

## Desktop app (binding; added 2026-11-11)
FEATURES-E1, part 1: the user chose an Electron app (no Brave at all, the same engine family as today), keeping the Python server (the app starts `tools/serve.py` as a child process) and its own copy of the code (rebuilt in one step after each change; half-finished changes in the project folder never reach it). This changes D1 ("browser app, no install") as D1 foresaw; D22 records the user's four answers. The web app stays plain HTML/CSS/JS with no npm and no build step: npm is used only in `desktop/` (beside `marginalia/` and `bin/`). Part 1 built and tested the app without changing anything in `marginalia/` (the bullets below, up to "Limits (part 1)", updated where part 2 changed them). Part 2 (FEATURES-E2, 2026-11-11; "Part 2" below) connects it: serve.py's `--app` flag, its write guard and `GET /api/backup/latest`; the web app's app mode, Brave's cover and the app's welcome card for the move; the window that hides when closed; persistent storage; signing with a certificate; `desktop/vendor-lock.json`; and `marginalia app`, which installs it. The move itself (FEATURES E2.7) is done with the user.
- **Layout and versions**: `desktop/package.json` pins exactly electron 45.3.0 (the newest stable release on 2026-11-11; Chromium 156.0.8078.96, Node 24.22.2), @electron/packager 20.3.0 and @electron/fuses 2.1.3, all devDependencies; `package-lock.json`; `.npmrc` `ignore-scripts=true` (no install script runs; a general guard, since none of the pinned packages has one: electron 45 dropped its postinstall and instead downloads its binary into node_modules the first time plain Node runs `require("electron")`, which nothing in `desktop/` does: the build downloads the Electron it packages into `.cache/electron`, and no test loads electron). The packaged app has no npm dependency. `node_modules/`, `.cache/` (downloads), `build/` and `out/` stay inside `desktop/`. Install with `npm ci`. `npm audit`: 5 moderate, all build-time (glob 13.0.7, minimatch 10.2.6 under @electron/asar 4.3.1 via @electron/packager and @electron/universal); the offered fix downgrades @electron/packager to 18.4.4, so it is not applied.
- **Files**: `main.js` (main process), `preload.js`, `lib/` (small modules, pure where possible: `args` flags and the self-test guard, `policy` navigation, external-link and permission rules, `hash` marginalia:// hashes, `python` the python3 search, `windowstate`, `rotatelog`, `menu`, `contextmenu` the right-click menu, `server` the serve.py child, `selftest` the in-page checks and verdict, `lifecycle` what closing the window does (part 2); build-only: `vendor` the CDN rewrite, SRI and lock checks, `icon`, `signing` the signing identity (part 2)), `scripts/build.js` (`npm run app`), `scripts/vendor.js` (downloads and cache), `scripts/selftest.js` (the self-test runner), `entitlements.plist`, `vendor-lock.json` (part 2; part of the project), `test/*.test.js` (`node --test`, no Electron needed; `test/main.test.js` loads main.js with Electron mocked). `.gitignore` ignores `node_modules/`, `.cache/`, `build/`, `out/` and `/sign-identity` (part 2).
- **Process model**: one main process; one window (one sandboxed renderer) on `http://127.0.0.1:<port>/`; one child, the app's copy of serve.py: `python3 -I -B -c <GUARD> <app>/Contents/Resources/web/tools/serve.py --port <port> --bind 127.0.0.1 --dir <app>/Contents/Resources/web --app <version>` (part 2: the app's version, which makes it the app's server; "Part 2" below) plus `--obsidian-sandbox`, `--obsidian-config` and `--backup-dir` when given. `-I` isolated mode; `-B` writes no .pyc into the signed bundle. GUARD (lib/server.js) starts a daemon thread that reads the child's stdin, a pipe from the app, with `os.read(0, 65536)` (never through `sys.stdin`: a daemon thread blocked in `sys.stdin.buffer.read` holds that reader's lock, and a serve.py that ends by itself, a taken port or a refused option, then aborts at interpreter shutdown with SIGABRT and a crash report instead of exiting 1; `test/server.test.js` expects exit code 1 and no signal for a taken port), and sends the server SIGTERM at end of file, then runs serve.py unchanged through `runpy.run_path(…, run_name="__main__")`: when the app ends in any way (quit, crash, `kill -9`) the pipe closes and serve.py ends too (`test/server.test.js` kills an app with SIGKILL and sees its server go). The child runs in its own process group (a Ctrl+C in a Terminal reaches the app, which stops the server itself) and without `MARGINALIA_OBSIDIAN_CONFIG` and `MARGINALIA_BACKUP_DIR` (the app's flags are the only way to point it elsewhere). python3: the first of `/opt/homebrew/bin/python3`, `/usr/local/bin/python3`, `/usr/bin/python3` for which `<python> -I -B <serve.py> --help` exits 0 within 10 s; `/usr/bin/python3` only when `xcode-select -p` succeeds (without developer tools that stub opens Apple's installer instead); none: the dialog "Marginalia needs Python 3" (Homebrew, python.org, `xcode-select --install`), then quit. The window loads only after the child printed `Marginalia: http://127.0.0.1:<port>/` (the port is bound) and `GET /` answers 200 with the `marginalia-build` meta. Server output and the app's own lines (`[app <ISO time>] …`, never a note's text or an obsidian:// URI) go to `<userData>/logs/server.log`, rotated at 512 KB into `server.log.1` and `.2` (folder 0700, files 0600).
- **Ports**: default 8765 (the address "Open in Marginalia" links use); `--port=<n>` for tests. Before starting, `probePort` connects to 127.0.0.1:<port>: refused means free; an answer to `GET /` with the `marginalia-build` meta is "a Marginalia server" (dialog: probably the one the `marginalia` command started, stop it with `marginalia stop` in Terminal; Try again / Quit); anything else is "another program" (dialog naming the port and the `lsof` command; Try again / Quit). The app never stops a process it did not start. A child that ends before it answers: the port is probed again (someone took it: the dialogs above), else "Marginalia's server could not start" with its last output, Try again / Quit.
- **Lifecycle**: quitting (the app menu's Quit, Cmd+Q, logging out or restarting the Mac, SIGTERM, SIGINT, SIGHUP; since part 2 closing the window hides it instead of quitting, see "Part 2", Window close) stops the server in `will-quit`: SIGTERM, SIGKILL after 5 s (serve.py's own SIGTERM handler waits at most 2 s for a note or backup write), then the app ends. An uncaught exception in the main process logs it, shows "Marginalia hit an unexpected error" and stops the server the same way before exiting 1 (a hard crash is covered by GUARD). The server ending on its own while the app runs: "Marginalia's server stopped" (Restart / Quit; Reload in the View menu shows it again instead of loading a dead page); after Restart the page is reloaded only when it had not loaded, and only once the server answers again. A renderer crash: "Marginalia's window stopped working" (Reload / Quit).
- **Data**: userData is `~/Library/Application Support/Marginalia` (`--user-data-dir=<dir>` overrides it, set at the top of main.js before anything reads it): the Chromium profile with the library's IndexedDB and localStorage (settings, API key, drafts), `logs/`, `window-state.json`. The app always appends Chromium's `use-mock-keychain` switch: nothing goes into the keychain and a rebuilt app never asks for it. Backups and Obsidian follow serve.py's defaults (`~/Documents/Marginalia Backups`, Obsidian's own vault list) unless the flags say otherwise.
- **Single instance**: `app.requestSingleInstanceLock()` once the app is ready (the lock lives in userData). A second launch hands its arguments to the running app (`second-instance`: a marginalia:// link opens, the window comes to the front, also a hidden one, or a new one when none exists: part 2) and quits (exit 0; exit 2 with a refusal line in self-test mode). Launches from Finder, the Dock and links reach the running app through macOS anyway (`open-url`).
- **Window**: title always "Marginalia" (`page-title-updated` prevented); default 1280 × 860 centred, minimum 480 × 400; `window-state.json` = `{x, y, width, height, fullScreen}` (normal bounds), written atomically on close and 0.8 s after a move or resize, read through `lib/windowstate.js` `sanitize` (whole numbers, between the minimum and the largest screen; the title bar must lie on a screen's work area, at least 100 px across, else centred on the main screen; then fitted inside that screen). Shown at `ready-to-show`; `backgroundColor` #efece4 or #1b1a18 (styles.css `--bg`) from the system theme, updated when it changes.
- **Security** (main.js, checked by `test/build.test.js` and the self-test): `contextIsolation: true`, `sandbox: true` (and `app.enableSandbox()`), `nodeIntegration: false` (also in workers and subframes), `webSecurity: true`, `allowRunningInsecureContent: false`, `webviewTag: false`, `navigateOnDragDrop: false`; `will-attach-webview` prevented; no remote module (gone from Electron). `lib/policy.js` `classify(url)`: "app" = the origin `http://127.0.0.1:<port>` (no credentials in the URL); "web" = any other http(s) URL; "external" = `obsidian:` (link mode opens notes so) and `mailto:`; everything else is refused. The main window navigates (`will-navigate`, `will-redirect`) only to "app"; "web" and "external" go to `shell.openExternal`, the only call of it in the app (`openOutside`, which re-checks the class); subframes may navigate only within the app's origin. `openOutside` opens only within `USER_ACTIVATION_MS` (5 s, Chromium's transient user activation) of a real click or key press in the main window (its webContents' `input-event` of type mouseDown, rawKeyDown, keyDown, gestureTap or touchEnd, not Developer Tools' input: `policy.activatesUser`, `policy.userActivated`), and each such input allows one open (consumed), as Brave lets a page open a tab or hand an app link to macOS only after a click; page script alone opens nothing outside the app, and a refusal is logged. Requests: one `session.webRequest.onBeforeRequest` listener (a session keeps one) cancels PDF.js's cMap requests to cdnjs (`policy.blockedRequest`: `https://cdnjs.cloudflare.com/ajax/libs/pdf.js/<version>/cmaps/…`; cdnjs answers every `.bcmap` with 403 and no CORS header, so they can never load; cancelled, they fail at once instead of waiting on the network) and, in self-test mode, records every request; nothing else is filtered (the Claude features reach the Anthropic API). Right-click (`lib/contextmenu.js`, webContents `context-menu`, `Menu.popup` on the window): on a misspelled word up to five suggestions (`replaceMisspelling`) or a disabled "No Guesses Found", and "Add to Dictionary" (`session.addWordToSpellCheckerDictionary`); in a text field the roles cut, copy, paste, pasteAndMatchStyle and selectAll, enabled from `params.editFlags`; on other selected text the role copy; on selected text "Look Up “…”" (`showDefinitionForSelection`); on a canvas or an image with contents "Copy Image" (`copyImageAt(x, y)`); nothing else (no menu when nothing applies; never Back, Reload, Save, Print, Inspect or link items; no accelerators). `window.open` and `target=_blank` never open a window (`setWindowOpenHandler` always denies): an "app" URL loads in the main window, "web" and "external" go to `openOutside`. Permissions (`lib/policy.js` `ALLOWED_PERMISSIONS`): `clipboard-read`, `clipboard-sanitized-write` and, since part 2, `persistent-storage`, each for the app's own origin only (request and check handlers); every other request is denied, `openExternal` included (external links go only through `openOutside`), and so are HID, serial, USB (device handler, chooser events cancelled) and Bluetooth. Electron fuses (set before signing, read back by the build): RunAsNode, EnableNodeOptionsEnvironmentVariable, EnableNodeCliInspectArguments, GrantFileProtocolExtraPrivileges and EnableCookieEncryption off; EnableEmbeddedAsarIntegrityValidation and OnlyLoadAppFromAsar on (`Info.plist` `ElectronAsarIntegrity` names app.asar's hash and @electron/packager embeds its digest). The app never calls `app.setAsDefaultProtocolClient` (it would write the dev binary into LaunchServices).
- **Preload**: exposes only `window.marginaliaDesktop = {app: true, version, platform}` through `contextBridge` (deep-frozen, the property read-only and not configurable); the version comes from the main process as the renderer argument `--marginalia-desktop-version=<x.y.z>` (`webPreferences.additionalArguments`). No ipc channel (part 2 adds none: the page knows it is the app from this bridge alone). The self-test checks the page sees no `require`, `process`, `module` or `Buffer`.
- **Menus** (`lib/menu.js`, every shortcut written out, none left to a role's default): Marginalia (About Marginalia; Hide Cmd+H; Hide Others Alt+Cmd+H; Show All; Quit Cmd+Q), Edit (the native roles Undo Cmd+Z, Redo Shift+Cmd+Z, Cut, Copy, Paste Cmd+V, Paste and Match Style Alt+Shift+Cmd+V, Select All Cmd+A: macOS needs them for Cmd+C, Cmd+V and Cmd+Z), View (Reload Cmd+R and Force Reload Shift+Cmd+R, both through the app so a dead server shows its dialog instead; Toggle Developer Tools Alt+Cmd+I; Actual Size, Zoom In, Zoom Out with no shortcut, since Cmd+0, Cmd+= / Cmd++ and Cmd+- zoom the PDF in the reader; Toggle Full Screen Ctrl+Cmd+F), Window (Minimize Cmd+M, Zoom, then since D30 the tab commands New Tab Cmd+T, Close Tab Cmd+W, Reopen Closed Tab Shift+Cmd+T, and Show Next Tab and Show Previous Tab with no shortcut, then Close Window Shift+Cmd+W (Cmd+W before D30), Bring All to Front, then (part 2) a separator and Marginalia, no shortcut, which shows the main window again). The three tab shortcuts are the one exception to "no key of the web app": see "Tabs", "In the Mac app". No File menu (Cmd+O adds a PDF, Cmd+F finds, Cmd+Enter saves). `test/menu.test.js` loads js/keys.js and checks that no menu shortcut equals any binding of READER_MAP, EDITOR_MAP, the review map or any review preset; Cmd+V is no binding there: the web app reacts to the paste event that Paste sends (an image into the editor, or a free margin note).
- **Downloads and files**: `will-download` sets the save dialog's options (`setSaveDialogOptions`: title "Save", default path `~/Downloads/<file name>`; the user picks where), in self-test mode too, where `setSavePath` then saves into userData without a dialog and the self-test reads the options back (`getSaveDialogOptions`); file inputs (Add PDF, Choose file again, Import) and drag and drop of PDFs are Chromium's own.
- **marginalia:// links**: the packaged app declares the scheme in Info.plist (`CFBundleURLTypes`, packager's `protocols`). `marginalia://open?hash=<hash>` (from `open-url`, or a second launch's arguments) makes the main window load `http://127.0.0.1:<port>/<hash>` (a hash change when the page is open). `lib/hash.js` passes only js/app.js's routes, read as its `parseHash` reads a hash: a leading "#" and "/" dropped, split at "/", each part percent-decoded; `#library` (or empty), `#deck/<id>`, `#reader/<id>`, `#reader/<id>/<prompt id>` (a trailing "/" and parts after those ignored, as `parseHash` ignores them). Ids are taken as the web app makes them, whatever their characters (a PDF fingerprint of any length or a SHA-256, a book added "as a separate book" `<fingerprint>:<8 hex>` from js/library.js `separateId`, `deck_<uuid>`, `p_<uuid>`); only an empty id, "." or "..", one with "/" or a control character once decoded, or percent-encoding that does not decode is refused; the whole link is capped at 4096 characters. The hash is then written again with each id percent-encoded (`encodeURIComponent`), so nothing in an id leaves the hash. Anything else opens the library. `test/hash.test.js` takes `parseHash` from js/app.js itself and checks that every hash it passes leads where the original leads. `#review` is not a route of js/app.js (a review is an overlay), so it opens the library, as the web app itself does. A link that arrives before the window or while the server is down waits for it.
- **Offline vendoring** (`lib/vendor.js`, `scripts/vendor.js`): the app's copy of index.html gets local files for its five CDN references, in `vendor/<library>/<version>/<file>` and `vendor/fonts/`: Google Fonts CSS (fetched with a Chrome User-Agent, so it names woff2 files; it must hold only @font-face rules whose single source is a woff2 file on https://fonts.gstatic.com; rewritten to local copies of exactly those files), katex.min.css 0.16.47 (checked against its index.html SRI, then each @font-face keeps only its woff2 source, as tools/bundle.py --artifact does, and only those 20 woff2 files are kept), pdf.min.js 3.11.174, katex.min.js 0.16.47 and pdf.worker.min.js (byte for byte as their SRI hashes and `WORKER_SRI` pin them). Tags keep `integrity` (the same values for the unchanged files; sha384 of the local file for the two rewritten stylesheets) and `crossorigin="anonymous"`, so the browser checks them again at every load. `var WORKER_URL = "<cdnjs>";` becomes `var WORKER_URL = new URL("vendor/pdf.js/3.11.174/pdf.worker.min.js", document.baseURI).href;` (the bootstrap fetches it inside a blob: worker, where a relative URL would not resolve). Only https from cdnjs.cloudflare.com, fonts.googleapis.com and fonts.gstatic.com is ever downloaded, redirects refused; each file once into `desktop/.cache/cdn/`, reused offline (`MARGINALIA_OFFLINE=1` forbids downloads: a missing CDN file stops step 2, and a missing `electron-v<version>-darwin-arm64.zip` in `.cache/electron/<hash>/` stops the build before step 1, since @electron/packager would otherwise download it); a file that fails its SRI (also from the cache) stops the build and names the cache file to delete; woff2 files must start with `wOF2` (the KaTeX fonts have no SRI in index.html: they are exactly the files the checked katex.min.css names, and the packaged self-test loads every @font-face of the page, so a damaged font fails the build). The build checks that only lines naming a CDN file changed (5), that no CDN host remains, that every local file with an integrity attribute matches it, and that every url() in the vendored CSS exists. PDF.js stays 3.11.174 with `isEvalSupported: false` (js/pdfview.js is unchanged). The project's index.html, `dist/` and the artifact are untouched.
- **Build** (`npm run app` = `node scripts/build.js`, arm64 Mac only): (1) copy `index.html`, `styles.css`, `css/*.css`, `js/*.js`, `tools/serve.py` and `test/sample.pdf` (the library's "Open sample PDF" on 127.0.0.1 loads it; the only file from `test/`) from `../marginalia` into `build/web`, never tests, docs, `dist/` or caches, and check every local file index.html names is there; (2) vendor (above); (3) stage `main.js`, `preload.js`, the runtime `lib/` modules and a minimal package.json in `build/app`; (4) draw the icon (`lib/icon.js`: a dark tile, a paper page with lines, one highlighted, a red margin line, a yellow card in the margin; ten PNG sizes) and `iconutil -c icns`; (5) @electron/packager: darwin arm64, name and executable "Marginalia", bundle id `local.marginalia.desktop`, version from desktop/package.json, category education, asar, `extraResource` build/web (→ `Contents/Resources/web`), `protocols` marginalia, Electron from `.cache/electron` checked against the electron package's `checksums.json`, temp files in `build/tmp`; the fuses are flipped in packager's `afterCopyExtraResources` hook, before packager embeds the asar digest and signs; (6) check Info.plist (since part 2 also `CFBundleShortVersionString` = package.json's version), fuses, `codesign --verify --deep --strict` and (part 2) the signature's kind and hardened-runtime flag, the web copy; (7) the self-test runner. Before step 1 (part 2) the build stops on a package.json version that is not digits.digits.digits and on an unusable signing identity; step 2 checks every file against `vendor-lock.json` ("Part 2"). Output: `desktop/out/Marginalia-darwin-arm64/Marginalia.app`. It never installs into /Applications and never starts the app without `--self-test`.
- **Signing**: through packager's `osxSign` (@electron/osx-sign, inside out, then verified strictly): ad hoc (`-`, identity not looked up, no hardened runtime) by default, or a certificate; since part 2 the identity comes from `MARGINALIA_SIGN_IDENTITY` or `desktop/sign-identity`, and only a "Developer ID Application:" identity gets the hardened runtime and the keychain look-up ("Part 2", Signing). `--timestamp=none` either way (nothing is notarized). The app bundle gets `desktop/entitlements.plist` (only `com.apple.security.cs.allow-jit`, which V8 needs under the hardened runtime); the helper apps keep osx-sign's default helper entitlements; `continueOnError: false`. An ad hoc signature's designated requirement is the code hash alone (`codesign -d -r-`: `cdhash H"…"`), so every build whose content changed is a new app to macOS's privacy settings: the first time its server reaches into `~/Documents` (the daily backup, an Obsidian note; serve.py is the app's child, so macOS asks for the app), macOS is expected to ask "Marginalia would like to access files in your Documents folder": at the first start after the first install (since part 2 the app's page lists the backup folder at every boot, so this comes before the move from Brave) and again after each update (Apple's documented behaviour; not observed, since tests never touch `~/Documents`). Allowing it is needed; "Don't Allow" makes backups, the move and sends fail with a permission error (the welcome card: "The backup folder cannot be read — …"; Brave's cover: "Could not back up this browser's library — …") until System Settings › Privacy & Security › Files and Folders turns it on. README "Install it" and step 1 of the move say so (repairs F4 and REQ-E of 2026-11-11; before, README named the prompt only after updates). A build of unchanged content is byte-identical (the same cdhash). A certificate's designated requirement names the bundle id and the certificate instead, so macOS keeps the permission across rebuilds ("Part 2", Signing).
- **Self-test** (`--self-test`, `lib/selftest.js`): requires `--user-data-dir`, `--port`, `--obsidian-sandbox` and `--backup-dir`, all absolute, and refuses (one JSON line `{ok: false, refused: […]}`, exit 2) without them, with `--obsidian-config`, any other argument or a marginalia:// link, port 8765 or a port below 1024, or a folder that is, holds or lies inside `~/Documents` (the real vault and backup folder), `~/Library/Application Support/Marginalia` or `~/Library/Application Support/obsidian`, under any of its names: by name (symlinks resolved, lower case, and each path also under its `/System/Volumes/Data` name and without it, since /Users, /private, /Applications and others are firmlinks that realpath does not merge) and by identity (`lib/args.js` `sameOrInside`: the existing part of each path compared by device and inode, the part that does not exist yet by name in lower case and Unicode NFC, so other letter case, Unicode forms and any other alias are caught), or a path through a symlink that leads nowhere. The home folder is the account's own from the user database (main.js passes `os.userInfo().homedir`), never `$HOME`, and a `HOME` that is not that folder (identity) is refused too. The one marginalia:// link it takes is `marginalia://open?hash=%23library` (`args.SELF_TEST_LINK`, for the runner's second launch). Chromium itself creates the `--user-data-dir` folder before main.js can refuse, so callers never pass a real folder. Then: no Dock icon (`app.dock.hide()` and the accessory activation policy at the top of main.js and again at ready), a hidden window (`show: false`, never shown, no background throttling), `shell.openExternal` replaced by a recorder, every session request recorded, no dialog (a problem is a failed check), a 150 s limit. In the page (`executeJavaScript` with a user gesture; in two phases since part 2) and in the main process it checks: boot, store-idb, backup-on (status "on" and the `--backup-dir` folder as serve.py shows it), backup-write (since part 2: `M.backup.runNow()` after the welcome card's import, with the app's header, writes `marginalia-backup-<today>.json` there, not skipped, and the file is then a backup of the imported library, made after the prepared one), obsidian-server (`M.obsidian.detect({force: true})`: server mode, exactly "Sandbox Vault"), desktop-bridge, no-node, pdfjs (3.11.174, a blob: workerSrc), katex (0.16.47 renders), fonts-local (every FontFace in `document.fonts` loads: at least the 20 KaTeX fonts and one Outfit face, none failing), sample-pdf (since part 2 the book the import restored, the web copy's test/sample.pdf, opens in the reader through `M.app.route("reader", …)` with all its pages rendered (part 1 added it with `M.library.addPdfFromUrl`); `window.pdfjsWorker` absent: PDF.js's real worker ran), cmaps-blocked (the page's fetch of `…/pdf.js/<version>/cmaps/UniJIS-UCS2-H.bcmap` on cdnjs fails within 3 s and the main process recorded that request as cancelled), download (saved into userData without a dialog; `getSaveDialogOptions().defaultPath` is `~/Downloads/<name>`), external-links (with no input before them `window.open`, a `target=_blank` anchor and a navigation to `https://example.invalid/marginalia-self-test` are refused; then the main process sends one key press (Shift, `sendInputEvent`) and the page calls `window.open` twice: the first reaches the recorder as opened, the second is refused; `window.open` returns null, the page stays, a `file:` navigation is refused and not recorded), requests-local (every request went to `http://127.0.0.1:<port>/`, or was data:/blob:, or one the app cancelled), hidden (both windows since part 2), mock-keychain, permissions (the page's `Notification.requestPermission()` reached the request handler and was denied, it answers "denied"; since part 2 persistent storage is never denied and `navigator.storage.persist()` answers true; `navigator.permissions.query` gives clipboard-read, clipboard-write and persistent-storage "granted" and notifications, geolocation and camera "denied", and nothing but the clipboard and persistent storage was granted; with the persistent-storage switch off, the opposite for persistent storage: "Part 2", Self-test), menu (the built menu's shortcuts equal lib/menu.js's), context-menu (right-clicks sent by the main process on a probe canvas and a probe text field the page script puts on top: the recorded templates hold "Copy Image", and Cut, Copy, Paste, Paste and Match Style and Select All; never shown) and, when a second launch handed a link over, link-handoff (openHash queues it in self-test mode; after the page checks it goes through the same `goToHash` as a real link, and the open reader becomes the library with `location.hash` "#library", the page's marker and `performance.timeOrigin` unchanged: the same document, no reload), part 2's app-api, app-mode, persisted, app-only, welcome-card, welcome-import, pdf-restore, cover-mode and close-hides ("Part 2", Self-test), plus page-errors when a step threw. It never opens an obsidian:// link, not even a recorded one, and never follows the cover's marginalia:// link. It prints one JSON line (checks, versions, python, port; info: persistedStorage, console errors, request count, permission requests, links handed over and, since part 2, coverRequests, closeHides, coverConsoleErrors), stops its server and exits 0 or 1.
- **Runner** (`scripts/selftest.js [app] [--keep] [--json <file>]`, step 7 of the build): refuses an app in /Applications or ~/Applications under any of its names (`args.insideAny`: by name with symlinks resolved, any letter case and the `/System/Volumes/Data` names, and by identity) and a `HOME` that is not the account's home folder (`os.userInfo().homedir`, which it also uses for every real path it checks); makes `user-data/`, `obsidian-sandbox/` and `backups/` in a new folder in `$TMPDIR` and picks a free port (never 8765, 8791 or 8792); runs the self-test; meanwhile starts a second app on the same `--user-data-dir` with `marginalia://open?hash=%23library` as soon as the first holds Chromium's SingletonLock (it must exit 2 while the first runs, the first must log "a second launch handed over 1 link(s)", and the app's link-handoff check must be there and pass); afterwards runs `lsregister -u` on the app and each helper app in `Contents/Frameworks` (under each of its names; macOS registers a helper as a record of its own when it writes a crash report for it), then on any record the dump still shows under the app, and checks that no line of `lsregister -dump` names the copy any more (`listedUnder`: the records and the count of lines, in the check's detail); checks that nothing listens on the port, that no "Marginalia Safe Storage" keychain item exists (`security find-generic-password -s …`, exit code only, nothing printed) and that `~/Library/Application Support/Marginalia` was not created; then real-preferences-clean: Chromium writes AppleTextDirection, NSForceRightToLeftWritingDirection and NSTreatUnknownArgumentsAsOpen to `~/Library/Preferences/local.marginalia.desktop.plist` through cfprefsd at every start, and when that domain holds nothing but those three keys after the run (whoever wrote them: runs that overlap each see the other's file, so the decision is made at the end, not at the start) the runner deletes it (`defaults delete`, then the empty file); a domain with any other setting (the installed app's own) is left alone, and the check fails only when a start-up-keys-only file is left. Every check is printed with its detail (`detailText`, never throwing), and the temp folder is deleted (unless `--keep`) and the copy unregistered in a `finally`, also when something failed on the way.
- **Safety rules** (tests and agents): the desktop tests and the self-test never touch `~/Library/Application Support/Marginalia`, the real vault, Obsidian's settings folder, the real backup folder or port 8765; every Electron or app launch uses `--self-test` with `--user-data-dir`, `--obsidian-sandbox` and `--backup-dir` in a temp folder and a free port; nothing installs into /Applications or ~/Applications, no installed Marginalia.app is ever started, a launched build copy is unregistered from LaunchServices, and no obsidian:// URI is ever opened. Since part 2 also: no marginalia:// link is ever opened through macOS (`open`, LaunchServices; the Node page tests stub `M.desktop._setOpener` before boot, as `M.obsidian._setOpener`); `marginalia app` and the command's open, start, stop and restart never run from the project's own `bin/marginalia`: whatever `MARGINALIA_APP` says, it works on port 8765, the user's server (its `is_ours` check names the project's own `marginalia/` folder): stop and restart stop that server, and open and start either open a browser page on it or, with a Marginalia app at `MARGINALIA_APP`, stop it and run `open -a` (repair CMD-2 of 2026-11-11: this rule named only `MARGINALIA_APP` before). They run only from a copy of `bin/marginalia` in a temp root with its own `marginalia/` and `desktop/` (its `is_ours` check then never matches the user's server), with `MARGINALIA_APP` in that temp folder, `MARGINALIA_PORT` and `MARGINALIA_APP_PORT` free ports (never 8765, 8791 or 8792), `HOME`, `TMPDIR` and `MARGINALIA_BACKUP_DIR` in the temp folder, and stand-ins for open, osascript, lsregister, npm and pgrep first on PATH, as test/test_command.py does; the cover's tests run serve.py with `--app` on a free port with a throwaway vault and backup folder, and a browser page on such a server is loaded only on `/` or `#library`: on `#reader/…` the cover follows `coverLink()` during boot, before a browser tool could set any `M.desktop._setOpener` stub, so the reader forward is tested only in test/desktop-dom.test.js, which sets the stub before boot (repairs SEC-4 and REQ-F of 2026-11-11).
- **Tests**: `node --test` in `desktop/` (102 tests in part 1, 136 in part 2, 145 since the repairs of 2026-11-11, 162 with the tabs of D30, no Electron; the server tests need a python3 that runs serve.py and use a throwaway vault, backup folder and port: start and SIGTERM stop, the guard ending a server whose app was SIGKILLed, a taken port ending with exit code 1; the guard tests also run on this disk in a temp folder: a fake home reached through `/System/Volumes/Data`, other letter case, a decomposed Unicode name, symlinks, a changed HOME) plus the packaged self-test. The web app's own checks in `marginalia/` still pass (part 2 adds its own tests there: "Part 2", Tests).
- **Limits** (part 1): js/pdfview.js's `CMAP_URL` points at cdnjs (only index.html changes in the app's copy), and cdnjs refuses every `.bcmap` file (403 "invalid file type", no CORS header), so a PDF whose CJK text needs one of PDF.js's predefined cMaps (a non-embedded CID font) shows none of that text, online or offline, in the app and in a browser alike (the browser page also waits on cdnjs); the app cancels those requests (Security), so it renders the rest at once and contacts no other host; making the text work needs the cMaps shipped (for example from the `pdfjs-dist` 3.11.174 package) and the app's `CMAP_URL` pointed at them (a decision for the user); part 1's other limit, `navigator.storage.persisted()` false in the app, is gone (part 2 grants `persistent-storage` to the app's own origin); macOS lists the process as a regular app for a few dozen milliseconds before main.js hides the Dock icon, so a self-test may flash one; not verified on this Mac: signing with a certificate, the visible window (dialogs, menus by hand, window placement, theme colour, Save dialog, drag and drop) and marginalia:// links delivered by macOS.

### Part 2: the app connected (binding; added 2026-11-11, FEATURES-E2)
The user's four answers are D22. The user confirmed three picks on 2026-11-11 (D23): closing the window hides it (`lib/lifecycle.js` `CLOSE_HIDES`); signing stays ad hoc by default, so macOS may ask for the Documents folder again after each update, and a self-made certificate still works (`lib/signing.js`); and a deleted book's saved PDF stays 30 days after the deletion, and longer while an older kept backup still lists the book alive; then the next backup written removes it (B9 above). A choice Claude made, one switch so it can change: the app grants persistent storage to its own page (`lib/policy.js` `ALLOWED_PERMISSIONS`; the self-test follows it, so turning it off changes that line and its unit tests only: repair REQ-D of 2026-11-11). Not in this part: PDF.js's CJK cMaps ("Limits" above) and the move itself (FEATURES E2.7, done with the user).
- **The app's server** (tools/serve.py, FEATURES E2.1a): `--app VERSION`, which only the desktop app passes: main.js gives `app.getVersion()`, desktop/package.json's `version` (the build stages it), and `lib/server.js` `serverArgs({…, appVersion})` puts `--app <version>` right after `--dir <web>`, before the sandbox, config and backup flags (no `appVersion`, no `--app`: the unit tests' plain servers). VERSION is digits.digits.digits in ASCII (`APP_VERSION_RE`, fullmatch; `1.0`, `v1.0.0`, `1.0.0.0`, Unicode digits and blanks are refused): serve.py stops at start with exit 2 before it makes anything ("argument --app: '<value>' is not an app version (digits.digits.digits, such as 1.0.0)"), `make_server(…, app_version=…)` raises ValueError (`check_app_version`), `lib/server.js` `appVersionOk` (at most 64 characters) makes `serverArgs` throw, and `scripts/build.js` `checkVersion` stops the build before step 1. With `--app` serve.py prints a fourth start line, "App: the server of the Marginalia app <version>; only the app's page writes (X-Marginalia-Client)" (the first three are unchanged; lib/server.js still waits only for the first).
- **`GET /api/app`** (`APP_PATH`, E2.1b): with `--app` 200 `{"ok": true, "app": true, "version": "<version>"}`, `Cache-Control: no-store`, nosniff; without it 404 `{"ok": false, "error": "not found"}`, byte for byte what an unknown route (and a serve.py from before the app) answers. The guards of every GET route (loopback client, Host); a query string is ignored, `/api/app/` is 404, HEAD is 404.
- **The write guard** (`Handler.client_may_write`, E2.1c, only with `--app`): every POST route (`/api/obsidian/note`, `/api/obsidian/notes`, `/api/backup`, `/api/backup/pdf`) needs `X-Marginalia-Client: app` (`CLIENT_HEADER`, `APP_CLIENT`); `/api/backup` and `/api/backup/pdf` also take `browser-move` (`MOVE_CLIENT`: Brave's one backup for the move). The value is trimmed and compared case-sensitively (`App`, `apps`, `app,browser-move` are refused). Anything else, a missing or empty header included: 409 `{"ok": false, "error": "app_only", "message": "Marginalia is now an app. Open the Marginalia app to do this."}` (`APP_ONLY`, `APP_ONLY_MESSAGE`; no-store, nosniff, the connection closed as for every error). Order: the route (404, also a backup route on a server without a backup folder), Origin (403), `X-Marginalia` (403), Content-Type (415), Content-Length (411/400), the size limit (413), then this check, then the body; the PDF route's `X-Marginalia-Sha256` check (400) comes after it. So a refused POST writes nothing and makes no folder (the backup folder, `PDFs/`, a vault folder). Without `--app` the header is ignored; GET routes take none. Why: a Brave tab still running older code must never write a backup or send a card next to the app's library; the never-twice rule of "Obsidian" holds only while one library sends. The guard is the server's, so it holds only while the app's server answers: after Cmd+Q a Brave tab that never showed the cover falls back to `obsidian://` links ("Limits (part 2)").
- **`GET /api/backup/latest`** (`LATEST_PATH`, E2.1d, with or without `--app`): the newest kept backup, `backup_files()[0]` (the first file `GET /api/backup` lists, which js/backup.js calls `last`), byte for byte, as `application/json; charset=utf-8` with `Content-Length`, nosniff, no-store and `X-Marginalia-Backup-Name: <file name>`; 404 `{"ok": false, "error": "no_backup"}` (`NO_BACKUP`) when there is none (an empty or missing folder, or a server without a backup folder). Only a file that listing names is sent. It is opened with `open_regular` (O_NOFOLLOW | O_NONBLOCK, then fstat must say a regular file; `open_pdf` calls it too): a symlink, FIFO or anything else put there after the listing, or a file gone meanwhile, gives 500 "<name> in the backup folder <dir> cannot be opened as a file" and sends nothing. Streamed in `PDF_CHUNK` (1 MiB) pieces, never read whole; a file that gets shorter while it is sent is cut off, the connection closed, and the log says "Backups: <name> got shorter while it was being sent, so it was cut off". Guards: loopback client, Host, and the Sec-Fetch-Site rule of `GET /api/backup/pdf/<sha256>` (403 "forbidden site", checked before the folder is read, so another site cannot tell a backup from none).
- **M.desktop** (`js/desktop.js`, a module beside SPEC §17.2, E2.2; `css/desktop.css`; host `#cover`, see "index.html skeleton"): loaded after js/library.js and right before js/app.js (29 namespaces since D27, 30 since D30, whose js/tabs.js loads between js/library.js and js/desktop.js, and 31 since D33, whose js/undo.js loads right after js/cache.js; in the order "Automatic backup" lists; tools/smoke.js `ORDER` and `HOST_IDS`, xref and the bundles follow index.html; tools/bundle.py is unchanged). It owns the page's mode, the client header and the cover (as `#tabLock` is M.app's); the welcome card's DOM is M.library's (it is part of `#library`), its data and its import are M.desktop's.
- **Modes** (`start()`, once per page; `mode()`): "app" when `window.marginaliaDesktop && window.marginaliaDesktop.app === true` (`isApp()`, the app's preload): no request, persistent storage asked once, the welcome card. "checking", then "cover" or "browser", in a normal browser page on `http:` or `https:` with host `127.0.0.1` or `localhost`, not the app, not the artifact (`M.app.isArtifact()`), with fetch: ONE `GET /api/app` (`X-Marginalia: 1`, `cache: no-store`, `credentials: same-origin`, an AbortController); `{ok: true, app: true}` (exactly true, 2xx) → "cover" (the version kept in `state().version`); 404, any other status, a network error, a body that is not JSON or says otherwise, or no answer within `CHECK_MS` = 3000 ms (the request is aborted) → "browser"; a late answer changes nothing. "browser" everywhere else (file://, the artifact, another host, no fetch): no request, nothing changes. "unknown" before start().
- **Boot** (js/app.js): `M.desktop.start()` right after bindBus/bindDom and BEFORE `M.store.open()` (the check runs while the library loads); after `M.cache.load()` boot waits for it. Cover mode: `M.desktop.showCover()` and nothing else (no attachments sweep, no route, no `?pdf=`, no `M.library.render`, no `M.backup.start()`; boot resolves true; a boot error in cover mode still shows the cover); otherwise boot goes on as before. `M.app.route()` answers false while covered, so a hashchange routes nothing. Until the check answers, `M.obsidian` send, sendAll and planAll wait for it (`M.desktop.ready()`) and `M.backup` writes nothing ("Marginalia is still starting — try again in a moment", which never counts as the day's failed automatic attempt). So nothing routes, writes or sends before the check has answered.
- **The client header** (E2.2a): every POST to the page's own server goes through `M.desktop.headers(base, client)`: in the app it adds `X-Marginalia-Client: app`; elsewhere the headers stay exactly as before (no key added), except the cover's backup (`client` "browser-move"). The call sites: js/backup.js `post` (POST /api/backup, automatic and Back up now) and `uploadOne` (POST /api/backup/pdf); js/obsidian.js `viaServer` (POST /api/obsidian/note) and `postInit` (POST /api/obsidian/notes and the per-card /note fallback). A 409 `{error: "app_only", message}` shows the server's `message` in every failure text of M.backup and M.obsidian (for example "Automatic backup failed — Marginalia is now an app. Open the Marginalia app to do this."); for Obsidian it is a definite refusal (a 4xx JSON answer), so the write-ahead marker goes back.
- **Cover mode** (E2.2c; `showCover()`, once): `#cover` (class `cover`, `is-hidden` removed) holds `.cover__panel` (role alertdialog, aria-modal, labelled by `#coverTitle`, described by `#coverBody`, tabindex -1): h1 `#coverTitle` "Marginalia is now an app"; `p.cover__forward` "Opening this page in the Marginalia app…" (hidden unless forwarding); `#coverBody`: "Your library in this browser stays here, unchanged. To move it into the app:" and an `ol.cover__steps` with "1. Click Back up this browser's library." and "2. In the app, click Import the newest backup." (the numbers are text); `.cover__actions`: `a.btn.btn--primary.cover__open` "Open the app" (href `coverLink()` = `"marginalia://open?hash=" + encodeURIComponent(location.hash || "#library")`, written again on every hashchange; it takes focus first) and `button.btn.cover__backup` "Back up this browser's library"; then `p.cover__status` (role status, aria-live polite), `p.cover__note` (at load, and again after a Cancel or an error, "This browser's library was backed up for the move <today|yesterday|on 10 Nov> at HH:MM. Once the app has imported it, the app keeps your library, so there is no need to back it up again." once this browser backed it up for the move, repair F1 below; else empty) and `p.cover__error` (role alert). All text through textContent. Lockout: every other child of `#app` gets `inert`, except `#confirm` and `#toasts`; capture-phase guards on window for keydown (Tab and Shift+Tab cycle inside the panel; Escape, and every plain key aimed outside the cover, is prevented and focus comes back; keys inside keep their native action, Enter on the link, Space or Enter on the button; Cmd/Ctrl shortcuts are not prevented; every key's propagation is stopped, so M.app's keydown listener routes none: no Mod+O, "," or "?"), paste, drop and dragover (prevented and stopped), and a focusin guard on document. The guards stand aside while M.app's confirm dialog is open and while `#tabLock` is up (`M.app.tabLock()` not null). Stacking (css/desktop.css): fixed, inset 0, z-index 1450 (above the ? sheet at 1400, below `#confirm` 1500, `#toasts` 1600 and `#tabLock` 2000), opaque `var(--bg)`, tokens only, one-column buttons below 600 px; the library is not rendered under it.
- **Nothing writes under the cover but its own backup**: `M.backup.start()` does nothing (the status stays "checking"); every other M.backup write (runNow without the client, savePdfs, restorePdfs, the import:finished restore) is refused "Marginalia is now an app — open it to back up" (`M.desktop.refusesWrite(client)`: true in cover mode and while checking, unless `client` is "browser-move"). M.obsidian refuses send, sendAll and a waiting link's click (the "Ready for Obsidian … Add to Obsidian" toast) with the toast "Marginalia is now an app — open it to send to Obsidian." (`M.obsidian.COVERED`, toast id "obsidian"; no request, no link, no marker, no copy offered); planAll answers `{ok: false, error: COVERED, covered: true}` and the library's Send all item toasts COVERED.
- **"Back up this browser's library"** (`backUp()`, one run at a time, the button disabled meanwhile): (1) a store in memory mode (`M.store.mode()` "memory": this browser's IndexedDB could not be opened, so the store holds none of the library kept there, and the `#banner` that says so is under the cover; repair F5 of 2026-11-11): `.cover__error` "Could not back up this browser's library — this page could not open the browser's storage, so it cannot read the library. Reload the page; if that does not help, quit the browser and open it again." (`TEXT.storage`; error "storage") and no request; else an empty library (no live book, deck or prompt): `.cover__error` "This browser's library is empty: there is nothing to move into the app." and no request; (2) status "Checking the backup folder…", `M.backup.refresh()` (GET /api/backup and GET /api/backup/pdfs; a server that cannot back up is an error line); (3) when the listing holds `marginalia-backup-<today>.json` (any listed file: `M.backup.hasToday()`; repair SEC-1 of 2026-11-11: the listing's newest file alone missed it whenever a file named after today, by a Mac clock that was once ahead or a time zone further east, sorted first, and the backup then replaced today's file without asking), `M.app.confirm({title: "Replace today's backup?", body: "Today's backup (made at HH:MM) is replaced with this browser's library. The app's own library does not change.", ok: "Replace it"})`, HH:MM being the local 24-hour time of that backup's `exportedAt`, read with `GET /api/backup/latest` (unknown, also when that sends a file named after today: the body without "(made at …)"); Cancel writes nothing and clears the lines; (3a) repair F1 of 2026-11-11: once this browser's library was backed up for the move (below), the question is instead, with or without a backup of today, `M.app.confirm({title: "Back up this browser's library again?", ok: "Back up again", danger: true})` with the body "This browser's library was already backed up for the move <when> at HH:MM. Once the app has imported it, the app keeps your library and backs it up itself.", then either "Backing up again replaces today's backup (made at HH:MM), which may be the app's own, with this browser's library, and saved PDFs that only that backup lists may be removed from the backup folder." (a backup of today) or "Backing up again makes this browser's library today's backup, and the app then makes no backup of its own today." (none), then "The app's own library does not change. Do this only if the move did not work." (one question, never two); Cancel writes nothing; (4) status "Saving this browser's library…", `M.backup.runNow({client: "browser-move", pdfs: false})` (the manual JSON backup, mode "manual"); a failure shows "Could not back up this browser's library — <the server's words>" and ends the run; (5) status "Saving PDFs…", then "Saving PDFs… k of n": `M.backup.savePdfs({client: "browser-move", quiet: true, onProgress})` (every PDF the folder lacks, no toast); (6) the result line "Saved this browser's library: <B> book(s), <D> deck(s), <P> prompt(s), <N> PDF(s) (HH:MM). Now open the app and click Import the newest backup." (singular for 1; B, D and P the live books, decks and prompts; N this library's books with a SHA-256 whose PDF the folder holds after the run, `M.backup.libraryPdfs()`; HH:MM when the JSON backup was written, by the page's clock), `.cover__note` "<K> book(s) has/have no PDF in the backup folder: in the app, Choose file again adds it/them." when some book has none there, and `.cover__error` with the PDF run's error. Repair SEC-1: when the listing after the backup still names a file after today (`GET /api/backup/latest`, which the app's Import the newest backup reads, sends that one), the line's second sentence is "Now open the app, click Import a backup file… and choose <the file written> in <dir>." and `.cover__note` starts with "The backup folder also holds a backup dated after today (12 Nov), so the app's Import the newest backup would import that one instead. Check the Mac's date and time.". Repair F1: once the JSON backup is written, the browser remembers the move: localStorage `marginalia.movedToApp` (`M.desktop.MOVED_KEY`) = `{at, file}` (when, by the page's clock, and the file written; this page keeps its own copy when storage refuses), which the note and the question (3a) read; nothing clears it. Nothing is retried by itself; focus returns to the button.
- **A reader route** (an "Open in Marginalia" link of an Obsidian note opened in Brave): a page loaded on `#reader/<id>…` (parseHash: the reader with a collection id) shows `.cover__forward` and follows `coverLink()` ONCE through the opener (a temporary `<a href>` click; test hook `M.desktop._setOpener(fn)`), never again in that page (`state().forwarded`; a hashchange only rewrites the link's href). "Open the app" is the fallback.
- **The welcome card** (E2.2d; DOM in js/library.js `welcomeCard()`, css/library.css `.lib-welcome`; data from `M.desktop.welcome()`): only in the app, while the library holds no live book, deck or prompt (folders and tombstones do not count) and the card was not dismissed, at the top of the home screen (after the header and the "written today" banner): `section.lib-welcome` (aria-labelledby `libWelcomeTitle`) with h2 "Welcome to Marginalia", two `p.lib-welcome__lead` ("New here? Click Start with an empty library, then add a PDF." and "Did you use Marginalia in a browser before? Bring that library here:"; since DECISIONS D32 the card names no browser and tells a new reader what to click; before, the h2 was "Bring your library from Brave"), the steps "1. In that browser, open Marginalia and click Back up this browser's library." and "2. Here, click Import the newest backup.", and three buttons: `button.btn.btn--primary.lib-welcome__import` (data-focus "welcome:import") "Import the newest backup (<when>)"; `button.btn.lib-welcome__file` (data-focus "welcome:file") "Import a backup file…" (the Import dialog, `M.exporter.openDialog({scope: "all", tab: "import"})`); `button.btn.btn--ghost.lib-welcome__empty` (data-focus "welcome:empty") "Start with an empty library" (localStorage `marginalia.welcomeDismissed` = "1"; a storage that refuses hides the card for that page; focus goes to "Add PDF"). <when> is the newest backup of `GET /api/backup` (`M.backup.state().last`): "today, 09:12", "yesterday, 18:40", "10 Nov, 08:00", "31 Dec 2025, 08:00" (a newest backup named for a day after today shows that day alone, "12 Nov", even when its exportedAt says today, as after a time-zone change); the time is the backup's own `exportedAt` (GET /api/backup lists no time), read with one `GET /api/backup/latest` per listed file (name and size) and again when the window comes back to the front (window focus, or visibilitychange to visible; at most every 2 s it also asks `M.backup.refresh()`), so a backup made in Brave meanwhile shows up. Disabled states, with `span.lib-welcome__none` and the button's title: "No backup yet", "Looking for a backup…" (before the first listing), "The backup folder cannot be read — <error>", and (repair SEC-1 of 2026-11-11) "The newest backup is dated after today (<12 Nov>) — check the Mac's date and time, or use Import a backup file…" when `GET /api/backup`'s last is named for a day after its `today` (`welcome().ahead`): `GET /api/backup/latest` would send that file, which a clock once ahead or a time zone further east named, never the backup Brave's cover just wrote (that one is today's), and importing it would fill the empty library with it; while importing, the label is "Importing the newest backup…". The import (`importLatest()`): `GET /api/backup/latest` → `M.exporter.importJSON(text, {restoreSettings: true})` (its own summary toast) → B9 restores the PDFs (import:finished → `M.backup.restorePdfs`, "Restored N PDF(s) from your backup folder") → it waits for that (`M.backup.idle()`) → the toast (id "desktop-key", 12 s) "Paste your Anthropic API key again in Settings → LaTeX from snips (backups never contain it)." after every successful import. `importLatest()` also refuses a file whose name's day is after `M.backup.state().today` ("it is dated after today (12 Nov): check the Mac's date and time, or use Import a backup file…"; nothing imported). A failure toasts (id "desktop", 10 s) "Could not import the newest backup — <reason>" ("there is no backup in the backup folder yet", "Marginalia's server did not answer", the server's error, "it is not valid JSON", "it is not a Marginalia backup", the exporter's own message) and the card stays; once the import saved a book, deck or prompt the card goes by itself. M.library re-renders the home screen on `desktop:state` (coalesced like the data events).
- **Persistent storage** (E2.2e, E2.3c): in the app, at boot (`start()` → `askPersist()`, once), `navigator.storage.persisted()`; exactly false → `navigator.storage.persist()` once (wrapped: a rejection does nothing; never in a browser page). The app allows `persistent-storage` for its own origin in the request and the check handler, so `persisted()` is true from the first start and `persist()` answers true. Whether Chromium inside Electron would ever evict the library without it is not known; the grant is what the contract asks and what the self-test checks.
- **SPEC §17 extensions**: `M.desktop`: `isApp()`, `start()` → Promise<mode>, `ready()`, `mode()` → "unknown" | "checking" | "app" | "browser" | "cover", `covered()`, `state()` → `{mode, version, app, covered, busy, forwarded, importing}`, `headers(base, client)`, `refusesWrite(client)`, `askPersist()` → Promise<true|false|null>, `coverLink(hash)`, `showCover()`, `backUp()` → Promise<{ok, file, at, books, decks, prompts, pdfs, failed, cancelled, error}> (error "storage" for a store in memory mode, "empty" for an empty library), `welcome()` → null | `{last, at, when, status, error, importing}` plus `ahead` (the day, "12 Nov") only when last is named after today, `importLatest()` → Promise<{ok, report, error}>, `dismissWelcome()`; constants `CLIENT_HEADER` "X-Marginalia-Client", `APP_CLIENT` "app", `MOVE_CLIENT` "browser-move", `CHECK_MS` 3000, `WELCOME_KEY`, `MOVED_KEY` "marginalia.movedToApp", `MODES`, `TEXT`; `_pure` {whenText, dayLabel, hhmm, madeAt}; test hooks `_setFetch`, `_setOpener` (what follows a marginalia:// link: stub it in every page test that may show the cover), `_setCheckMs`, `_setNow`, `_reset`. `M.backup`: `runNow(opts)` (`{client, pdfs: false}`), `savePdfs(opts)` (`{client, onProgress(k, n), quiet}`; a run with its own client is another kind of run, and one with onProgress is never shared), `latest()` → Promise<{ok, name, text} | {ok: false, error, none?, outdated?}> (GET /api/backup/latest, the name from `X-Marginalia-Backup-Name`; 404 `no_backup` → none, "there is no backup in the backup folder yet"; another 404, 405 or 501 on 127.0.0.1 or localhost → outdated, "Marginalia's server cannot give the newest backup yet — restart Marginalia (marginalia restart in Terminal)"; nothing in the artifact, off http(s) or in memory mode), `idle()` (the old `_idle` hook is the same function), `libraryPdfs()` → `{books, sha, saved}`, `hasToday()` → boolean (the last listing holds `marginalia-backup-<today>.json` among any of its files; repair SEC-1); the texts "Marginalia is now an app — open it to back up" and "Marginalia is still starting — try again in a moment". `M.obsidian`: `COVERED`; send, sendAll and planAll wait while the mode is "checking" and refuse in "cover". `M.library`: the welcome card (no new API), `desktop:state`, planAll's `{covered: true}` → the toast COVERED. `M.app`: the boot order above; `route()` false while covered. New event (an addition to the SPEC §17.3 table): `desktop:state` (payload `M.desktop.state()`), desktop → library.
- **Window close** (E2.3b): the one switch is `lib/lifecycle.js` `CLOSE_HIDES = true` (main.js reads it once: `const CLOSE_HIDES = lifecycle.CLOSE_HIDES;`). `closeAction({closeHides, quitting, fullScreen})` → "close" (the switch off, or the app quitting), "hide" or "leave-full-screen". The window's close handler saves the window state as before, then for "hide" prevents the close and hides the window; for "leave-full-screen" it prevents the close, calls `setFullScreen(false)` and hides on `leave-full-screen` (a window hidden in full screen would leave its empty Space behind; the saved state then says `fullScreen: false`). What shows it again, all through `focusWindow` (restore if minimized, show if hidden, focus; never in self-test mode): the Dock icon (`activate` → `showWindow`), a second launch (`second-instance`: its link if any, then `showWindow()` and `app.focus({steal: true})`), a marginalia:// link (`open-url` → `goToHash`) and Window › Marginalia (`lib/menu.js` action `showWindow`, the last item after a separator, no shortcut). The window sets `excludedFromShownWindowsMenu = true`, so macOS's own window list does not show it a second time in that menu. With no window at all (destroyed some other way) each of these opens a new one (a link on its hash). Quitting is unchanged: Cmd+Q, the app menu's Quit, logging out and restarting reach `before-quit`, which sets `quitting` (Electron sends it before it closes the windows), so the close goes through; `will-quit` holds the quit, stops the server and quits again; SIGTERM, SIGINT and SIGHUP call `app.quit()`. `window-all-closed` quits only when the switch is off (`lifecycle.quitWhenAllClosed(CLOSE_HIDES)`; Electron does not send it while quitting). With the switch off the app behaves as in part 1 (closing the window quits). Why hide: the app and its server keep running, as Mac apps do, so "Open in Marginalia" links from Obsidian keep working while the window is closed.
- **Signing** (E2.3d; `lib/signing.js` `resolveIdentity({env, file, readFile})`, build only): the identity is `MARGINALIA_SIGN_IDENTITY` when it is set, trimmed (set to nothing or to "-" means ad hoc, also when desktop/sign-identity names a certificate: `MARGINALIA_SIGN_IDENTITY= npm run app` builds ad hoc); else the first non-empty line of `desktop/sign-identity`, trimmed (a certificate's name, not a secret; `desktop/.gitignore` ignores `/sign-identity`; a missing file means none); else ad hoc. A file that cannot be read for another reason (a folder, no permission), a name with a control character or longer than 512 characters stops the build before step 1. How (`scripts/build.js` `osxSignOptions`): ad hoc as in part 1; a name that starts with "Developer ID Application:" (case-sensitive; Apple's certificate, which has a Team ID) gets the hardened runtime (with entitlements.plist) and osx-sign's keychain look-up (`identityValidation`); any other name (a self-made certificate, which has no Team ID) is signed WITHOUT the hardened runtime (its library validation would refuse Electron's framework signed by a certificate without a Team ID, so the app would not start) and without the look-up (macOS does not trust a self-made certificate, so `security find-identity -v` leaves it out; codesign finds it by name, and the build fails when it cannot). Step 6 (`signatureInfo`, from `codesign -dv`): ad hoc must say `Signature=adhoc`; a named identity must be the first `Authority` (a 40-hex SHA-1 hash is accepted as given); the runtime flag must be on exactly for a Developer ID. The build prints its choice ("signing ad hoc"; "signing with "<name>" (from desktop/sign-identity), without the hardened runtime (a certificate that is not a Developer ID has no Team ID)"). What a certificate changes: its designated requirement names the bundle id and the certificate, which stay the same across builds, so macOS keeps the Documents permission (and any other privacy permission) across rebuilds; switching from ad hoc is one more new app to macOS (asked once more), then no more. It does not notarize the app, and Gatekeeper does not need it (the app is built on this Mac, never downloaded, so never quarantined). The Keychain Access steps are in desktop/README.md.
- **`desktop/vendor-lock.json`** (E2.3e; part of the project, not ignored): `{comment, files: {<URL>: "sha384-<base64>"}}`, the URLs sorted (`lib/vendor.js` `lockText`, `lockProblems`). It pins every file the build downloads for the offline copy, keyed by the URL it comes from: katex.min.css and the Google Fonts CSS as downloaded (before the rewrite), the 20 KaTeX woff2 fonts, Outfit's woff2 fonts (2 today), pdf.min.js, katex.min.js and the PDF.js worker: 27 URLs. For the four cdnjs files index.html pins with SRI the lock's value equals that SRI (test/vendor.test.js checks it). Every build (`scripts/build.js` always passes `lockFile`; `scripts/vendor.js` `readLock`, `lockVerifier`, `finishLock` and a verify hook in `makeFetcher`) checks every file, from the cache or the network, before it is used, and a download before it is cached. A mismatch stops the build: for a cached copy "<url> does not match vendor-lock.json (it pins …; the copy in <file> is …). That cached file is not the one this project pinned: delete it to download it again."; for a download, which never enters the cache, "… the download is … The CDN now sends another file than the one this project pinned, so the build stops. If that is expected, check the new file, then delete its line from desktop/vendor-lock.json and build again.". A URL the lock does not name yet is pinned (trust on first use, from the download or from a cache made before the lock existed); the lock is written only when the whole vendoring succeeded and it gained or lost an entry, and URLs the build no longer uses are dropped. A lock that is not valid JSON or not this shape (unknown keys, a URL the build would not download, a value that is not a sha384) stops the build. The build prints "vendor-lock.json: 27 files pinned and checked" (plus "N newly pinned", "N no longer used and dropped", "(written)"). Why: the Google Fonts CSS and the fonts have no SRI in index.html; the lock makes a changed file stop the build also after `.cache/` is deleted or on another Mac.
- **Self-test** (E2.3g; `lib/selftest.js`, main.js `runSelfTest`): the page checks run in two phases (`pageScript("start")`, `pageScript("import", {expect})`). Between them the main process notes the backup folder and the sandbox vault (`folderSnapshot`) and writes a prepared backup into `--backup-dir` only (each file created new, `wx`, mode 0600, folders 0700): `marginalia-backup-<local day>.json` (`preparedBackup()`, as Brave's cover writes it: one book, the web copy's test/sample.pdf with its PDF.js fingerprint as id, name "Self-test sample", its SHA-256, size and page count, in the folder "Self-test folder", two Q./A. prompts, settings `{obsidianFolder: "Self-test notes"}`, exportedAt now) and `PDFs/Self-test sample [<sha12>].pdf`. New checks: app-api (GET /api/app from the main process: 200 `{ok: true, app: true, version}`), app-mode (`M.desktop.mode()` "app", `#cover` hidden and empty), persisted (`navigator.storage.persisted()` true after boot's askPersist; since repair REQ-D of 2026-11-11 it follows the switch: main.js passes `persistentStorage: policy.ALLOWED_PERMISSIONS.has("persistent-storage")` to `evaluate`, and with the switch off persisted must be false and the permissions check expects persist() false, the query "denied" and only the clipboard granted; anything but false counts as on; before, a build with the switch off failed persisted and permissions, so `marginalia app` could not install it), app-only (from the app's page, a valid POST to /api/backup and to /api/obsidian/note without `X-Marginalia-Client` get 409 `{error: "app_only", message}`;
