no backup file and no note written), welcome-card ("Welcome to Marginalia", "Import the newest backup" disabled with "No backup yet", the two other buttons; after `M.backup.refresh()` "Import the newest backup (today, HH:MM)" with the backup's exportedAt, enabled), welcome-import (a click: the toasts in this order, "Imported 2 prompts (2 new, 0 updated, 0 unchanged) · 1 new collection · 1 new folder", "Restored 1 PDF from your backup folder" and the key reminder; the card gone; exactly the book in its folder, the folder and the two prompts; obsidianFolder restored), pdf-restore (hasBytes, sha256, hashAlgo, size; the stored bytes hash to that SHA-256), cover-mode and close-hides, and since D30 tabs ("Tabs", "In the Mac app"); backup-write, sample-pdf, permissions and hidden changed (above). cover-mode: a second hidden window (`show: false`, the same webPreferences, NO preload) on `ORIGIN + "/"` (no hash, so nothing is followed) in its own in-memory session partition `marginalia-self-test-brave` (on the app's session the one-writing-tab rule would make the app's own window step aside), which refuses every permission and download, cancels the cMap requests and records every request; in real Chromium: `window.marginaliaDesktop` absent, boot true, mode "cover", the cover with its title, "Open the app" an `<a>` whose href is `marginalia://open?hash=%23library` (read, never clicked) and the backup button, the other `#app` children inert, `M.obsidian.send()` refused with COVERED and its toast, `M.backup` never started (status "checking"), and after 1.5 s more its session asked GET /api/app and nothing of /api/backup, sent no POST, contacted no other host, and its window tried no navigation; the window is then destroyed. close-hides: the main window's `close()` (the red button, Shift+Cmd+W, and since D30 Cmd+W with one tab left) leaves it not destroyed and hidden, its page answering and the server running (with the switch off it passes as skipped: the close would quit the app mid-test). The self-test never shows the window, so `isVisible()` is false whether or not the close hid it; since repair F2 of 2026-11-11 `lib/selftest.js` `closeProbe(win, {settle, serverRunning})` (main.js `probeClose`) also watches that window's own `hide()` from the close until 300 ms later (a wrapper on that one window, removed again afterwards, also when `close()` throws), and the check needs `hid: true` (before, a close that was prevented but never hid the window passed). Order: the start phase, the folder snapshot, the prepared backup, the import phase, downloads, right-clicks, the key press and window.open, the second launch's link (from the restored book's reader), the tab commands (D30), GET /api/app, the Brave stand-in, the close; about 25 s; 31 checks plus the runner's 7 (the runner is unchanged).
- **The command** (`bin/marginalia`, E2.4): the installed app is `MARGINALIA_APP` (default `/Applications/Marginalia.app`, only when the variable is not set; trailing slashes dropped). Fail-closed: set but blank → "MARGINALIA_APP is set but empty; unset it, or set it to the whole path of Marginalia.app."; not an absolute path ending in `.app` → "MARGINALIA_APP must be the whole path of an app, such as /Applications/Marginalia.app (it is: <value>)."; both exit 1 for open, start, stop, restart, status and app (help, test and build ignore it). "Installed" = `<app>/Contents/Info.plist` has `CFBundleIdentifier` `local.marginalia.desktop` (`plutil -extract … raw`); nothing there, or another app, keeps every command as before (the browser and the command's own server). With the app installed: `marginalia` (open) and `start` stop the command's own server on the app's port, 8765 (the `is_ours` check; "Stopped the server that the marginalia command started at http://127.0.0.1:8765/ (the app uses that port).") and, when `MARGINALIA_PORT` names another port P, the one there ("… at http://127.0.0.1:P/ (the app replaces it)."; repair CMD-3 of 2026-11-11) and run `open -a <app>` ("Opened the Marginalia app."; a failure says "Could not open <app>." and exits 1); the app's own server or any other program on the port is never stopped (the app shows its own port dialog). `stop`: the app's pid is `pgrep -f -- "^<app, ERE-escaped>/Contents/MacOS/Marginalia( |$)"` (the main process only; helpers, other copies and look-alike paths never match); not running → "The Marginalia app is not running." (no Apple event: AppleScript would launch an app it tells to quit); running → `osascript -e 'if application id "local.marginalia.desktop" is running then tell application id "local.marginalia.desktop" to quit'`, and when osascript fails (Apple events refused in Privacy & Security › Automation) SIGTERM to that pid, which the app handles as a quit; it waits up to 10 s (`QUIT_WAIT`) → "The Marginalia app has quit." or "The Marginalia app (pid N) did not quit within 10 seconds. Quit it with Cmd+Q, then try again." (exit 1); then it stops the command's own server too. `restart` = stop, then start (nothing opens when the app did not quit). `status` (exit 0): "The Marginalia app <version> is installed at <app> and running (pid N)." or "… and not running. Open it with: marginalia", then port 8765, the app's port (repair CMD-3 of 2026-11-11: the app always uses 8765, since main.js reads only its own `--port` flag and `open -a` passes none; `MARGINALIA_PORT` moves only the command's own server; `MARGINALIA_APP_PORT`, default 8765, stands for it in test/test_command.py, which never looks at 8765): "Port 8765 is free." / "Port 8765 is used by the app's server (pid N)." / "Port 8765 is used by the server that the marginalia command started (pid N; log: L). marginalia stops it before it opens the app." / "Port 8765 is used by another program (pid N), so the app cannot start its server there."; plus "Port P (MARGINALIA_PORT) is used by the server that the marginalia command started (pid N; log: L). marginalia stops it before it opens the app." when the command's server runs on another `MARGINALIA_PORT`. Help names 8765 ("so the app gets port 8765", "status shows it and port 8765"). With `MARGINALIA_PORT` unset all output is as before. `marginalia app` (installed or not): refuses another app at MARGINALIA_APP ("<app> is not the Marginalia app, so it was left as it is. Set MARGINALIA_APP to install somewhere else.") and a running app ("Quit Marginalia first (Cmd+Q), then run marginalia app again."); needs `desktop/package.json` ("The Mac app's folder is missing: <desktop>"), npm on PATH and `desktop/node_modules` ("The Mac app's build tools are not installed yet. Install them once with: cd "<desktop>" && npm ci"; no automatic `npm ci`); prints "Building and testing the Marginalia app (npm run app in <desktop>)." and runs `npm run app`. A failure → "The build or its self-test failed, so the installed app was left as it is." (exit 1); a build with no app of this bundle id at `desktop/out/Marginalia-darwin-arm64/Marginalia.app` → "The build made no Marginalia app at <path>, so the installed app was left as it is." (exit 1). It checks again that the app does not run; only then it installs in three steps (repairs SEC-3 and CMD-1 of 2026-11-11; before, a failed move or copy could delete the previous copy and a signal could leave a half-finished install): (1) `ditto` copies the new app next to the installed one, to `<MARGINALIA_APP>.new` (`STAGED`; a name that does not end in .app, so LaunchServices never takes it for an app, and only the app's main Info.plist declares marginalia://), after removing what a killed install left there (`clear_leftovers`); (2) the installed copy, if any, moves to `desktop/out/previous/Marginalia.app`, while an older previous copy waits beside it as `Marginalia.app.old` (`SET_ASIDE`); (3) `<app>.new` is renamed to `<app>` (one rename in the same folder); only then the older previous copy is deleted. A failed step puts everything back, the older previous copy included (`undo_install`, the last step first): "Could not copy the new app to <app>, so the installed app was left as it was." (the copy or the rename) or "Could not move the installed app to <previous>, so it was left as it is." (the move), exit 1; when the installed copy cannot be put back, "Could not copy the new app to <app>, nor put the old one back: it is in <previous>.", and "The version before that is in <previous>.old." when that one is still aside. From the copy on, INT, HUP and TERM are only noted by a trap (`interrupted=SIG`; bash runs it once the running ditto or mv has ended) and acted on at each checkpoint (`give_up`): everything is undone, "Stopped before the new app was in place, so the installed app was left as it was." is printed (or "…, and the old one could not be put back: it is in <previous>."), and the command ends by that signal; a signal after the rename undoes nothing: the install finishes (lsregister, "Installed …"), then the command ends by it (`end_install`). After `kill -9` or a power cut the next run removes `<app>.new`, and puts `Marginalia.app.old` back when `previous/` is empty, otherwise deletes it. Then it runs `lsregister -f <app>` and `lsregister -u` on the build copy and on the previous copy (so a marginalia:// link never opens an older version; lsregister from PATH first, else the system's; a failure prints "Note: lsregister … failed." and does not fail the install), and prints "Installed Marginalia <CFBundleShortVersionString>. Open it with: marginalia". `test` also runs `node --test` in desktop/ when `desktop/package.json` exists (installed or not); `help` adds "marginalia app" and a paragraph on the commands with the app; `build` is unchanged, and `web` (it opened the claude.ai copy) was removed with that copy on 2026-11-11. Without the app installed, open, start, stop, restart, status and build behave exactly as before (test/test_command.py's `WithoutTheAppTest` passes against the old command, except help's new line and test's desktop step). It runs on macOS's bash 3.2.
- **Tests** (part 2): Python: test/test_serve.py `AppServerTest`, `LatestBackupApiTest` and `ServerOptionsTest.test_the_app_flag_takes_only_a_version_and_the_apps_server_says_so` (every rule with and without `--app`, every POST route × refused header values with nothing written, the guard's place in the order, the version rule, a real serve.py); test/test_command.py (16 in part 2, 20 since the repairs of 2026-11-11: `WithoutTheAppTest`, `WithTheAppTest` with the real pgrep against `exec -a` look-alikes, `InstallTest`; a copy of the command in a temp folder; stand-ins for open, osascript, lsregister, npm, pgrep, node and python3 on PATH, and ditto and mv stand-ins that run the system's commands; `MARGINALIA_APP` in the temp folder, free ports in `MARGINALIA_PORT` and `MARGINALIA_APP_PORT`, a fake HOME and `MARGINALIA_BACKUP_DIR`; the repairs add a failed copy, move or rename, INT, HUP and TERM during the install, a killed install's leftovers and the app's port); 179 Python tests in all since D23 (169 in part 2, 173 after its repairs). Node in `marginalia/`: test/desktop.test.js (27, 26 in part 2: the modes and the check, the headers, persist, the cover's refusals in M.backup and M.obsidian, the move client, the 409 app_only text, latest(), `hasToday()` with a file named after today (repair SEC-1), the time helpers, welcome() and importLatest(), index.html and the CSS) and test/desktop-dom.test.js (27, 19 in part 2: the real modules in tools/smoke.js's window (27 then, 28 since FEATURES P2.4) and the real boot: the cover, its keys and focus, `#tabLock`, Obsidian refused, the backup with and without today's file, errors, an empty library, the reader forward once; normal mode for a 404, an error, a timeout and a slow answer; the artifact unchanged; the welcome card and the real import end to end; the repairs of 2026-11-11: SEC-1, a file named after today beside today's (the question still asked, Cancel writes nothing, the result line naming the file to choose) and without one of today (no question, the note), and the welcome card refusing such a newest backup (disabled with why, `importLatest()` refusing it); F1, the move remembered only after a backup written, the note at the next load, "Back up this browser's library again?" with and without a backup of today (Cancel writes nothing, one question, Back up again backs up and moves the record); F5, a store in memory mode said to be unreadable, not empty, with no request); test/smoke.test.js (27 modules then, 28 since FEATURES P2.4) and test/app.test.js (boot's first request is GET /api/app) changed; 873 tests (864 in part 2). `desktop/`: test/main.test.js (13: main.js loaded in plain Node with Electron mocked through `Module._load`, the electron package never loaded; findPython, probePort and ServerProcess replaced; open-url valid, invalid and before ready, a link at the first launch, a second launch, close hides, full screen, the switch off, Cmd+Q and SIGTERM stopping the server, `--app`, persistent-storage, new windows after a destroy, and (repair F2) the self-test's close probe against main.js's own close handler, also from full screen), test/lifecycle.test.js (3), test/signing.test.js (8), and menu, policy, server (a real serve.py with `--app`), build, vendor (5 lock tests, the real lock against index.html's SRI) and selftest updated: 136 tests in part 2; the repairs of 2026-11-11 add test/selftest.test.js's closeProbe test (a close that hid the window, one only prevented, one that went through, a stopped server, the wrapper removed) and its persistent-storage switch test (off: no grant expected and a grant is a fault; on, or not said: the grant demanded), a "prevented, never hidden" fault case, a source check in test/build.test.js, and test/docs.test.js (5: the facts these repairs put into the docs, read from this checkout: the command tried only as a copy on free ports and a cover test page only on `/` or `#library` in CLAUDE.md and "Safety rules"; README's "marginalia app" promise, the Documents prompt before the move, the stale Brave tab after Cmd+Q, Open in Marginalia with the app): 145 tests.
- **Decisions made in the builds** (binding until the user changes them): `marginalia stop` falls back to SIGTERM when osascript fails; `MARGINALIA_APP` is fail-closed; `marginalia app` also unregisters the previous copy and needs `desktop/node_modules` (no automatic `npm ci`); `marginalia test` always runs the desktop tests, and help changes with or without the app; a new module owns the mode, the header and the cover; a backup's time comes from its own `exportedAt`; the key reminder shows after every successful import; the cover is opaque and nothing renders under it; lines beyond the contract (the cover's note line, the empty-library message, the card's "Looking for a backup…", "The backup folder cannot be read — …" and "Importing the newest backup…", singular forms); "Open the app" takes focus first, and after "Start with an empty library" focus goes to Add PDF; Window › Marginalia is an explicit menu item and the window stays out of macOS's own list; `MARGINALIA_SIGN_IDENTITY` set to nothing means ad hoc; a self-made certificate is signed without the keychain look-up; the lock is keyed by URL, pins the downloaded bytes (not the rewritten CSS), trusts on first use and drops unused URLs; the self-test's sample PDF arrives through the import and the B9 restore; a second launch, the Dock icon, the menu item or a link open a new window when none exists. Repairs of 2026-11-11 (Claude's choices, open to the user): a backup of today is any listed file (SEC-1); a newest backup named after today is never imported by the card's button (disabled with why; Import a backup file… still opens the file picker), and the cover's result line then names the file to choose; the browser remembers its move in localStorage `marginalia.movedToApp` and from then on asks "Back up this browser's library again?" (danger style) instead of backing up or asking "Replace today's backup?" (F1; a question, not a block, so a move that did not work can be done again); a store in memory mode is called unreadable on the cover (F5); `marginalia app` copies the new app next to the installed one and renames it into place, keeps the older previous copy until the new app is in place, undoes the install on INT, HUP or TERM before the rename, and after the rename finishes the install before the signal ends the command (SEC-3, CMD-1); with the app installed the app's port is 8765 whatever `MARGINALIA_PORT` says, the command's server on `MARGINALIA_PORT` is stopped too ("the app replaces it"), and `MARGINALIA_APP_PORT` stands for 8765 in the command's tests (CMD-3); the self-test's close probe watches the window's own `hide()` through a wrapper on that one window during the close (F2); the self-test follows the persistent-storage switch, and a report that does not name it counts as on (REQ-D).
- **Limits** (part 2): a Brave tab still running code from before part 2 shows no cover until it is reloaded; while the app runs, its writes get the 409, and its notices show the code ("Automatic backup failed — app_only"); a tab with the new code that checked while no app server ran (normal mode) shows the 409's message instead. Once the app quits nothing answers on 8765, and such a tab (either kind: it never showed the cover) falls back to link mode (`M.obsidian.detect`) for a send the user starts: the first send after the quit fails ("Marginalia's server did not answer") and keeps its marker, later ones and Send all open `obsidian://new … append` links (no vault parameter: whatever vault Obsidian has open), so a card the app's library already sent can get a second block in the note (review finding REQ-A of 2026-11-11, reproduced with real serve.py processes). README "What Brave shows now" and desktop/README say to reload or close Brave's tabs once the app is installed; a tab that showed the cover refuses every send also after the quit. Switching a browser-mode tab to the cover when a POST gets 409 `app_only` would cover tabs with the new code only, not those with older code (not done; js/desktop.js is web code). `marginalia app` moves the installed copy to `desktop/out/previous` with a rename only when the app's folder and `desktop/out` are on one disk, as on this Mac; across disks `mv` copies, and a failure partway through that copy is not fully undone. Chromium may block the reader route's automatic marginalia:// link without a click, or ask "Open Marginalia?" first; "Open the app" is the fallback (not verified in Brave). After Cmd+Q nothing listens on port 8765, so an "Open in Marginalia" link reaches no server until the app runs again. Two Brave tabs in cover mode keep the one-writing-tab rule (the older shows "Marginalia is open in another tab" over its cover). A self-test run that crosses local midnight fails (the backup's day and "today" disagree); run it again. Repair F1 is per browser profile: another browser or profile, a private window, or a browser whose site data was cleared asks no more than the contract (with a library of its own, a backup from there is a move in its own right); the server does not record which client wrote a file, so the app's automatic backup still counts a `browser-move` file of today as the day's backup, and `GET /api/backup/latest` still sends the newest file by name (both need serve.py; a decision for the user). Not verified on this Mac (agents never install, launch the installed app, open marginalia:// links or touch the keychain): the visible window (close hides it, full screen left first, the Dock icon, Window › Marginalia and whether macOS's own list really omits the window, Cmd+Q, logout or restart while hidden), marginalia:// links delivered by macOS to a running app with a hidden window, Brave's forwarding of the link, `osascript` quit and any Automation prompt it triggers, `open -a`, `lsregister -f/-u` in /Applications, `marginalia app` end to end, signing with a self-made certificate (the "Always Allow" keychain prompt, the app starting without the hardened runtime, the Documents permission surviving a rebuild) and with a Developer ID.

## Prompt craft (binding; added 2026-11-11)
After reading Andy Matuschak's guide "How to write good prompts" the user chose four features (DECISIONS D25, FEATURES-P1): cues in cloze blanks, more writing hints, flag and delete during review, struggling prompts. No data-format change: `DB_VERSION` and `SCHEDULER_VERSION` stay 1, no new stored field on prompts, cards or collections except the prompt field `revisedAt` (FIXES-1 FIX-5, below), old JSON backups import unchanged. Choices Claude made, each one named constant or rule: the cue separator `::` (`M.prompts.CUE_SEP`), the review keys F and Backspace (`M.keys.PRESETS`), 3 days for "struggling" (`M.scheduler.STRUGGLE_DAYS`), the hint thresholds (`M.prompts` constants below).
- **Cue syntax** (P1.1; js/prompts.js `scanDeletions`, the source of truth; js/markup.js and js/exporter.js scan by the same rule and read `M.prompts.CUE_SEP`, falling back to "::"): inside a cloze deletion, every unescaped `::` at depth 1 of a text run (not inside math, an image token or nested braces) is a candidate and the LAST one splits the deletion into the hidden part (before) and the cue (after); `{a:::b}` gives hidden `a:` and cue `b`. Both parts must be non-blank after trimming, else there is no cue and the deletion is exactly as before (`{a::}`, `{a:: }`, `{::b}`, `{a::b::}` have none; an earlier `::` is never tried). A backslash escapes the next character as before; when that character starts a `::`, both colons are skipped: `\::` is one literal `::`, `\\::` an escaped backslash and a real separator. The set of deletions (count, start, end) is the same as before for every text: a cue only splits the inside of a deletion.
- **`M.prompts.parse`**: a deletion is `{start, end, text, cue?}`; `start`/`end` span the braces; without a cue the object has no `cue` key (as before); with one, `text` is the hidden part as typed (untrimmed) and `cue` the trimmed cue; the separator sits at `start + 1 + text.length`. `reconcileCards` is unchanged: it matches cards on `text`, now the hidden part, so adding, changing or removing a cue never resets, drops or reorders a card (also with two deletions that share a hidden part). New export `M.prompts.escapeCues(text, from, to) → string`: every unescaped `::` of the text runs inside `[from, to)` becomes `\::` (math and image tokens untouched; `:::` → `\:::`, `::::` → `\::\::`).
- **`M.markup.render`** (class `.cloze-cue`): the asked deletion while hidden is the blank as before (width and `data-len` from the hidden part), then a space and `<span class="cloze-cue">(CUE)</span>`; revealed, `<mark class="cloze-reveal">HIDDEN</mark>` with no cue. Other deletions under a mask show their hidden part only. Without a mask on modes card, list and preview (or `cloze: true`): `<mark class="cloze-mark">HIDDEN</mark>` then ` <span class="cloze-cue">(CUE)</span>`. Mode review without a mask (and `cloze: false`) keeps the text as typed. A cue renders like other prompt text (math, images, escaping); the blanks before the separator are not shown and the cue is trimmed. `\::` shows as `::` exactly where `\{` shows as `{`. render() does not know a prompt's kind: in Q./A. text, the list and the editor preview already showed `{x}` as a mark and now show mark + cue for `{x::y}`; margin Q./A. cards and review cards show such text as typed. CSS: one rule in styles.css beside `.cloze-blank`, `.cloze-cue { color: var(--ink-2); }` (no size: the surrounding text's); it is ≥ 4.5:1 on `--bg`, `--surface` and `--review-bg` in light and dark (`--review-ink-2` was 3.5:1 on the light review background, so the overlay does not override it). The margin never hides a cloze card's answers (A and Enter hide `.card__a`, which a cloze card has not), so a margin cloze card shows the marks with each cue beside its mark.
- **Exports**: `plain()`, `toAnki()`, `toMarkdown()`, the Markdown export and the JSON backup keep the text as typed. `M.markup.toObsidian`: `{x::cue}` → `==x==` (separator, cue and the blanks around the separator dropped), only where `M.prompts.parse` reports a cue for the same braces; `\::` stays as typed. Anki TSV (`M.exporter.numberClozes(text, deletions, colon)` and `ankiRow`): `{x::cue}` → `{{cN::x::cue}}` (the hidden part as typed minus the blanks before the separator, `::`, the trimmed cue). Anki splits at the FIRST `::`, so in the hidden part of a deletion with a cue every colon that follows a colon, and a last colon of the hidden part, is written `&#58;` (`{a::b::c}` → `{{c1::a:&#58;b::c}}`, `{a:::b}` → `{{c1::a&#58;::b}}`); in a deletion without a cue only a typed `\::` is guarded (`{a\::b}` → `{{c1::a\:&#58;b}}`); the cue itself is not guarded. Byte-identical: for every text in which parse reports no cue and no deletion holds `\::`, parse, plain, toObsidian, toAnki, toMarkdown, the Anki TSV and the Markdown export give the bytes they gave before this build (test/cues-golden.json, written by the code before cues, pins 107 texts; never regenerate it from new code). The Obsidian never-twice rule depends on this: no note sent before looks edited.
- **The cloze commands**: C in the reader (`M.anchors.sentenceAround`, and the reader's fallback `"{" + quote + "}"`) and Alt+Shift+C / the `{}` button in the editor (`M.editor.wrapBraces(text, start, end)`, whose `end` grows by the backslashes added) call `M.prompts.escapeCues` on the wrapped part only, so selected text never makes a cue; braces inside a selection are still not escaped (they never were), and a `::` inside math in the selection is left alone. A selection without `::` is wrapped exactly as before.
- **Existing prompts** (P1.1 f, accepted): a stored cloze prompt whose deletion already holds a top-level `::` with text on both sides (`{10::30}`) now has a cue: the blank shows "(30)", its card and schedule stay, its Anki row is the same bytes when no blanks surround the `::`, and its Obsidian block reads `==10==` the next time it is sent (an unedited prompt that was already sent is not sent again: the send state goes by the stamps). A Q./A. prompt with `{a::b}` shows mark + cue in the list and the editor preview only.
- **Hints** (P1.2, `M.prompts.lint`; shown where hints show: the editor's lint line and batch chips, the margin card's chip; never blocking). Constants exported on `M.prompts`: `LONG_ANSWER_WORDS` 15, `LONG_QUESTION_WORDS` 25, `MANY_DELETIONS` 3 (the old thresholds, now named), `EITHER_OR_WORDS` 6, `LIST_MIN_ITEMS` 3, `LIST_ITEM_WORDS` 5, `LONG_CLOZE_WORDS` 30. Order, worst first (with the three hints of D26, below): no_answer, empty_answer, yes_no, either_or, example_prompt, list_answer, long_answer, long_question, book_bound, undated, multi_sentence_cloze, long_cloze, many_deletions. Words are counted as before (a formula or an image is one word).
  - `yes_no` (code and message unchanged, "Yes/no questions are easy to guess — ask for the fact itself"): the question starts with is, are, do, does, can, did, was, were, has, have, had, will, would, should, could or must (whole word, any case), OR the answer, trimmed, lower-cased and without trailing `. ! ? … , ; :` and blanks, is exactly yes, no, true or false, OR (FIXES-1 FIX-6 e) the trimmed answer starts with "yes" or "no" followed by a comma, a period or its end (`YES_NO_LEAD_RE`: "No, never", "Yes. Always"; not "No net force acts", "Nothing", "No: …"). One hint when both hold. It fires only when either_or does not (FIXES-1 FIX-3).
  - `either_or` (qa), "The question offers the answer as a choice — ask for the fact itself": checked BEFORE yes_no (FIXES-1 FIX-3): when it holds and the answer is no yes/no answer (above), the hint is either_or and yes_no does not fire, also for a question that starts with a yes/no word ("Are electrons bosons or fermions?" / "Fermions"); never both. It holds when the answer has 1 to 6 words, they stand in the question as one run of whole words, and the question holds the word "or". Words: lower case; apostrophes (' ’ ʼ) dropped; any other character that is not a letter, digit or combining mark reads as a blank; each formula is one word made of its source without blanks (`$x^2$` ≠ `$x$`), each image one word made of its id.
  - `list_answer` (qa), "The answer is a list of N items — a whole list is hard to recall; make one {blank} per item": each formula and image first becomes one placeholder word (a line break, comma or "and" inside math never splits). (i) Several non-blank lines: a line is an item when it starts with a list marker (`- `, `* `, `• `, `– `, `1. `, `1) `, `a. `, `a) `), or has at most 5 words, does not end with a colon and is more than one formula or image alone; 3 or more item lines are a list (N = the item lines); so are 3 or more lines that are each one formula or image alone when no line of words is an item. When no line is a sentence or an intro (every non-blank line is an item line or one formula or image alone) and at least `LIST_FORMULA_LINES` (2) lines are a formula alone, the formula lines count as items too, N = all those lines (FIXES-1 FIX-6 c: "$V(x)$" / "$\Psi(x,0)$" / "boundary conditions" is a list of 3). A sentence, a formula and a second sentence are no list ("It reads" / formula / "for every state" neither). (ii) Exactly one non-blank line that splits into 3 or more non-blank parts at `;`, `,` (not a comma between two digits, as in 1,000), ` and `, ` or ` (any case, a blank on each side), each part at most 5 words, and (FIXES-1 FIX-6 b) whose last joint is " and " or " or " (also after a comma: "a, b, and c"), or which holds `LIST_SEMICOLONS` (2) or more semicolons (one semicolon joins two clauses: "Uniform inside, $x$; a pure dipole outside" is no list), or whose parts all have at most `LIST_SHORT_PART_WORDS` (2) words, or which has `LIST_MANY_PARTS` (4) or more parts (four law names joined by commas are a list; the last two conditions were set by the parent session after the repair): N = the parts. "The electric field, not the potential, is discontinuous" is no list; "up, down and strange" and "$\hat x$, $\hat p$, $\hat H$" are. Two items are never a list. It may come with long_answer, either_or or yes_no.
  - `long_cloze` (cloze), "Long cloze (N words) — keep only the words around the blank": the body without its cues has more than 30 words and multi_sentence_cloze did not fire. multi_sentence_cloze and long_cloze both read the body without its cues (for each deletion with a cue the blanks before the separator, the separator and the cue are cut); a text without a cue reads as before.
- **Review keys** (P1.3 a; js/keys.js, js/settings.js, js/app.js, js/library.js): `M.keys.REVIEW_ACTIONS` = showAnswer, forgot, remembered, undo, skip, goToSource, flag, delete, exit; every preset binds flag "f" and delete "Backspace" (Default `Space 1 2 u s g f Backspace Escape`, Arrows `Space ArrowLeft ArrowRight u s g f Backspace Escape`, Vim `Space j k u s g f Backspace q`, Anki-like `Space 1 3 z s g f Backspace Escape`). `M.keys.LATE_REVIEW_ACTIONS` = flag, delete. A stored `settings.reviewKeys` from before this build lacks them: `M.keys.reviewMap` gives a missing late action its Default binding unless another review action of the stored map already uses that key (canonical comparison, every key of an array binding; unknown ids ignored), else it stays unbound (null). js/settings.js keeps its own copy of the Default preset and fills the stored map itself at load and on `set("reviewKeys", …)` by the same rule (`sanitizeReviewKeys`, with its own copy of the key-name tables so that it never depends on keys.js; test/review-keys.test.js checks that both give the same map for the same vectors): never two review actions on one key. `M.keys.presetOf`: a missing late action does not count against a preset, so a stored map equal to a preset on the seven old actions still reports it. Settings › Review shortcuts lists "Flag for revision" and "Delete prompt" (rebindable like the others; the shown map is `M.keys.reviewMap(settings.reviewKeys)`); the `?` sheet's Review section names them the same (its Reader section keeps "Flag card" / "Delete card"). `M.app` dispatches flag → `M.review.flag()`, delete → `M.review.remove()`.
- **Flag in review** (P1.3 b): `M.review.flag()` toggles `flagged` on the current card's prompt with the reader's kind of save, `M.cache.savePrompt(Object.assign({}, p, {flagged: !p.flagged}), {content: true})` (so `contentUpdatedAt` moves, `revisedAt` does not: the prompt keeps struggling, FIXES-1 FIX-5), before and after the answer shows; nothing on the end and empty screens or while suspended; not an undo step. The `prompt:saved` it causes re-renders the card in place (the answer stays shown). While the prompt is flagged the card shows `span.review__flag` "⚑ Flagged". Under the bar and the undo line, `div.review__tools` holds the quiet buttons `button.review__link.review__tool[data-action=flag]` "Flag" / "Unflag" and `…review__tool--danger[data-action=delete]` "Delete", each with its key cap (none when unbound), before and after the answer shows; in the Tab cycle they come last (after Undo).
- **Delete in review** (P1.3 c): `M.review.remove()` deletes the current card's prompt at once with `M.cache.removePrompt` (the whole prompt, all its cards; no confirm, as in the reader), before or after the answer shows. Order, so that nothing is handled twice: an undo snapshot "delete" (queue, index, asks, skips, dropped, the session's `removed` set, the log length, revealed), then every queue item of that prompt from the current index on leaves the queue, the prompt id joins `removed`, revealed becomes false, then `removePrompt` (its `prompt:deleted` then finds another current card, or none), then the next card or the end. A delete the cache refuses (a tab that stepped aside: the prompt stays live) puts the session back and makes no undo step. `undo()` of a delete restores the prompt (`M.cache.restorePrompt`, in memory at once) and the session exactly as the snapshot holds it; the undo link reads "Undo delete" (the end screen's too; "Undo" / "Undo last grade" otherwise, as before). A toast "Prompt deleted" with one action "Undo" (8 s, id "review-undo-delete") does the session's undo while the delete is still the session's last step and the session is not suspended; otherwise (after a later grade or skip, or once the session is gone) it only restores the prompt, which never rejoins the session (an item of a prompt in `removed` is never queued again, not even as a retry tail). The session's undo closes the toast. `restorePrompt` moves `contentUpdatedAt` (a resurrection must win in import merges) but not `revisedAt`, so a struggling prompt that was deleted and restored keeps its count (FIXES-1 FIX-5; before, it started again).
- **Summary and end screen** (P1.3 c, d): the summary (`review:ended`) gains `deleted` (the prompts deleted in this session that are still deleted when it is made) and `flagged` ([{promptId, cardIndex}]: the prompts shown in this session, in the order first shown, that are live and flagged when it ends and are not still shaky). Grades, skips and still-shaky items of a prompt deleted in the session are not counted (`reviewed`, `grades`, `remembered`, `forgot`, `skipped`, `log` leave it out; `total` is the first pass as before). The line reads "… · N skipped · N deleted · …" ("N deleted" only when N > 0). Under the still-shaky list, `h2.review__flagged-title` "Flagged for revision" and `ul.review__shaky.review__flagged`, rows as the still-shaky rows (context line, question, "Edit"); a flagged still-shaky prompt shows once, in the still-shaky list, with `span.review__shaky-flag` "⚑" in its context line. The starburst draws no ray for a deleted prompt.
- **Struggling** (P1.4; js/scheduler.js, pure): `STRUGGLE_DAYS` 3; `missDays(card, since)` → the number of different local days (`M.util.dayKey`) that hold a log entry with outcome "forgot" and a numeric `at >= since` (since: a number, default 0); `struggling(card, since)` → `missDays >= STRUGGLE_DAYS`; extension `promptMissDays(p)` → the most of any card's missDays since `p.revisedAt` (0 when absent), 0 for anything but a live Q./A. or cloze prompt. A prompt struggles when `promptMissDays(p) >= STRUGGLE_DAYS`. The review log is the source, so `replay` does not change. Only a change of the prompt's TEXT restarts the count (FIXES-1 FIX-5, replacing P1.4 b's "any edit"): a flag, tags, a move to a deck, another passage, or a delete and its undo keep a struggling prompt struggling. Three misses on one day are one miss day.
- **`revisedAt`** (FIXES-1 FIX-5; js/cache.js, js/exporter.js): a prompt field (a number, ms), the time the prompt's text last changed. `M.cache.savePrompt(p, {content: true})` sets it to now when the text it stores differs from the text the cache last loaded or stored for that prompt (a second small map beside the content keys); a new prompt has none; no other writer sets it (a `{content: false}` save such as an import stores the record's own value). It is a content field of the §9 import merge (`PROMPT_CONTENT_FIELDS`): it travels with the text of the side that wins, and the merged record has none when that side has none; a value that is not a finite number is dropped on import. It rides in JSON backups like any prompt field. `DB_VERSION` stays 1. `contentUpdatedAt` keeps its meaning for the merge; nothing that counts struggling reads it any more.
- **The struggling line** (P1.4 c): once the answer shows, a card with `missDays(card, prompt.revisedAt || 0) >= STRUGGLE_DAYS` shows `div.review__struggle` "Forgotten on N days — split it or add a cue" (in `div.review__struggle-slot`, which stays empty before the answer shows), never before.
- **Prompt list chips** (P1.3 e, P1.4 d; js/list.js, css/list.css): under the search box, `div.list__chips` (role group, "Filters") holds one `button.chip.list__chip[data-chip=<id>][aria-pressed]` per entry of `M.list.CHIPS`, "<label> <n>" (`span.list__chip-label`, `span.list__chip-count`): "Flagged n" (flagged live prompts of the list, any kind) and "Struggling n" (live Q./A. and cloze prompts that struggle). n counts the list's live prompts whatever the search and the other chips say. A chip shows only while n > 0 or while it is on (`.list__chip--on`, aria-pressed true); the bar hides when no chip shows; a chip that hides while it has focus hands focus to the search box. A click toggles it; chips that are on combine with each other and with the search (AND). Chips are plain buttons (Tab stops) inside the list, so the list's own key rules apply as to its other buttons, except (FIXES-1 FIX-2) that while the focus is anywhere inside `.list__chips`, Backspace, Delete, F and E do nothing (`CHIP_INERT_KEYS`; stopped, so neither the list nor M.app acts on the selected row); Space and Enter press the chip; Esc and the arrows keep the list's behaviour. The counts follow every re-render and every one-row update (`updateRow` now also checks the chips: a saved prompt that leaves the filter re-renders the list). With chips on and nothing shown the empty line reads "No prompts match the filter" or "No prompts match “q” with the filter" (without chips as before). A struggling row's meta line carries `span.list__struggle` "struggling" titled "Forgotten on N days". To add a chip, add an entry `{id, label, test(p, info, data)}` to `CHIPS` (info = `_pure.rowInfo(p)` → {flagged, missDays, struggling}; an optional `prepare(prompts)` computes `data` once per render over the list's live prompts, and a chip with `prepare` makes every one-prompt save re-render the list). Extensions: `M.list.CHIPS`, `M.list.activeChips()` → [id], `M.list._pure.rowInfo`. D26 adds a third chip, "Look-alikes n" (below).
- **Second set (D26, FEATURES-P2, 2026-11-11)**: from an analysis of Matuschak's guide and its further reading the user chose four more features: three more hints, Split, "Another angle" starters and look-alike prompts. Still no data-format change (`DB_VERSION` and `SCHEDULER_VERSION` 1, no new stored field on prompts, cards or collections; FIXES-1 later added the prompt field `revisedAt`); one new pure module, js/similar.js (`M.similar`), and one new settings key, `notAlike`. Choices Claude made, each one named constant or rule: the word lists of the three hints, `EXAMPLE_GAP`, the year range, the narrowing of above/below and of "the" + section/book/paper/article/text/passage (accepted by the user's session on 2026-11-11), the Split flow, the starters' wording (`M.editor.ANGLES`: 28 in 10 groups since the user's note of 2026-11-11, eight before), the similarity rule and its numbers (`M.similar` constants), "Not alike" kept in settings. Nothing here ever blocks a save, a review or an export.
- **Three more hints** (D26, P2.1; `M.prompts.lint(parsed, now)`: `now`, ms or a Date, default the clock, gives only the year in undated's message; `parse(text)` calls `lint(out)`). Text inside math runs and image tokens never fires any of them. New exports: `EXAMPLE_GAP` 3, `YEAR_MIN` 1500, `YEAR_MAX` 2199, `UNDATED_WORDS` (a copy); FIXES-1 adds `UNDATED_NOUNS`, `LIST_SHORT_PART_WORDS` 2, `LIST_MANY_PARTS` 4, `LIST_SEMICOLONS` 2, `LIST_FORMULA_LINES` 2 and `EXAMPLE_TRIGGERS` (the phrases).
  - `example_prompt` (qa), "Example questions train one remembered instance — also ask from an instance to the idea": over the question's words (as either_or reads them), "example" or "examples" stands at the start of the question or after a trigger (`EXAMPLE_TRIGGERS`: give, name, list, state, provide, "what is", "what are"; whole words) with at most `EXAMPLE_GAP` (3) words between, none of them in `EXAMPLE_BREAKS` (this that these those in on at of from for with by to into about under over as is are was were be been does do did which what where when why how and or if it its not). An article or a number word between is no break (FIXES-1 FIX-6 f: "the" left the list, so "Give the standard example of …" fires); a possessive ("the example's", "the examples'") is read as another word. Fires: "Name an example of a stock", "Give two examples of …", "What are examples of …", "Examples of …?", "Provide an example of …". Not: "In which example does …", "What is the force in the example …", "What is the example's main assumption?", "counterexample".
  - `book_bound` (qa: the question; cloze: the body), "The wording points into the book (“<match>”) — phrase it so it stands alone": one case-insensitive expression, `BOOK_BOUND_RE`, over each text run (braces read as blanks, so "the {author}" matches); the first match by position wins and <match> is it as typed (braces removed, blanks collapsed). It fires on: "this" + `BOOK_NOUNS` (chapter, section, book, paper, article, text, passage, author, authors); "the" + chapter, author, authors; "the" + `BOOK_LOOSE` (section, book, paper, article, text, passage) only as a reference: after `BOOK_PREPS` (in, from, according to, as in, throughout; the match then includes the preposition, as in “In the text”), before a verb of saying (`BOOK_VERBS`: say, state, claim, argue, define, show, give, use, call, describe, derive, mention, note, explain, introduce, prove, discuss, present, assume, conclude, suggest, mean, measure, report, find, propose, write, refer, list, cover, treat, contain, include, with or without -s/-es; the verb is not part of the match) or as a possessive ("the article's"); "in" or "from" + "the" + figure, diagram, table, example or problem (FIXES-1 FIX-6 d: "In the figure", "in the example"); "the" + last, next or following + chapter or section (FIX-6 d: "the last chapter"); "the following", "the previous", "earlier in"; "above" / "below" only as a reference (and never before a hyphen, "the above-barrier …", nor after "cross section"/"cross-section": FIX-6 d): after "the", after a word that names a part of a text (`REF_NOUNS`: equation(s), eqn., eq., expression, formula, figure, fig., diagram, table, example(s), passage, paragraph, sentence, section, chapter, text, list, definition, result, derivation, argument, discussion, statement, quote, quotation, proof, problem, exercise, graph, plot, sketch) or after a verb of showing (`REF_VERBS`: see, as, shown, given, described, mentioned, defined, discussed, derived, stated, listed, quoted, cited, noted, explained, introduced, presented, outlined, illustrated, depicted, written, displayed), so "below $T_c$", "the energy above the gap" and "from above" do not fire; a numbered reference: `REF_LABELS` (figure(s), fig(s)[.], table(s), equation(s), eqn(s)[.], eq(s)[.], section(s), sec(s)., chapter(s), ch(s)., page(s), p., pp., pg., example(s), problem(s), prob., exercise(s); the abbreviations and the last four by FIXES-1 FIX-6 d) then a number, parenthesised ("Eq. (1.5)", "Eq. (A.3)") or bare ("Fig. 2.3", "p. 12", "Example 2.6", "Sec. 4.3", "pg. 112"), which may end in one letter when no word character and no operator follows it (FIXES-1 FIX-8 f: "Figure 3a", "Eq. 2.4b"), but not running on into a further letter, digit, `_`, `=`, `+`, `*`, `/` or `^` (so "the equation 2x = 4" and "Figure 3ab" do not fire). "Griffiths (2.17)" does not fire (a named source with a bare number: a known miss). A named source ("according to Griffiths") and "the equation of motion" do not fire; "the section of a cone" and "a force on the book" do not either.
  - `undated` (qa: question and answer; cloze: the body), "“<match>” will age — add a date (for example “as of <year>”)": one of `UNDATED_WORDS` (most recently, most recent, currently, nowadays, at present, latest, newest, recently, so far, to date, this year, last year, state of the art, state-of-the-art, as of now; whole words, any case, any blanks between the words), narrowed by FIXES-1 FIX-6 a: "latest", "newest", "most recent" and "most recently" fire only directly before a word of `UNDATED_NOUNS` (measurement(s), value(s), result(s), data, experiment(s), record(s), model(s), estimate(s), version(s), edition(s), findings, limit(s), bound(s): "the latest measurement", not "the latest time" or "the most recent collision"), and "recently" right after "most" belongs to that phrase; "so far" does not fire before from, away, as, that, beyond or out (`SO_FAR_NOT_BEFORE`) nor after "in" ("in so far as"); and no year in the prompt's text (question + answer, or the body; the raw text, math included): `YEAR_RE`, a 4-digit number from 1500 to 2199 that is not part of a longer number or a decimal. The question is searched first, then the answer; <year> = `new Date(now).getFullYear()` (local). "2000 K" silences it (accepted).
- **Split** (D26, P2.2; js/margin.js, js/list.js, js/editor.js): "Split" comes right after "Edit" in the margin card menu (act "split") and in the prompt list's row menu, for Q./A. and cloze prompts only (the margin decides by `parse(p.text).kind`, the list by `p.kind`). Split opens the normal editor on the prompt (margin `openEditor({promptId, split: true})`, which remembers the stored text as `splitText`; the list `editRow(p, {split: true})`). Cancel there: nothing happens. Its save is the usual edit (`reconcileCards`, the dropped-card question first; "Keep editing" saves nothing and opens no second editor), and once it resolves a second editor opens for a NEW prompt at the same passage, filled with the text from before the edit (tags line included): in the margin `openEditor({promptId: null, anchor: <a JSON copy of the anchor>, initialText, note, draftKey})`, in the list a row of its own, `div.list__row.list__row--editing.list__row--split[data-split=<first id>]`, right after the first prompt's row (at the top when that row is not shown). Its save makes a new prompt as "save and write another" does (same collection, a copy of the anchor, context from `M.context.contextFor`, so a deck keeps its section; fresh cards, not flagged, never sent to Obsidian); a blank save in the list stores nothing. Cancel in the second editor: the first edit stays and no second prompt is made. While it waits, the second editor shows `div.editor__note` "Second half of the split — keep what the first prompt no longer asks" at its top (`M.editor.create({note})`, `SPLIT_NOTE` in margin.js and list.js); its draft key is "split:<first id>", so a stale draft of another new prompt at the same passage never replaces the old text. A save that fails reopens the editor with the user's text. Nothing is saved that the user did not save.
  - **The second half is never lost** (FIXES-1 FIX-1): right before the second editor opens after the first save, a stored "split:<id>" draft is removed (`M.editor.dropDraft`), so it always shows the text from before THIS edit. The second editor is opened with `keepDraft: true` (`M.editor.create`): its text is the draft "split:<id>" from the moment it opens (also untouched, also when it equals `initialText`) until its Save or Cancel, whatever else closes it (another editor opening, a route change, the list or the book closing, a reload); Save stores the second prompt and removes the draft, Cancel removes the draft. While such a draft exists for a live Q./A. or cloze prompt, the margin card menu (act "finishSplit") and the list's row menu show **"Finish split"** in the place of "Split": it opens the second editor directly (a new prompt at the same passage, or the same deck and section, with the note line and the draft's text); the first prompt is not edited again; an editor that is open closes first, so its latest text is what comes back. After Save or Cancel the menu shows "Split" again. Deleting the first prompt removes its draft (`M.editor` listens to `prompt:deleted`, best effort); a draft of a prompt that is gone or deleted is never offered (the menus only list live prompts). Every other editor keeps the old draft rule (a draft only when the text differs from `initialText`; a stored draft wins). `M.editor` extras: `keepDraft` (option and Editor field), `draftText(override)` → string|null, `dropDraft(override)` → boolean, `SPLIT_DRAFT_PREFIX` "split:".
- **Another angle** (D26, P2.3; js/editor.js, js/margin.js, css/editor.css): `M.editor.ANGLE_GROUPS` [{id, label}] and `M.editor.ANGLES` [{id, group, label, text}] (the one place; label = the question as the picker shows it, text = "Q. " + label). The list of 2026-11-11 has 28 starters in 10 groups, one group for each angle of the sources (the user found the first list of eight too narrow and approved this one): attributes "Attributes and tendencies": attributes "What is always true of …?", never "What is never true of …?", usually "How is … usually …?"; compare "Similarities and differences": similar "How is … similar to …?", difference "How is … different from …?"; parts "Parts and wholes": parts "What are the parts of …?", kind "… is a kind of what?", examples "Name three examples of …", instance "… is an example of what?"; causes "Causes and effects": cause "What causes …?", effect "What does … do?", used "When is … used?"; meaning "Significance and implications": matters "Why does … matter?", implies "What does … imply?", askmyself "What should I ask myself when …?"; explain "Explanation": why "Why …?", produce "How does … produce …?"; procedure "Procedure": when "When should you …?", after "What comes after …?", howlong "How long does … take?"; use "Use": ways "Name two ways to …", novel "Name a … you have not named before", do "What should I do when …?"; depth "Depth": whatif "What changes if …?", true "Why is … true?", connect "How does … connect to …?"; memory "Memory": term "What is the term for …?", mnemonic "Mnemonic for …?". The first five groups are Matuschak's lenses for a concept; Explanation, Procedure and Use are his other prompt kinds (Use holds his open-list, creative and behaviour prompts); Depth holds Nielsen's questions; Memory holds Wozniak's two directions and mnemonics. The eight starters of the first list keep their ids and sentences (attributes, difference, parts, cause, effect, why, matters, whatif). "Name three examples of …" trips the `example_prompt` hint on purpose (its advice is the starter "… is an example of what?"); no other starter trips a hint by its own wording (a test checks all 28); `ANGLE_ANSWER` "\nA. " is appended when one is used. The margin card menu has "Another angle…" (act "angle", every kind of card, right after Split): it opens the new-prompt editor at the same passage with "Q. " and the picker open (if a draft for that passage replaced "Q. ", the picker stays shut). The editor's quiet `button.btn.btn--ghost.editor__angles` "Angles" (in `.editor__bar` after `{}`) shows only for a new prompt outside the deck's batch box whose text is empty or just "Q." with blanks (`anglesShown(text, isNew, batch)`, checked on every input); it opens `div.editor__angles-pop[role=menu]` (`.editor` is `position: relative`) with one `div.editor__angle-set[role=group][aria-label=<group label>]` per group that has starters: first its heading `div.editor__angle-group[aria-hidden=true]` (sticky at the top of the scrolling picker, never focusable), then one `button.editor__angle[role=menuitem][data-angle=<id>]` per starter. Place (pure `M.editor.anglesPlace(editorRect, popSize, bounds)` → {up, right, maxHeight}; all in viewport px; `popSize` is the picker's natural size, measured once at opening with `max-height: none`, so a later resize works with the same size; `ANGLES_EDGE` 8 px stays free at the area's edge): below the editor when the whole picker fits there, else above it (class `editor__angles-pop--up`) when it fits there, else on the side with more room (below on a tie) with `max-height` set to that room, never under `ANGLES_MIN_HEIGHT` (120 px), and the picker scrolls inside itself; right-aligned to the editor (class `editor__angles-pop--right`) when its left-aligned box would pass the area's right edge and the right-aligned box stays inside. Area (pure `M.editor.anglesArea(editorRect, clips, win, stay)` → {bounds, escape}): `clips` are the boxes of the editor's ancestors that clip their content (computed `overflow-x` / `overflow-y` not `visible`), nearest first, each {top, bottom, left, right, x, y}; `win` is the window's box. The picker stays inside all of them (`escape` 0: an absolute child of the editor, it moves with it) when they leave it `ANGLES_MIN_HEIGHT` above or below the editor (`ANGLES_EDGE` taken off); the wide layout's `#scroller` does. Else it leaves the nearest boxes, as few as possible (`escape` > 0): the rail layout's card `.popover` and the stack layout's `.sheet__body` are just around their editor. It then gets class `editor__angles-pop--fixed` (`position: fixed`) and inline `top` or `bottom`, `left` or `right` at the editor's edge, inside the area of the boxes it did not leave (in the rail layout `#scroller`, so it stays under the toolbar); while it is fixed, a scroll of anything but the picker itself places it again on the next animation frame, so it follows the editor (a resize places a fixed and an absolute picker again). When the editor of a fixed picker has left the area (pure `M.editor.anglesOut(editorRect, bounds)`: no px of it inside, for example scrolled under the toolbar) the picker closes and the focus goes back to the textarea; `openAngles()` returns false then. An absolute picker is not closed: it leaves with its editor and is clipped with it. A fixed box is held by an ancestor with a transform: the picker measures itself after it is placed, and when it is not at the editor's edge it stays inside its boxes (`stay`, `escape` 0). Closing the picker removes its mousedown, scroll and resize listeners and cancels a waiting frame. A mousedown inside the picker that is not on a question (a heading, the gap between two groups, the padding) is `preventDefault`ed, so the focused question keeps the focus and the keys stay the picker's (otherwise Esc would reach M.app and cancel the editor). The picker has `overscroll-behavior: contain` (its scroll never moves the page), `scrollbar-gutter: stable` and `scroll-padding-top` (a focused question stays clear of the sticky heading). Without layout (the Node tests) the picker stays below, left-aligned, uncapped. (Found in a real browser on 2026-11-11: an editor low on the page cut the last starters off, and the rail layout's popover hid the list; the first rule, `anglesOpenUp`, only chose the side and is gone. Hosts: the margin's three (a card in the margin, the rail's popover, the stack's sheet) and the prompt list's Split row (js/list.js: the second editor of a Split is a new prompt, so its Angles button shows once its text is emptied to "Q. "; there the list's rows are the clipping box). The deck's batch box has no Angles button.) Its keys stay inside it and never reach M.app: ↓ / Tab next, ↑ / Shift+Tab previous (wrapping; they move over the 28 questions and skip the headings), Home, End, Enter or Space picks, Esc closes it and gives the focus back to the textarea (the editor stays open); a click picks, a mousedown outside closes it. A pick replaces the text with the starter + "\nA. " and selects its first "…" (`angleStarter(id)` → {text, start, end}). Editor API: `openAngles()` → boolean, `closeAngles()`, `anglesOpen()`. No key is added to the reader or the editor map (D20).
- **Look-alike prompts** (D26, P2.4; js/similar.js, pure, `M.similar`, loaded right after js/prompts.js, its one dependency):
  - `words(text)` → the comparison words of the asking side, each once in order of first appearance: a Q./A. prompt's question; a cloze's whole body with each deletion read as its hidden part (braces and cues removed); [] for notes and highlights. Text runs: lower case, apostrophes (' ’ ʼ) dropped, any other character that is not a letter, digit or combining mark reads as a blank. Each math run is one word, "$" + its source without blanks, case kept (E and e are different symbols). Images are ignored. No stemming. `STOP_WORDS` are dropped: articles (a, an, the); forms of be, do and have (be, am, is, are, was, were, been, being, isnt, arent, wasnt, werent, do, does, did, done, doing, dont, doesnt, didnt, have, has, had, having, havent, hasnt, hadnt); prepositions (about, above, across, after, against, along, among, around, as, at, before, behind, below, beneath, beside, besides, between, beyond, by, despite, down, during, except, for, from, in, inside, into, near, of, off, on, onto, out, outside, over, past, per, since, through, throughout, till, to, toward, towards, under, underneath, until, up, upon, via, with, within, without); question words (what, which, who, whom, whose, when, where, why, how, whats, whys, hows); and (FIXES-1 FIX-7 a) conjunctions, pronouns, modal verbs and a few adverbs (and, or, but, nor, not, it, its, this, that, these, those, than, then, so, if, can, will, must, may, would, should, could, we, you, they, their, there, here, also, only).
  - `score(a, b)` → |A∩B| / |A∪B| over the two word sets (0 when either is empty); `alike(a, b)` → score ≥ `MIN_SCORE` (0.6) and |A∩B| ≥ `MIN_SHARED` (3); a and b are prompt texts or word lists. `key(idA, idB)` → the two ids sorted and joined with "|".
  - `pairs(prompts, ignored)` → [{a, b, score}] (a < b) for the live (deletedAt null or absent) Q./A. and cloze prompts given, every unordered pair that is alike and whose key is not in `ignored` (an array or a Set), best score first, then by key. Two cloze prompts whose bodies are equal once braces and cues are removed (blanks collapsed) and whose sets of hidden parts differ are skipped (FIXES-1 FIX-7 b: the same sentence with different blanks asks different things, as the two halves of a cloze split do); the same sentence with the same blanks is still a pair (a duplicate); `score` and `alike` themselves still compare words only. It indexes the prompts by word and compares only prompts that share a word (2,000 prompts: about 50 to 90 ms on a laptop). It reports at most `MAX_PAIRS` (5,000) pairs and stops searching there: a collection made from one template ("What is the capital of X?" two thousand times) has a pair for every two prompts, which froze the list and filled the memory (review of 2026-11-11, safety F1); such a collection gets the first 5,000 pairs found. `nearest(text, prompts, exceptId, ignored)` → the most alike such prompt for `text` (alike as above, with the same cloze rule; ties: the higher score, then more shared words, then the earlier prompt) or null; never the prompt `exceptId`, and never one whose key with `exceptId` is in `ignored` (a new prompt, exceptId null, has no pair key). `oneLine(text, max)` → the asking side as one plain line of at most `ONE_LINE_CHARS` (90) characters, cut with "…" (math as typed, images "[image]", a cloze's braces and cues removed with no blank added where a blank stood, so "{density}." reads "density."; `\::` outside math reads "::": FIXES-1 FIX-4). words() remembers the words of the last 10,000 texts (a cache keyed by the text).
  - **`settings.notAlike`** (js/settings.js): an array of pair keys, default []. Entries that are not strings, are empty or longer than `NOT_ALIKE_KEY_MAX` (200) characters are dropped, and so are repeats (the first stays); of more than `NOT_ALIKE_MAX` (5,000) keys the last 5,000 stay (a mark is appended, so the oldest marks go first). A value that is not an array is refused (`set` → false; a stored one loads as []). It is one of the exporter's `SETTINGS_KEYS`, so a JSON backup carries it and "Also restore settings" replaces the library's list with the backup's (the import dialog's box now ends "…, Obsidian vault and folder, “Not alike” marks)").
  - **The prompt list** (js/list.js, css/list.css): the third chip `button.chip.list__chip[data-chip=alike]` "Look-alikes n", after P1's two: n = the live prompts of the list that are in at least one pair of `M.similar.pairs(the list's live prompts, settings.notAlike)`. It follows the chip rules above (shown while n > 0 or on; AND with the search and the other chips). The pairs are computed in `renderRows` by `ensureAlike` only when the list opens, when a prompt's text or the set of live prompts changed since the last computation (it compares each prompt's text with the texts it last used), or when `settings:changed` brings another `notAlike`; a search key stroke (the debounced search, `search(q)`) never computes them (test: M.similar.pairs calls counted). A save that changes a prompt's text computes them again in `updateRow`; the one-row update still applies when the pairs stay the same. While the chip is on, the rows are grouped: each group of prompts connected by pairs starts where its earliest member stands in the list's order and lists its members breadth first from there (partners in the list's order), so the two members of a pair stand together; each row has `div.list__alike` "≈ <the most alike partner's question on one line>" (`M.similar.oneLine`), plus " (+N more)" when the row has further partners; the row menu gains "Not alike" right after Split (only while the chip is on and the row is in a pair), which appends the keys of all the row's pairs to `settings.notAlike`; the rows left without a pair leave the filter and the selection goes to the neighbouring row (the next one still shown, else the one before). If the setting cannot be saved (a tab that stepped aside) a toast says "Could not save “Not alike”". Every save while the chip is on re-renders the list. Extension: `M.list.alikePairs()` → [{a, b, score}].
  - **The editor** (js/editor.js, css/editor.css): in single mode, `div.editor__similar` sits right under `.editor__lint`. While the text parses as Q./A. or cloze and the editor knows its collection (`create({collectionId})`; else the edited prompt's `collectionId`; the margin passes the open book's, the list's split row the first prompt's), it reads "Similar prompt: “<question, one line>” (p. N)" (`SIMILAR_LABEL`, `M.editor.similarLine`) for `M.similar.nearest(text, M.cache.promptsOf(collectionId), <the edited prompt's id or null>, settings.notAlike)`; " (p. N)" only for a prompt of a book with a page (its context's page label, else its anchor's or context's page index + 1). Otherwise, and for notes, highlights and the deck's batch box (which has no such line), it is hidden (`.is-hidden`). It is computed with the preview and the lints (their 120 ms debounce), inside a try, so it never blocks or delays a save. CSS: quiet text (12/16, `--ink-2`); `.editor__similar` may wrap in a narrow margin card (its question is already one line of at most 90 characters), `.list__alike` stays on one line, cut with an ellipsis.
  - **Per collection**: look-alikes are found within one book or one deck. Pairs across books or decks are a known limit.
- **Tests**: test/cues.test.js and test/cues-golden.json, test/hints.test.js (P1.1, P1.2); test/review-keys.test.js (actions, presets, a legacy stored map, collisions, settings.js agreeing with keys.js, presetOf, loading a legacy map from localStorage); test/review-craft.test.js (flag, delete in the middle, on the last and only card and of a cloze prompt with two queued cards, undo exact, delete then grade then the toast's Undo, a refused delete, the summary's deleted and flagged); test/struggle.test.js (missDays vectors, since, promptMissDays); appended tests in test/app.test.js (F and Backspace dispatch; the `?` sheet and F / Backspace / U on the real cache), test/review-dom.test.js (buttons, ⚑ Flagged, the toast, the end screen lists and summary, the struggling line before / after reveal / after an edit or a flag, one miss day), test/list-dom.test.js (chips, counts, AND, live updates, the struggling mark), test/library-dom.test.js (the settings rows), and one each in test/reader-dom.test.js, test/editor.test.js and test/margin-dom.test.js (P1.1). D26: test/hints-p2.test.js (the three hints, positives and negatives); test/split-angles.test.js (on a copy of margin-dom's fake DOM with the real editor, margin and list: ANGLES and ANGLE_GROUPS (the whole list pinned), the starters against the writing hints, anglesPlace, anglesArea and anglesOut vectors, the Angles button and the picker with its headings, the picker's place with a given layout (its natural size, fixed outside a low box, placed again on a scroll or a resize with one frame, closed when the editor leaves the area, held by a transform, listeners removed and the frame cancelled), a press on a heading, the menus, Split save / cancel in either editor, a cloze that drops a blank, a stale draft, a deck's and a book's list rows, no key map change); test/similar.test.js (words / score / alike vectors with stop words, math, cloze braces and cues, both thresholds at their edges; pairs with ignored keys; nearest; a mark that outlives a change; 2,000 prompts; settings.notAlike sanitising with 5,001 keys, non-strings and overlong keys; the backup round trip with restoreSettings); test/lookalikes-dom.test.js (on the same fake DOM: the chip and its count, the grouping and the "≈" line, "Not alike" and the neighbour's focus, a restored notAlike, M.similar.pairs calls counted over quick search typing, a flag, an edit, a delete and a mark; a deck; the editor's line shown with its page, never the edited prompt, hidden for notAlike and notes, no page in a deck, none in the batch box). test/smoke.test.js now expects 28 modules with js/similar.js right after js/prompts.js. FIXES-1: test/hints-fixes.test.js (FIX-3, FIX-6, FIX-8 c and f), test/revised.test.js (FIX-5 on the real cache and exporter), and appended tests in test/split-angles.test.js (FIX-1 margin and list, FIX-7 c), test/list-dom.test.js (FIX-2), test/similar.test.js (FIX-4, FIX-7, FIX-8 d), test/review-craft.test.js (FIX-8 a), test/lookalikes-dom.test.js (FIX-8 b). Existing tests changed by D26: test/margin-dom.test.js (two: the card menu's order) and test/list-dom.test.js (one: the row menu's labels gain Split), test/smoke.test.js (27 → 28).
- **Known limits** (D26): look-alikes are per book or deck, never across two; a "Not alike" mark belongs to the two prompt ids and stays when either prompt changes later; marks of deleted prompts stay in `notAlike` until the 5,000 newest push them out; no stemming ("electron" and "electrons" are two words); two cloze prompts of one sentence with different blanks are no look-alikes (FIXES-1 FIX-7 b), even when one of them is a near copy that only moved a blank; the editor's line looks only at the prompts saved in the cache when the debounce fires. The book_bound and undated hints read single words: "the section of a cone" no longer fires, but other everyday uses of the BOOK_NOUNS after "this" do ("this text message"); "2000 K" silences undated.
- **Version**: desktop/package.json (and the root package in package-lock.json) was 1.1.1 with the starter list of 28 (1.1.0 was the two prompt-craft sets with the first list of eight; 1.0.0 the app before them). It was 1.2.0 with D27 ("Scheduling and statistics" below) was 1.3.0 with D28 and D29 ("Wrong answers and the next day" below) was 1.4.0 with D30 ("Tabs" below), 1.5.0 with D31 ("Windows" below), 1.5.1 since the user's first wishes for the windows (a tab that is dragged down, "Windows" below) was 1.6.0 with D32 (the Obsidian switch, "Obsidian" above, and the project made ready for other people) and is 1.7.0 since D33 (undo and redo for cards, "Undo and redo" below), so `marginalia app` ("Installed Marginalia 1.7.0") and `marginalia status` show that the update is in.
- **Repairs of 2026-11-11** (FIXES-1, after the independent review of FEATURES-P1 and P2; tests in test/split-angles.test.js, test/list-dom.test.js, test/revised.test.js, test/hints-fixes.test.js, test/similar.test.js, test/review-craft.test.js, test/lookalikes-dom.test.js):
  - FIX-1 Split: the second half is kept as the draft "split:<id>" from the moment its editor opens until its Save or Cancel; a stale one is dropped first; "Finish split" brings it back (above).
  - FIX-2 Prompt list: on a filter chip, Backspace, Delete, F and E do nothing to the selected row.
  - FIX-3 either_or before yes_no: an answer that is one of the offered choices gives either_or, also after a yes/no start word.
  - FIX-4 The "≈" line and the editor's "Similar prompt" line: no blank before punctuation where a blank stood; `\::` reads "::".
  - FIX-5 Struggling counts from `revisedAt`, the last change of the prompt's text (a new prompt field); a flag, tags, a move, a delete and its undo no longer restart it. D25 says "since the prompt's text last changed".
  - FIX-6 Hint precision: undated (so far, latest / newest / most recent(ly) + `UNDATED_NOUNS`), list_answer (ii) needs and / or, two semicolons, short parts or four parts, (i) formula lines count beside item lines, book_bound (new labels and references, no above-/cross section below), yes_no ("No, never"), example_prompt ("the" is no break), multi_sentence_cloze (`SENTENCE_ABBREVS`: e.g., i.e., cf., vs., etc., Eq., Eqs., Fig., Figs., Sec., Ch., Dr., Prof. and No. end no sentence).
  - FIX-7 Look-alikes: more stop words; two clozes of one sentence with different blanks are no pair.
  - FIX-8 Tests for rules that had none (a deleted prompt's dropped card is not skipped; "Not alike" with two partners; one example per trigger; `oneLine` at 90 and 91 characters); SPEC §6 names Split / Finish split and Another angle…; a numbered label takes one trailing letter ("Figure 3a"); the SELF-CHECK of js/prompts.js says lint(parsed, now).
  - Existing tests changed by FIXES-1 (each where the repair changes what it pins): test/hints.test.js (yes_no: "Yes, because …" is now yes_no; either_or: "Is light a wave or a particle?" / "a wave" and "Should we use a wave or a particle here?" / "a particle" are now either_or; list (i): three formula lines and "all hold" are a list of 4; list (ii): "gravity, the weak force, the strong force, electromagnetism" is no list); test/hints-p2.test.js (undated: "Which model is newest?" and "Who most recently won?" became "the newest model" and "the most recently data"; "What is the latest?" no longer fires, "the latest value?" does); test/similar.test.js ("and" is a stop word now); test/struggle.test.js (promptMissDays reads revisedAt); test/review-dom.test.js (an edit sets revisedAt; a flag keeps the struggling line); test/list-dom.test.js (a flag keeps "Struggling 1"); test/lookalikes-dom.test.js (the second Aare cloze is another sentence).
- **Known limits**: after an even number of backslashes a brace is a brace for parse and a literal for render (`x \\{y}`: parse makes a card, render shows no blank); cues did not change that, and toObsidian follows parse about cues there. In an Anki deletion without a cue, a `::` inside math or nested braces is written as before (Anki misreads it as before).

## Scheduling and statistics (binding; added 2026-11-14)
- **Source (D27, FEATURES-P3)**: on 2026-11-13 the user asked Claude to make sure the scheduler follows the eight articles (Matuschak's "How to write good prompts" and its further reading: Wozniak twice, Nielsen twice, Bjornstad, Case, Matuschak and Nielsen) and works correctly. The audit: js/scheduler.js equals Orbit's source rule for rule (growth 2.3, first interval 5 days, a wrong answer one step back and due in 10 minutes, the 16-hour lookahead, the jitter; Orbit's floating point differs from the exact integer arithmetic here by at most 1 ms); all of the user's 46 stored schedules and 78 answers equalled the rules replayed over their history; the queue never left a due card out. Two differences from the articles and one missing view were found; the user chose "All three". No data-format change: `DB_VERSION` and `SCHEDULER_VERSION` stay 1, no stored field is added, nothing is migrated, and `M.scheduler.grade` is unchanged. (D28 changed `grade` later and added the card field `growth`: "Wrong answers and the next day" below.)
- **A card never asked is due at once** (P3.2; every source asks a new card on its first day: Quantum Country in the text, Case's Level 1, Anki after 1 and 10 minutes; Orbit creates a task due at creation): `newSchedule(now)` = {createdAt: now, lastReviewedAt: null, intervalMs: FIRST, dueAt: now, log: []} (before: dueAt now + FIRST). `neverAsked(s)`: lastReviewedAt null or undefined. `dueTime(s)`: createdAt while never asked (when it is a number), else dueAt, null without that number. `isDue(s, now)`: true for a card never asked with a createdAt, else `dueAt <= now + LOOKAHEAD` as before (since D29 also: its own answer is 16 hours old). So a card made before D27 and never asked (it stores dueAt = createdAt + 5 d) is due at once too; its stored dueAt stays as it is (`prompt.dueAt` and the byDue index have no reader that decides dueness; `M.cache.counts().nextDueAt` is shown only when nothing is due; since D29 it is the earliest `readyTime`). A skip is no answer: the card stays due. `grade` gives the first answer what Orbit gives it from interval 0: right on day 0 → 5 d; right after 3 d → 6.9 d; right after 5 d → 11.5 d; wrong → 5 d kept, due in 10 minutes (since D28: 1 day, due the next day). `replay(createdAt, [])` equals `newSchedule(createdAt)`, so the import merge keeps the rule. "Review today's" (T, scope "today") takes the same prompts as before (those written today, due or not); like every session it now asks one card of each. The prompt list's row text uses `dueTime` ("new · due now"); `M.review`'s next due time uses it too (both since D29: `readyTime`). A card never asked whose createdAt lies ahead (a clock set back) is due all the same: the row text and the review's next due time never show a later time for it than now.
- **One card per prompt in a session** (P3.1; the guide: one blank per session, so that one blank never gives another away; Orbit: one component per task per queue): in `M.scheduler.buildQueue` each prompt gives at most one card. Among its candidate cards (scopes book, deck, all and so folder: its due cards; scope today: all its cards) it was the card with the smallest `dueTime` (Orbit's rule, the earliest due time), a tie going to the lowest card index, and the other cards stayed due. Changed by D28 and D29 ("Wrong answers and the next day" below): the candidates are the cards that can be asked now (`isReady`), the pick is the card most overdue for its wait, and the other cards wait until the next day. The other cards are not written to. The sort keys are as before; scope "all" sorts by `dayKey(dueTime(card))`. `buildQueue` no longer calls `spread()` (a queue holds no siblings); `spread` stays exported and tested. Scope "today" walked through a sentence's blanks one session at a time; since D29 it asks the same blank again, because the other blanks wait until the next day.
- **The session** (js/review.js; the retry, `held`, `nextDueAt` and the end screen's words were changed by D28 and D29: the present rules are in "Wrong answers and the next day" below, and this bullet is the state of D27): the retry tail is unchanged (the same card returns, up to `MAX_ASKS`). The summary gains `held`: the cards that are due when the session ends (`isDue`), belong to a prompt that had an item in the session and is still in the session's scope (a prompt deleted in the session does not count, also when the toast's Undo brought it back; nor does one moved to another book or deck meanwhile), and were not items of it. `nextDueAt` is the smallest `dueTime` in the scope, without the still-shaky and the held cards. End screen: the title is "Done for now" while `held > 0` or another card of the scope is due now (a prompt written during the session is due at once), else "All caught up"; the line gains, after the still-shaky part, "1 more blank waits for your next session" / "N more blanks wait for your next session", and then ends "nothing else due until <day>" (without held blanks: "nothing due until <day>"). Counts stay counts of cards: "Review all · 30 due" may start a session of 17.
- **The statistics page** (P3.3; js/stats.js `M.stats`, css/stats.css, host `#stats`; loaded after js/review.js and before js/library.js): `M.app.route("stats")` ↔ `#stats`; the library's header has `button.btn.btn--ghost.lib__stats[data-focus=stats]` "Statistics" before the gear. Pure: `rows(prompts, collections, now)` gives one row per live Q./A. or cloze prompt of a live collection that has a card: {id, collectionId, book (the collection's name, else "Untitled" / "Untitled deck"), kind, text (`M.similar.oneLine`, 110 characters), source (the asking side as typed: the question, or the cloze sentence with its braces), flagged, createdAt, cards, neverAsked (cards), due (cards; since D29 `isReady`), lastAskedAt (the latest answer of any card, or null), nextDueAt (since D29 the smallest `readyTime`), intervalMs (`waitOf` the card with that time; null while that card was never asked), growth (D28: that card's growth factor in thousandths, null while it was never asked), answers, wrong, missDays, struggling}; `totals(rows)`; `forecast(prompts, collections, now, days = FORECAST_DAYS 30)` → {days: [{at (a local midnight), dayKey, count}], later, max, total}, where day 0 holds every card that is due now (since D29 `isReady`: never asked, overdue, or inside the lookahead, and not held back by an answer) and every card due later today (every other card is in the column of its `readyTime`), and a card after the last day counts in `later`; `sortRows(rows, key, dir)` (keys = `COLUMNS`: text, book, cards, lastAskedAt, nextDueAt, intervalMs, answers, wrong; text and book without letter case; a missing value first ascending and last descending; ties by nextDueAt, then createdAt, then the given order; an unknown key sorts by nextDueAt); `filterRows(rows, show)` with `SHOWS` "" All prompts, due (a card due), never (a card never asked), flagged, struggling.
