Words: `dayLabel` ("today", "yesterday", "tomorrow", "12 Nov", "12 Nov 2025"), `fullTime` ("Sat 14 Nov 2026, 10:06"), `lastAskedText` ("never"), `nextDueText` ("now" while a card is due), `waitText` (`M.util.fmtInterval`, "—" without a wait), `totalsText` ("4 prompts · 6 cards · 3 due now · 2 never asked · 8 answers · 4 wrong (50%)"), `slotLabel`, `forecastSummary` (the chart's `aria-label`: the days that have cards, the first 8 named), `labelled` (the columns that carry their number: today's and the highest).
- **The page's DOM**: `.stats` > `.stats__header` ("‹ Library" `button.stats__back`, `h1` "Statistics"); `.stats__bar` with two `select.stats__select` ("Book or deck": "All books and decks (n)" and each book or deck that has a row, by name; "Show": the five choices, each with the number of rows it keeps in the chosen book) and `p.stats__totals`; `section.stats__due` "Coming due": `.stats__chart[role=img][aria-label]` with `.stats__plot` (30 `.stats__slot[data-i]`, in each a `span.stats__col` whose height is its share of the highest day, and a `span.stats__val` on the labelled columns), `.stats__axis` (a date under every seventh day, "today" first; every second one hidden under 520 px), `.stats__tip` (the hover line "N cards · <day>", shown while the pointer is over a slot), `p.stats__later` ("Later than these 30 days: N cards") and `details.stats__list` "Show as a list" (the same days as text: the chart's values are reachable without the pointer); `section.stats__prompts` with `table.stats__table` in `.stats__scroll` (the only part that scrolls sideways): `th[aria-sort]` > `button.stats__sort[data-sort]` (a click sorts by the column in its first direction: text, book, last asked, next due and wait ascending, the three numbers descending; a second click turns it round), rows `tr.stats__row[data-id]` (class `stats__row--due` while a card is due) with `button.stats__open` > `span.stats__q`, ⚑ `span.stats__flag` and `span.stats__chip` "struggling". An empty library reads "No prompts to review yet"; a filter that keeps nothing reads "No prompt matches." The two filters choose prompts, and the totals, the chart and the table then show those prompts with all their cards ("Due now" keeps a sentence with one blank due, and its other blanks count too). The page keeps its filters and its sort while the app runs (`reset()` goes back to the first view). A redraw keeps the keyboard focus (`data-focus`), an open "Show as a list" and the table's sideways scroll. When the page opens, its heading (`h1.stats__title[tabindex=-1]`) takes the focus. Esc on a filter goes back to the library by the page's own listener (M.app routes no key typed in a form field). The hover line is centred over its column and kept inside the plot by its measured width; after a tap it goes by itself after 2.5 s.
- **Text and HTML on the page**: every text is `textContent`, except a prompt's question: `span.stats__q` holds `M.markup.render(source, {mask: null, mode: "list", imageUrl})`, as the prompt list draws it (formulas, a sentence's hidden parts marked, images small), clamped to two lines; rendered questions are kept between redraws by their source (after a draw the entries it did not use go, oldest first, down to 4,000; a draw never drops what it needs itself); a question whose image is still loading (`att--missing`) is not kept, and `attachment:ready` redraws the page. Without `M.markup` the row's `text` is shown.
- **Behaviour**: a click on a prompt calls `M.app.route("reader", {collectionId, promptId})` (a deck's prompt opens its deck page). While open the page redraws 150 ms after `prompt:saved`, `prompt:deleted`, `prompt:restored`, `prompts:recontexted`, `collection:changed`, `collection:deleted`, `attachment:ready` or `review:ended` (one redraw for a burst), and puts the keyboard focus back on the element with the same `data-focus`. While a review runs over the page (`M.review.isActive()`) it waits, and redraws once on `review:ended`. A deck's prompt opens its deck page (the route carries no prompt there; the deck's list has the Flagged chip). The desktop app's link check knows the route (`desktop/lib/hash.js` `validHash("#stats")`). `close()` removes the listeners and empties `#stats`. The page reads `M.cache` and writes nothing. No key was added to a key map (D20); Esc on the page is `M.app`'s escape chain routing to the library.
- **The chart** follows the chart method Claude used: one series, one colour (`--stats-bar`: #e04a2f on the light surface, #e8553a on the dark one, each checked for its lightness band and for 3:1 against its surface), columns at most 24 px wide with rounded tops on one baseline and a 2 px gap, numbers only on today's column and the highest one, text in the ink tokens, a hover line on the whole slot, and the same values as a list.
- **Choices Claude made** (each a named constant or one rule; the user can change them): `FORECAST_DAYS` 30, the columns and their first sort direction, the five "Show" choices, the wording of the end line and of "Done for now", the tie rule of one card per prompt (Orbit's earliest due time; since D28 the card most overdue for its wait).
- **Kept as they are, on purpose**: no cap on a session (D9; Orbit caps a session at 50 cards), a skip is not graded (Orbit grades a skip as remembered), no card retires (Case's box retires a card after its top level), and a wrong answer steps one level back (Quantum Country and Orbit; Anki and Case restart from the beginning, which Nielsen calls too harsh). The last of these was changed by D28: after a wrong answer the wait is 1 day, and the growth is slower.
- **Tests**: test/scheduler.test.js (the changed vector "new → due at once", one card per prompt in every scope, a card never asked in old and new storage, the first-answer intervals); test/d27.test.js (vm pages with the real store, cache and review: a new prompt in the counts and the queues, an old never-asked card, a skip, a sentence with three blanks over three sessions with `held`, a wrong blank and its sibling, today's scope, the import replay); test/review-dom.test.js (the end screen's title and line); test/review-craft.test.js (remove and undo with one card per prompt, a prompt deleted on its retry, FIX-8 a in the form that is left); test/list.test.js (`dueText`); test/stats.test.js (the pure figures and words, and the page on a small fake DOM: what it draws, sorting, the two filters, the hover line and the list, opening a prompt, the debounced redraw with focus kept, close, and prompt text escaped or as text); test/app.test.js (the "stats" route, its hash, Esc); test/smoke.test.js, tools/xref.py, tools/xref-rt.js and tools/smoke.js know the 29th module. Fixtures in older tests that meant "not due" by a card never asked now give the card a lastReviewedAt (test/review.test.js, test/review-owner.test.js, test/folders.test.js, test/folders-dom.test.js).
- **Known limits**: the statistics page draws every row at once (a library of some thousand prompts takes a moment on the first draw; the rendered questions are kept for the next ones). A session started right after another one took the held blanks: the rule was per session, as in the guide and in Orbit, not per day (repaired by D29: the held blanks wait until the next day). A held blank is graded from the time its prompt was made, as Orbit grades a component never reviewed: with sessions a week apart the third blank of a sentence gets 32 days for its first right answer (2.3 × 14 days), although the sentence was last shown a week before. With daily sessions the 5-day floor decides and the limit does not show.
- **Research note: after a wrong answer** (2026-11-14; written for a decision the user then made: D28, the slower growth of the last source below): the rule here was Orbit's shipped rule, one step back (÷ 2.3, at least 5 days). No controlled experiment compares it with a restart. Three data sources point the same way, to a short wait after a lapse whatever the wait was before: SuperMemo (the post-lapse interval is "usually" 1–4 days and depends on the number of lapses, not on the earlier interval; Wozniak calls halving intervals harmful); FSRS, fitted to about 10,000 Anki users and 727 million reviews (FSRS-6 defaults give about 1 day after a lapse at a 5-day stability and about 6 days at 322 days, where ÷ 2.3 gives 2.2 and 140 days); and Matuschak's own working notes, which call his step-back rule "pretty clearly a mistake" on his Orbit data, say a forgotten prompt should probably be reviewed the next day, and sketch a slower growth factor after lapses (× 0.75 per failure, recovering 20 % per success; not implemented in Orbit). Against a full reset to zero stand relearning savings (Ebbinghaus) and Bjork's storage strength: a forgotten item is not new. Sources: supermemo.guru "Post-lapse stability"; the FSRS wiki "The Algorithm" and srs-benchmark; notes.andymatuschak.org "Forgotten spaced repetition prompts should probably be reviewed the next day" and "… should probably have smaller future spacing intervals".

## Wrong answers and the next day (binding; added 2026-11-14)
- **Source (D28, D29, FEATURES-P4)**: (1) on 2026-11-14 the user asked what the research says about the wait after a wrong answer ("Research note: after a wrong answer" above), got three options (keep one step back; the next day and then the usual growth; Matuschak's sketch) and chose "Option 2", the sketch: the next day, and slower growth for prompts that were forgotten. (2) The same day the user said: "I will review all cards and it will immediately say that some are due, shouldn't they only be due the next day?" With 1.2.0 the other blanks of a sentence (D27) and a card answered wrong three times were due again at once, so a second session could ask them minutes later. Both wait until the next day now. A card that was never answered wrong is graded exactly as before, to the millisecond (test/d28.test.js compares 1,500 random histories with the old function; a reviewer's own run compared about a million).
- **Constants** (`M.scheduler`): `LAPSE` = 1 day (the wait after a wrong answer); `NEXT_DAY` = 16 hours (two answers less than 16 hours apart are on the same day; the mirror of `LOOKAHEAD`, 16 hours, by which a card that comes due within 16 hours counts as due today); `GROWTH_FULL` = 2300 and `GROWTH_LEAST` = 1000 (the growth factor in thousandths; 2300 is `GROWTH`, 2.3). `RETRY` (10 minutes) is gone.
- **"The next day" (D29)**: `isDue(s, now)`: true for a card never asked that has a createdAt (D27); else `dueAt <= now + LOOKAHEAD` and `now >= lastReviewedAt + NEXT_DAY`. So no card is due again within 16 hours of its own answer: a card answered wrong (dueAt = the answer + 1 day) is due from 16 hours after it, not from 8, and a card that 1.2.0 stored after a wrong answer (dueAt = the answer + 10 minutes) waits the 16 hours too. `heldUntil(p, i)`: `NEXT_DAY` after the latest answer to ANOTHER card of prompt p, 0 when no other card was answered: an answer to one blank holds the sentence's other blanks back (Anki's deck option "bury siblings until the next day"). `isReady(p, i, now)`: `isDue(card, now) && now >= heldUntil(p, i)`. `readyTime(p, i, now?)` is the time from which `isReady` holds: for a card never asked its createdAt (never later than `now` when `now` is given: `isDue` calls such a card due whatever its createdAt says, as after a clock was set back); for any other card `max(dueAt − LOOKAHEAD, lastReviewedAt + NEXT_DAY)`; for both not before `heldUntil(p, i)`; null for a card without those numbers. With `now` given, `isReady(p, i, now)` is true exactly when `readyTime(p, i, now) <= now` (tested on 3,000 random prompts). "16 hours" is not always the next calendar day: after a session before 8 in the morning the cards come back late that evening; every text names the real day.
- **One rule for "due" and for "when"**: every count of "due" uses `isReady`, and every text that says when a card comes uses `readyTime` with `now`, so the two never disagree: `M.cache.counts` and `countsBy` (`due` = the cards that can be asked now; `nextDueAt` = the earliest `readyTime` of a card, before: the earliest stored dueAt), `buildQueue`, the review's summary and end line, `M.stats` (rows, totals, forecast) and the prompt list's row text. Right after a session in which every due card was answered the home screen shows 0 due. (Before D29 the "when" texts used the stored due time without the lookahead: a card could read "due in 3 h" while the count called it due.)
- **Grading (D28)**, `grade(s, now, outcome)`: elapsed = max(0, now − (lastReviewedAt ?? createdAt)); g = `growthOf(s)`; wasDue = `isDue(s, now)`; iv = `waitOf(s)`: intervalMs (FIRST when it is no number), and at most LAPSE when the card's latest answer was wrong (a card that 1.2.0 stored after a wrong answer keeps a wait of 5 days or more in its record). **remembered**: least = (iv < FIRST and g < 2300) ? LAPSE : FIRST; grown = floor(elapsed · g / 1000) in exact integer arithmetic (floor(ms / 1000) · g + floor((ms mod 1000) · g / 1000); with g = 2300 it is the old floor(elapsed · 23 / 10)); interval = elapsed < iv ? max(iv, least, grown) : max(least, grown); growth = wasDue ? min(2300, g + ceil((2300 − g) / 5)) : g. **forgot**: interval = LAPSE; growth = g when the card has a wrong answer less than NEXT_DAY before now (the latest "forgot" entry of its log), else max(1000, round(g · 3 / 4)). Both: dueAt = now + jitter(now) + interval; the result has `growth`; the log entry keeps its four fields. A time that is no number counts as "no time has passed" (before: NaN).
- **In words**: a wrong answer sets the wait to 1 day, whatever it was, and leaves three quarters of the card's growth factor (2300 → 1725 → 1294 → 1000, never less). A wrong answer less than 16 hours after another wrong answer is the same lapse and does not count again, whatever came in between (the retries of a session; a right retry and then "Review today's" asking again). A wrong answer with no wrong answer in the 16 hours before it always counts, also when it comes early. A right answer to a card that was due wins back a fifth of what is missing (1725 → 1840 → 1932 → 2006 → …, rounded up, so the factor is 2300 again after 25 right answers); a retry in the session and any other answer before the card is due win nothing back and keep the wait. While a card comes back from a wrong answer (its wait is below 5 days and its factor below 2300) its least wait is 1 day, else 5 days as before. Waits after one wrong answer when every answer comes exactly on time: 1, 1.7, 3.2, 6.1, 12.3, 25 days (never forgotten: 5, 11.5, 26.5, 61). With one session a day the real times decide: the card is asked on days 1, 3, 7, 15, 31 and 64 (never forgotten: days 0, 5, 16, 41, 98). A card that is forgotten again and again stays at waits of 1 to 2 days: it comes almost every day until it is known or its prompt is rewritten (the "Forgotten on N days" line of D25 says so).
- **The sketch and what differs, on purpose**: Matuschak's note "Forgotten spaced repetition prompts should probably have smaller future spacing intervals" sketches the factor × 0.75 on a failure (at least 1), 0.2 · 2.3 + 0.8 · factor on a success, a wait of 1 day after a failure and wait × factor on a success. He did not build it into Orbit and says he is not sure about it. Three things differ here. (1) The sketch grows the planned wait; the app grows the real time since the last answer, as Orbit does and as it did before (so a late answer earns more, and a card never forgotten is unchanged). (2) The sketch counts every failure; the app counts one per 16 hours (three wrong answers in one session would else bring the factor to its least at once; Anki: failures while a card is being relearned do not lower its ease again). (3) The sketch recovers on every success; the app only on an answer to a due card (a right retry a minute after a wrong answer proves nothing).
- **Never stuck** (test/d28.test.js, for 1, 2 and 3 lapses and a session every 8, 12, 16, 20, 24, 36 and 48 hours; a reviewer's run: every starting factor, sessions every 1 to 72 hours and at random): a forgotten card is asked at most once per 16 hours and has a wait of 5 days again within 14 days and 11 right answers (13 with a session every hour); with one session a day after 3, 6 or 7 right answers (1, 2 or 3 lapses). Why the 16-hour rule is needed: with the 16-hour lookahead alone a card with a 1-day wait would be due 8 hours after its answer, and a reader with two sessions a day would answer it every 12 hours for ever, because 12 hours times the factor is less than the wait the card has.
- **Stored data**: a card (schedule) has one new field, `growth` (an integer from 1000 to 2300). `newSchedule` sets 2300 and every graded card carries it. No migration: `growthOf(s)` returns a valid stored value, else `replay(s.createdAt, the log's answers).growth` (2300 without an answer; log entries that are no answers are left out), so the wrong answers given before D28 count too. `waitOf(s)` is the wait the next answer is graded from (see Grading). Stored waits and due times are never rewritten; a card changes only at its next answer. `replay` gives the new rule's result, so an import merge whose logs differ recomputes the card by it (a merge whose union equals the local log keeps the local card: js/exporter.js `sameLog`). `M.prompts.reconcileCards` copies whole cards, so `growth` and the answer times survive an edit of the text. `DB_VERSION` and `SCHEDULER_VERSION` stay 1 (nothing reads `SCHEDULER_VERSION`); the JSON backup keeps its shape and carries `growth` inside each card. An older copy of the app (1.2.0) would ignore the field.
- **The queue** (`buildQueue`): a prompt's candidates are its cards with `isReady` (scopes book, deck, all, and so folder); in scope "today", which asks due or not, its cards that no other card holds back (`now >= heldUntil(p, i)`), so "Review today's" may ask the same blank again but never another blank of a sentence on the day of an answer. Of the candidates the queue takes the card that is most overdue for its wait: (now − its last answer, or − createdAt for a card never asked) / `waitOf(card)`; a tie goes to the shorter wait, then to the smallest `dueTime`, then to the lowest index. So a blank that was forgotten yesterday (a day of its 1-day wait: 1.0) comes before blanks never asked (a day of their 5 days: 0.2), and a blank that is passed over rises each day, so none is left out. The first form of this rule, "the shortest wait first", was replaced after a review: a blank that was forgotten on every third ask got 42 of 60 daily sessions and left the two other blanks 14 and 10 days overdue; one forgotten on every ask left the others never asked. With the present rule the other blanks are asked at most a day and a half after their due time, and with a blank forgotten on every ask the three blanks never asked come on days 5, 7 and 9 (test/d28.test.js; never wrong: days 1, 2, 3; 1.2.0 took the smallest `dueTime`).
- **The session** (js/review.js): the retry is the session's own business, because the scheduler no longer calls a card "due" after a wrong answer: at the end of a pass every item whose latest answer in this session was "forgot" is appended again, in original order, unless it was asked `MAX_ASKS` times, skipped away or deleted; an item that was never answered in the session (its prompt was away when its turn came) is appended while its card `isReady`. The summary: `stillShaky` = the graded items whose latest answer in the session was "forgot", and `shakyDueAt` the earliest `readyTime` of their cards (null without one); `held` = the cards of the session's prompts (in scope, not items, not deleted) that are due by their own schedule (`isDue`) and held back now (`heldUntil > now`), and `heldUntil` the earliest `readyTime` of those (null without one); `dueNow` = the cards of the scope with `isReady` when the session ends (cards skipped away, prompts written meanwhile); `nextDueAt` = the earliest `readyTime` in the scope without the held cards. End screen: the title is "Done for now" only while `dueNow > 0`, else "All caught up"; the line reads "… · 2 still shaky (due again tomorrow) · 3 more blanks wait until tomorrow · nothing else due until Thursday" ("nothing due until …" without held blanks; "more due now" while `dueNow > 0`). Each day word comes from its own time: "later today" for a time on the same day (after a session at 7:30 all three read "later today"), else "tomorrow", a weekday or a date (`M.util.nextDayLabel`). The Forgot button's small text is "again this session" while the item has an ask left after this one, else "again in 1 d" (the new wait, as Remembered shows "next in <wait>").
- **Statistics page and prompt list**: `M.stats.rows`: `due` = the cards with `isReady`; `nextDueAt` = the smallest `readyTime(p, i, now)`; `intervalMs` = `waitOf` that card; new `growth` = `growthOf` that card (both null while it was never asked). The Wait cell reads "1 d · ×1.7" while the factor reads lower than 2.3 with one decimal (`growthText`, `waitText`), with the title "After a right answer this wait grows 1.7 times, not 2.3 times: the prompt was answered wrong" (`waitTitle`). `forecast`: a card with `isReady` is in today's column, every other card in the column of the day of its `readyTime`, the day from which a session can ask it (a card due tomorrow at 06:00 can be asked from 14:00 today and is in today's column). The prompt list's `dueText` uses `readyTime`: "due in 15 h" for a blank that waits for its sentence, "due in 4 d" right after an answer that gave 5 days.
- **Choices Claude made** (each one constant or one rule; the user can change them): `NEXT_DAY` 16 hours for "the next day" (not the calendar day: a session at 23:50 and one at 00:10 are the same day, and an evening answer comes back the next afternoon at the earliest); one lapse per 16 hours; recovery only on an answer to a due card; the card most overdue for its wait among a sentence's blanks; the hold also in "Review today's"; the lookahead inside every "when" text; the wording of the end line and of the Forgot button; the factor on the statistics page.
- **Two independent reviews (2026-11-14) and what was repaired**: (1) "the shortest wait first" left a sentence's other blanks out (see The queue); (2) `readyTime` had no lookahead, so "due in 8 h", "tomorrow" and the forecast disagreed with the due counts for 8 hours a day after every wrong answer; (3) "still shaky (due again tomorrow)" was a fixed text, false after a session before 8 in the morning; (4) a wrong answer after a right retry on the same day counted as a second lapse, against the rule in words; (5) a card never answered wrong with a stored wait below 5 days (hand-made data) got a least wait of 1 day; (6) a card never asked with a createdAt in the future showed a future time while it counted as due; (7) one bad log entry made `growthOf` forget every wrong answer; (8) the statistics page showed the stored wait of 5 days for a card that 1.2.0 stored after a wrong answer. Each has a test, and 20 mutations of the repaired code are caught by the suite.
- **Known limits**: (1) "Review today's" run twice asks the same blank of a sentence twice (its other blanks wait). (2) A blank that is forgotten on every ask keeps the first asks of its sentence's other blanks back (days 5, 7, 9 for three other blanks). (3) A skip is no answer: it holds nothing back, so after a card was skipped away another blank of its sentence can come in the next session; and an edit that takes the answered blank out of a sentence ends its hold on the other blanks. (4) The counts are counts of the cards that can be asked now, and a session takes one card per prompt: "Review all · 30 due" may still start a session of 17, and shows 0 after it. (5) A card whose answer time lies ahead (a clock set back) waits until 16 hours after that time. (6) A held blank is graded from the time its prompt was made (the limit of D27, unchanged). (7) A card that lived under the rule before D28 and was also answered while it was not due ("Review today's") can get another factor from a later import merge than the one it stores (the merge replays the whole log by the new rule; both are valid). (8) A card stored before D28 gets its factor from its log on every draw of the statistics page until its next answer (12,000 such cards with 20 answers each: about 0.2 s a draw; the user's library had 51 cards). (9) As before D29: "Review today's" from the home screen reads "more due now" while any card of the library is due, and the statistics page and the prompt list redraw on changes of the data, not when a wait ends by time alone (the home screen redraws every 60 s).
- **Tests**: test/d28.test.js (14: never forgotten = the old rule on 1,500 random histories; the exact integer growth against BigInt; the waits and factors after a wrong answer; one lapse per 16 hours; recovery only when due; the least wait; never stuck for seven session rhythms; `isDue` with the 16-hour rule; `heldUntil`, `readyTime`, `isReady` and their agreement on 3,000 random prompts; the queue's candidates and order in every scope; no blank left out over 60 days; `growthOf` on odd values; replay = step by step for 400 random histories; cards stored by 1.2.0); test/scheduler.test.js (the §7 vectors, the retry driven by the outcome, a sentence over three days); test/d27.test.js (vm pages: nothing due after a session, the other blanks one day at a time, a blank answered wrong goes first the next day, today's scope, a move to another book); test/cache.test.js (held blanks in `counts` and `countsBy`, `nextDueAt` to the millisecond, a card made "in the future"); test/review.test.js and test/review-dom.test.js (the summary, an item that was away, the end line at 0:30, 7:30, 8:30, 12:30 and 23:30, a skip that holds nothing, the button texts); test/stats.test.js (rows with a held blank, a lapsed card and a card stored by 1.2.0, `growth`, the Wait cell, the forecast's days); test/list.test.js (`dueText`); test/exporter.test.js (a merged sentence over two days). The fixtures that named `RETRY` use the number.

## Tabs (binding; added 2026-11-15)
- **Source (D30, FEATURES-T1)**: on 2026-11-14 the user asked: "also add tab functionality so I can open something in a new tab with command+click and can open a new tab with command t etc." Claude showed three designs (A: tabs that the web app draws inside its one page; B: one window per tab, as macOS window tabs; C: A now and B later). The user chose A ("Yes, build A") with the defaults Claude listed, and then added: "I want the ability of both A and the possibility of separate pages". This section is step 1, design A. Step 2, a tab in a window of its own, is "Windows" below (D31): two pages that both show the library hand the writer role to each other.
- **Words**: a TAB here is a place inside one page: `{id, screen, collectionId, position, promptId, shown, editing}`. `screen` is "library", "deck", "reader" or "stats" (M.app's screens). `position` = `{pageIndex, fraction, zoom}` of a book tab that is not on screen (null before it was left). `promptId` is set only for a tab that was opened on one prompt and not shown yet. `shown` = the place the tab was last shown with (used when the tab is left while its book still loads). `editing` = the prompt editor that was open when the tab left the screen (below). The ACTIVE tab is the one on screen. The "tab" of "One writing tab per origin" is a whole page; that rule is unchanged, test/tabs.test.js tests it, and test/d30.test.js tests `M.tabs`. The page still shows one screen and holds one open PDF: a tab that is not on screen holds only its place, so 20 tabs cost no more memory than one.
- **Module** (`js/tabs.js`, `M.tabs`; `css/tabs.css`; host `#tabs` right after `#banner`; loaded after js/library.js and before js/desktop.js, 30 namespaces then, 31 since D33): state `{tabs, active, closed}`. Constants: `TABS_MAX` 20, `CLOSED_MAX` 10, `STORE_KEY` "marginalia.tabs", `SAVE_MS` 200, `COMMANDS` (newTab, closeTab, reopenTab, nextTab, prevTab, tab1 … tab8, lastTab), `MENU_KEYS`. Pure (`_pure`, loads in Node): `place(route)` → `{screen, collectionId}` (the library and the statistics page have no collection; a book or deck without one is the library), `samePlace(a, b)`, `validPosition(p)`, `title(tab, collection)` ("Library", "Statistics", the collection's name, else "Untitled" / "Untitled deck"), `after(list, activeId, tab)`, `without(list, activeId, id)` → `{tabs, active}` (the right neighbour becomes active, else the left one), `step(list, activeId, delta)` (round the ends), `nth(list, n)` (1 to 8; 9 = the last tab), `clean(saved, find)` (stored JSON → a valid list: what is no tab is dropped, and so is a tab whose book or deck is gone; the collection's kind decides "reader" or "deck"; at most 20), `serialize(tabs, activeId)`.
- **API**: `start(route, explicit)` → the first route `{screen, collectionId, promptId, position}` (boot; it never routes by itself); `run(id)` → boolean (true when the command did something); `handleKey(ev)` → boolean; `wantsTab(ev)` → boolean; `go(ev, screen, params)` → boolean (for click handlers: a Cmd+click, Ctrl+click off the Mac, opens a tab in the background, with Shift an active one, and returns true; any other click calls `M.app.route(screen, params)` and returns false); `link(route)` → boolean; `open(place, {background})` → the new tab's id, or null (the tab goes right after the active one; with 20 tabs open nothing opens and a notice says "Close a tab first: 20 tabs are open"); `close(id)` → boolean (never the last tab); `select(id)` → boolean; `reopen()` → boolean; `list()` → `[{id, screen, collectionId, title, active}]`; `active()`; `started()`; `render()`; `save()`; test hook `_reset()`.
- **How a tab is shown**: `M.app.route()` emits `app:routed {screen, collectionId, promptId}` each time it settles on a screen, and the active tab takes that place. So "‹ Library", a book opened from the library, "Go to source" and a deleted book's way home all move the tab on screen. To show another tab, `M.tabs` first stores the leaving book tab's place: `M.reader.position()` → `{pageIndex, fraction, zoom}`|null (new; it reads `M.pdfview.currentPage()`, because the reader's own last position follows `scroll:settled`, a second late). Then it calls `M.app.route(screen, {collectionId, promptId, position, replace: true})`. `M.app` passes `position` to `M.reader.open(id, {position})` (new option): the book opens at that place and zoom instead of at the collection's `lastPosition`; when that book is on screen already (two tabs on one book) the reader moves and zooms in place. A tab's stored place and prompt are used once. `lastPosition` is still saved when the reader closes, so "Continue reading" follows the tab that was used last. A Cmd+click on "Continue reading" only adds a tab: nothing is written (no `lastOpenedAt`).
- **A tab's open editor**: when a book tab leaves the screen, its book closes, and the margin closes an open prompt editor with it; what the user typed stays the editor's draft (js/editor.js, as when "‹ Library" closes a book). `M.tabs` asks `M.margin.editing()` first (new: the options that open the open editor once more, `{promptId, anchor, initialText?, obsidian?, splitText?, note?, draftKey?, keepDraft?}`, or null) and keeps the answer in the tab (`editing`; memory only, also in a closed tab). The editor comes back when the tab is on screen AND its book is open: `restoreEditor()` runs when the route to the tab is done, on `doc:opened` and on `review:ended`, and does nothing until `M.reader.isOpen(collectionId)` (new) is true and nothing blocks. Then it calls `M.margin.openEditor(…)` once, and the editor opens with the draft. A route's promise alone is not enough: it can come before the book is open (the same book was loading already, or a newer open replaced this one). A tab that is left again before its book got open keeps its editor for the next time. Not when an editor is open already, and not for a tab that was asked to show one prompt (a link). When the next tab shows the SAME book, the reader keeps the book open and the editor stays on screen: the tab that left then keeps no editor, and a tab closed that way has none for Cmd+Shift+T (the user goes on with the editor, and may save it, in the other tab). Before a tab leaves, a text field that has the focus is blurred (`blurField`: INPUT, TEXTAREA, SELECT), so a name that is being changed takes its text while its screen is still there (the reader's rename field commits on blur only while the book is open). Not restored: the Angles picker, the editor of the prompt list's drawer (its text stays a draft), and nothing after a restart (the draft stays).
- **Commands, and what blocks them**: newTab (a library tab after the active one, active); closeTab (the active tab; false with one tab); reopenTab (the last closed tab that still has its book or deck, after the active tab, at its place; false with 20 tabs open, with the notice, and the closed tab is kept); nextTab and prevTab (false with one tab); tab1 … tab8 and lastTab (true also when that tab is on screen already; false when there is no such tab). No tab is shown, opened or closed while `M.app.modal()` is true (new: the ? sheet, a confirm question, Settings, the "other tab" notice, the cover, or a `.dialog` of another module, which is Export / Import or the "Move to folder…" picker) or while `M.review.isActive()`: `run`, `handleKey`, `link`, `wantsTab` and `go` (a Cmd+click is then a plain click), and also `select`, `close`, `open` and `reopen` themselves, which the strip's clicks and keys call. A link that comes then is left to M.app, which routes the page as before the tabs (the tab on screen takes that place). The strip is under the review overlay as well (z-index 35 against 40), so a review covers it. `collection:changed` redraws the strip (a rename). `collection:deleted` turns that collection's background tabs into library tabs and forgets its closed tabs; for the active tab M.app routes home and the tab follows. With 20 tabs open a link from outside has no room for a tab: the tab on screen takes it, with the notice "20 tabs are open: this tab took the link".
- **Keys** (`M.keys.TABS_MAP`, "Key routing"; inField true, so they work while a text field has the focus): `M.app.handleKeydown` asks `M.tabs.handleKey(ev)` after the lock, the ? sheet and a confirm question, and before the focus owner's map. Cmd+W with one tab is not handled (a browser closes its tab; the Mac app hides its window). A held key acts once (the repeats are swallowed). Cmd+Shift+] and Cmd+Shift+[ also match by the keys' place on the keyboard (`ev.code` BracketRight / BracketLeft with Shift and Cmd, Ctrl off the Mac; `bracketKey`), for an engine that reports the key as "]" with Shift instead of "}". No key of D20 or of another map changed: test/d30.test.js checks the tab keys against READER_MAP, EDITOR_MAP and every review preset. In a browser, the browser keeps Cmd+T, Cmd+W, Cmd+Shift+T and Ctrl+Tab (and often Cmd+1 … 9) for its own tabs, and the page never sees them: there the strip's "+" and "×", a click on a tab, and Cmd+click do the work.
- **In the Mac app** (`desktop/lib/menu.js`, `desktop/main.js`, `desktop/lib/lifecycle.js`): the Window menu has New Tab Cmd+T, Close Tab Cmd+W and Reopen Closed Tab Shift+Cmd+T, and Show Next Tab and Show Previous Tab without a shortcut (for a click). The page leaves exactly the three menu keys alone (`M.tabs.MENU_KEYS` = "Mod+t", "Mod+w", "Mod+Shift+t" while `M.desktop.isApp()`), so such a key runs once, whatever the order in which macOS offers it to the menu and to the page; the page only swallows a held one, so a held key acts once there too. Every other tab key is the page's in the app as well: Ctrl+Tab, Ctrl+Shift+Tab, Cmd+Shift+] and [, Cmd+1 … Cmd+9. They have no menu shortcut on purpose: a key without Cmd may never reach a menu on macOS, so a menu that owned Ctrl+Tab could leave it dead, and a key with a shortcut in the menu AND a handler in the page could run twice. Each menu item calls `tabCommand(id)` in main.js. `lifecycle.tabAction(id, {front, window, quitting})` decides: "run" (the main window is in front: `runTab(id)` runs `menu.tabScript(id)` in the page with `webContents.executeJavaScript`; no ipc channel; `tabScript` gives null for any name but the five); "show-and-run" (New Tab and Reopen Closed Tab while the window is hidden, minimized or behind another window: it comes to the front first); "show" (the same two with no window: a new window opens, and its page brings its tabs back by itself); "close-front" (Close Tab while another window is in front, such as the About panel or Developer Tools in a window of their own: `Menu.sendActionToFirstResponder("performClose:")`, so Cmd+W closes that window as before); "none" (Show Next Tab and Show Previous Tab without the main window in front; every command while the app quits). The page's answer is one of `menu.TAB_ANSWERS`: "done" (`window.Marginalia.tabs.run(id)` answered true), "last" (it did not, and the page has at most one tab), "blocked" (it did not, and the page has more tabs: a review or a dialog is on screen, or there was nothing to do), "none" (the page is not the web app: it did not load; or the script failed; `menu.tabAnswer` turns anything else into "none"). `lifecycle.closesWindow(id, answer)`: only Close Tab closes the window (which hides it: D23 (1), `CLOSE_HIDES`), and only on "last" or "none". So Cmd+W with one tab left hides the window as before the tabs; with more tabs open and a review or a dialog on screen it does nothing (before this repair it hid the window then). A late answer closes nothing when the app has begun to quit or the window is gone. Close Window keeps its role and is Shift+Cmd+W. `runTab` asks the page each time and trusts no flag of the main process: the tabs need no server, so they also work while the server is down. Self-test check `tabs` (`lib/selftest.js` `tabsProbe`, `tabsVerdict`, `TABS_LOOK`, `TABS_STEPS`, `TABS_NOT_COMMANDS`; main.js `probeTabs`): in the window that is never shown, `runTab` runs New Tab twice, Show Next Tab, Show Previous Tab, Close Tab until the page answers "last", then New Tab, Show Previous Tab, Close Tab (which closes the tab the page started with, the book's), Reopen Closed Tab and Close Tab. After each step the page reports its tabs' places in order, the tab on screen and whether the strip is shown, and the verdict checks exactly which tab came, which tab went and which one is on screen (a new tab is a library tab right after the tab that was on screen; next and previous go one tab on and back, tried with three tabs or more, where they differ; a close removes the tab on screen and shows its right neighbour, else its left one; the reopened tab is the one that was closed last), that the strip shows with two or more tabs only, and that `tabScript` gives null for "tab1", "lastTab" and "not-a-tab-command". A failure reports what blocked the commands in the page. The check runs `runTab`, not the menu's key presses and not `tabAction`: those are in test/main.test.js with Electron mocked (there the same sequence runs through the real menu items).
- **A flaw this check found in main.js** (in the app since 1.0.0; repaired with D30): `state.pageHealthy` ("the web app's page is loaded") went false when any navigation of the page started and came back only when a load finished. A link to another site starts a navigation that the app refuses (the link opens in the browser), so after one such link the flag stayed false for good. Its one use then was wrong: when the server came back after a stop, a page that was fine was reloaded (an open editor closed; its text stayed a draft). Now a main-frame navigation that starts remembers the flag's value (`healthBefore`; only the first of a series that overlaps), and one that is refused or given up (`did-fail-provisional-load` with -3, ERR_ABORTED, for the main frame) puts it back; a load that finishes or fails for real settles the flag and forgets the remembered value. A same-document navigation (a hash change) and a frame's navigation never touch it. The first form of `runTab` trusted the flag, and the self-test (which tries a refused link before the tab commands) showed every tab command doing nothing. Not checked: whether Chromium's own error page also ends with `did-finish-load` (the flag would then read true on it), and `render-process-gone` does not clear it; both are as before.
- **Links, Back and Forward**: boot asks `M.tabs.start(route, explicit)`; explicit = the address names a place (a reader, deck or stats hash). The stored tabs come back first. An explicit address then goes to a tab that shows that place already, else to the active tab when that is a library tab, else to a new tab after the active one; that tab is the one on screen. A plain address shows the stored active tab. While the app runs, a hashchange to a NEW address (a `marginalia://` link that the app's main process passes on, an address the user typed) goes through `M.tabs.link(route)` with the same rule; when it answers false (blocked), M.app routes as before. Back and Forward move the tab that is on screen; they pick and open no tab. M.app tells the two apart: every history entry it writes carries `HISTORY_MARK` `{marginalia: 1}` as its state (`writeHash`: pushState and replaceState both; boot marks the entry the page started on; an entry that a new address made is marked when it arrives, `markEntry`; the mark is written only where it is missing, `marked()`), and a hashchange on a marked entry (`ownEntry()`) routes directly. Going back adds no entry (`fromHistory` replaces), so Forward still works. Without the history API (a sandboxed frame), and after a history call that failed until one works again (`state.hashFallback`: a browser's limit on such calls), every address change routes directly, as before the tabs. `M.app.route()` answers a second route to the screen that is on its way with the first one's promise, but not a route that names a place inside the book (one prompt, a page, a tab's own place): that one goes on to `M.reader.open`, which keeps the place until the book is open (before this repair a second tab of a book that was still loading lost its place). Showing a tab replaces the history entry (`replace: true`) and adds none; the page has one history, not one per tab.
- **Storage**: localStorage "marginalia.tabs" = `{v: 1, tabs: [{screen, collectionId, position}], active: index}`: the places in order and the active tab's index; no ids, no titles, no prompt, no editor, no closed tabs. Since D31 the tab on screen is stored with the place that its book shows now (`M.reader.position()`; before, null: the book's `lastPosition` served), and a scroll that settled (`scroll:settled`) stores the list too; a window of the Mac app that is not the first has its own key ("Windows"). Written 200 ms after a change and on `pagehide`; never by a page that stepped aside (`M.store.locked()` with a reason other than "window") and never by the cover (`M.desktop.covered()`). Reading and writing never throw. It is on this Mac only: the key is in no JSON backup and no export.
- **The strip** (`#tabs`, css/tabs.css; `is-hidden` with fewer than two tabs): `.tabs__list[role=tablist][aria-label=Tabs]` > per tab `.tabs__tab[.is-active][role=presentation][data-id]` > `button.tabs__select[role=tab][aria-selected][title]` (tabindex 0 for the active tab, −1 for the others; the title as textContent) and `button.tabs__close[aria-label="Close tab <title>"]` "×"; then `button.tabs__new[aria-label="New tab"]` "+". A click shows the tab; a middle click on a tab closes it. Keys on a tab that has the focus (they stop at the strip, so the screen below does not get them: in the reader Enter shows a card's answer and Backspace deletes a card): ← and → move the focus along the strip, Enter and Space show the tab, Delete and Backspace close it. After a redraw the focus stays in the strip when it was there (on the tab that is on screen); it is never left on a button that is gone, and a redraw never takes the focus from the page. The strip is sticky at the top, 34 px high (`--tabs-h`, set on `#app` in styles.css), z-index 35: above the page and its menus (30), below the review overlay, the snip overlay and the bottom sheet (40), the banner (50), Settings, the dialogs and the toasts. A tab is 208 px wide and shrinks to 96 px; then the list scrolls sideways inside the strip, and the active tab is scrolled into view. Nothing scrolls sideways outside the strip. styles.css, while the strip is shown (`#app:has(> #tabs:not(.is-hidden))`): with the reader open `#app` is a column as high as the viewport and the reader takes the rest; `#library` and `#deck` are at least the viewport less `--tabs-h` high; `.lib-prep` sticks below the strip. With the banner shown too, the strip is not sticky (`position: static`): the banner keeps the top of a page that scrolls (both sticky at top 0, the banner's text would slide under the strip). `html` gets `scroll-padding-top: 34px` while the strip shows, so a row that the keyboard focus scrolls into view stops below the strip.
- **Other modules**: `M.app`: `app:routed`, `route(…, {position})`, `modal()`, the ? sheet's "Tabs" section, `HISTORY_MARK`, `marked()`, `ownEntry()`, `markEntry()`. `M.reader`: `position()`, `isOpen(id)`, `open(id, {position})` (a tab's place and zoom also when it comes while the book loads); an open that `close()` or a newer open replaced ends quietly also when the engine ends its load with an error ("Worker was destroyed"): before, M.app took that error for the book's and went to the library, also when the same book was on its way again. `M.margin`: `editing()`. `M.keys`: `TABS_MAP`. `M.library`: "Statistics", "Continue reading" and a deck's "Open" give their click to `M.tabs.go`. `M.stats`: a click on a prompt does too. index.html's own SELF-CHECK comment lists `#tabs`, css/tabs.css and the 30 modules. tools/smoke.js: `ORDER` and `HOST_IDS` have "tabs"; its fake DOM keeps `className` and `classList` as one thing, as a browser does (before, a class set through `className` was invisible to `querySelector`).
- **Choices Claude made** (each a named constant or one rule; the user can change them): 20 tabs at most; 10 closed tabs to bring back; a new tab opens right after the active one; Cmd+click opens behind and Shift+Cmd+click in front; the tab width; a link to a place that no tab shows reuses a library tab before it opens a new one; Back and Forward move the tab on screen.
- **Limits**: a tab that leaves the screen closes its book; coming back opens the PDF again at the tab's place (a moment for a large book). The closed tabs are forgotten at a restart. (Since D31 a tab can be dragged to another place in the strip, and in the Mac app to a window of its own: "Windows".) In a browser the browser's own tab keys win (above). A held Cmd+T, Cmd+W or Shift+Cmd+T acts once only when the page gets the key before the menu (Chromium's order for a key with Cmd; then the page swallows the repeats); not while Developer Tools have the focus. Not verified on the real Mac app: the keys as macOS delivers them (Cmd+T, Cmd+W and Shift+Cmd+T to the menu; Ctrl+Tab and the others to the page): the packaged self-test runs the commands, not the key presses.
- **Tests**: test/d30.test.js (35 tests: the pure helpers; then the real modules in the fake window of tools/smoke.js, with a reader stand-in that follows js/reader.js (a load can be held back; an open that another one replaced ends without a book) and a history that is a real stack: the commands, the strip, the keys through M.app's one listener, the Mac app's three menu keys left alone, a held one swallowed and the other keys handled there too, Cmd+click, the statistics page, a tab's editor (it returns; it waits for its book; two tabs on one book; a closed tab), what blocks every command, click and strip key, the ? sheet, the limits (20 tabs, 10 closed tabs, a link with 20 tabs), storage and restart, a page that stepped aside and the cover storing nothing, links, Back and Forward over pushed entries and over a link's entry, a history call that fails once, clicks and keys on the strip and the focus, the bracket keys by their place, a text field's blur, the strip's z-index against the other stylesheets); test/reader-dom.test.js (a tab's own place and zoom, also asked during a load; `isOpen`; a replaced open that ends with the engine's error); test/margin-dom.test.js (`editing()` for each kind of editor); test/smoke.test.js and test/desktop.test.js (30 modules, the stylesheet list). Desktop: test/menu.test.js (the five items, three with a key; Close Window on Shift+Cmd+W; the menu's tab keys equal `M.tabs.MENU_KEYS` and are the web app's own keys for the same commands; no other tab key and no Tab key in the menu; `tabScript`'s four answers, `tabAnswer`), test/lifecycle.test.js (`tabAction`, `closesWindow`, main.js's wiring), test/main.test.js (main.js with Electron mocked: the page of the window in front runs the command; Cmd+W with one tab left hides the window, and does nothing while the page says "blocked"; a late answer during a quit or after the window is gone; the window hidden, behind another window, minimized or gone; nothing while quitting; the commands after a refused link and while the server is down; the page-loaded flag after a refused link, a hash change, a frame's navigation and overlapping navigations; the self-test's sequence through the real menu items), test/selftest.test.js (`tabsProbe`, `tabsVerdict`, each fault failing only the `tabs` check), test/docs.test.js (the READMEs say what Cmd+W does).

- **Repairs after the two independent reviews** (2026-11-15; part of the contract, and they win over anything above that differs): the strip sat over the review overlay (z-index 60 against 40) and its clicks skipped the block, so a click changed tabs during a review: now z-index 35, and `select`, `close`, `open`, `reopen` and `go` check the block themselves. A second tab of a book that was still loading lost its place and its prompt (M.app's route answered with the first route): a route that names a place inside the book now reaches the reader. A tab closed while its editor stayed on screen in the next tab of the same book brought a second editor back with Cmd+Shift+T: the closed tab keeps none now. A tab left before its book got open lost its editor, and a route's early promise could open the editor before the book: the editor now stays with the tab until `M.reader.isOpen`, and comes on `doc:opened`. In the Mac app, Cmd+W during a review or a dialog hid the window also with several tabs open: the page now answers "blocked" and the window stays. The focus was left on a button that a redraw had removed, and Enter on a focused tab went to the reader's keys: the strip keeps its focus and takes Enter, Space, Delete and Backspace itself. One failed history call turned the tab links off for the session: the fallback now ends when a history call works. The banner slid under the strip: the strip is not sticky while the banner shows. A book left while it loads, and shown again at once, could send the page to the library (the first load's "Worker was destroyed" counted as a failed open): a replaced open ends quietly now (found in the real browser's console after the repairs). Also: the bracket keys by their place; a text field's blur before a tab leaves; a notice when a link takes the tab on screen with 20 tabs open; the page-loaded flag keeps the first value of overlapping navigations; the self-test's tab check became strict about which tab comes and goes. The mutation check `feat13/check/mutate.py` (in Claude's scratch folder; 53 single changes to the repaired code, all caught) is the measure for the tests.

## Windows (binding; added 2026-11-15)
- **Source (D31, FEATURES-T2)**: after step 1 ("Tabs") Claude asked for a go-ahead for step 2 and said how the library stays safe (one window saves at a time; the window that is used takes over). The user: "I want it the way preview works. So that you can drag a tab to a new window and command N opens a new window". Claude's choice, told to the user: the windows use the web app's own tab strip, not the tab bar of macOS (a window then holds one open book; the gestures are the same).
- **Words**: a WINDOW is one page of the web app in one window of the Mac app, with its own tabs. The WRITER is the one window that may write to the library; every other window is a READER: it shows its page and writes nothing until it is the writer. HANDOVER MODE: the pages of the Mac app (`M.desktop.isApp()`, the store in idb mode, `navigator.locks` there). Everywhere else LEGACY MODE: "One writing tab per origin", unchanged.
- **The writer role** (`js/store.js`): `M.app` calls `M.store.handover(true)` before `open()` (→ boolean; `handover()` → whether the mode is in force). In handover mode a page starts as a reader: `locked()` → `{reason: "window", by: null, at}`, every write is refused with code "locked" exactly as in a tab that stepped aside, but no `store:mode` "othertab" goes out and nothing is announced on localStorage "marginalia.tab". The role is the Web Lock "marginalia.writer" (`WRITER_LOCK`): it goes free when its page dies, so a window that closed or crashed never blocks the others. `acquire(sync)` → Promise<boolean>: the page takes a number for its ask (the clock, and above every number it has seen), asks for the lock, and says `{type: "want", id, at}` on the BroadcastChannel (again every 300 ms until it has an answer). Of two asks the later one wins (the higher number, then the higher page id), whatever the order in which the browser gives the lock: the writer that hears a later ask refuses new writes at once and lets the lock go when its writes under way have ended (`idle()`); a page whose own ask is the earlier one ends that ask (→ false), also while its sync runs (the lock then goes on at once: a read that hangs holds the role back from nobody). A writer that hears an ask that is not later than its own answers `{type: "held", id, at}` with its number; the page that asked (it started later, or its clock stands behind) asks again with a later one. The page that gets the lock calls `sync(true)` and waits for it: a page that takes the role was not the writer just before, so another page may have written, and it reads the library again. No record outside the lock decides that (localStorage is not in step between the pages of one origin at every moment). Only then `writer()` is true, `locked()` is null, and `store:writer {writer: true}` goes out; a page that loses the role sends `store:writer {writer: false}`. A sync that fails rejects `acquire` with code "sync" (`err.cause`), and leaves the page a reader and the role free. A readwrite transaction that began as the writer ends as it began (it is counted by `idle()`, not aborted). `during(work)` → the work's promise: while it is pending the page keeps the role and a page that asks waits (an import, a PDF that is added); when the work ends, the role goes to a page that asked within the last `WANT_FRESH_MS` 1500 ms (a page whose window closed meanwhile gets nothing; one that still waits says its ask again). `exclusive(name, fn)` → a Web Lock "marginalia.<name>" taken only when it is free (`fn(true)` holds it until its promise ends; `fn(false)` otherwise); `hold(name)` holds one for the page's life; `held()` → Promise<string[]|null> (the names that some page holds). Outside handover mode: `acquire` → true unless a newer tab took over, `during` runs the work, `exclusive` → `fn(true)`, `hold` → false, `held` → null.
- **What a page reads again**: `M.cache.reload()` → Promise<`{prompts, collections, folders}`> (how many changed): it reads every record again and brings the maps to the store's state. A record that is the same (JSON) keeps its object; a changed one is replaced, and the bus events of a local change go out: `collection:changed` and `folders:changed {reloaded: true}` first, then `prompt:saved {prompt, isNew}`, `prompt:deleted`, `prompt:restored`, then `collection:deleted`. So every view follows. It writes nothing. A `reload()` that a newer `reload()` overtook changes nothing when it ends (a window whose ask was ended while it read lets the lock go at once and reads on; that old read may end after the window has taken the role again and read anew). `M.settings.reload()` reads localStorage again and emits `settings:changed` for each key that differs (→ the keys).
- **M.app** (`js/app.js`): boot turns handover mode on in the Mac app, opens the store, holds the Web Lock "marginalia.window.<id>" (first thing, for the page's life: "this window is there"), then takes the role BEFORE `M.cache.load()` (so the library is read once, after the last writer's writes); a writer that does not answer within `ROLE_WAIT_MS` 1500 ms does not hold the window up: it starts as a reader. `takeRole()` → Promise<boolean> = `M.store.acquire(→ settings.reload + cache.reload)`, one at a time; true at once outside handover mode. When the role comes while boot's first `cache.load()` is still under way (it began as a reader), that load ends first and the library is then read once more: what the load read can be older than the last writer's last write. The window's `focus` event takes the role. In a reader, `keydown`, `keypress`, `pointerdown`, `mousedown`, `click`, `dblclick`, `contextmenu`, `paste`, `cut` and `drop` (`ROLE_EVENTS`, capture phase on the window) take the role, and the event is swallowed: this page's copy of the library may be old. Two drops go through, because a drag gives no focus and the page handles them as a reader: a tab of another window (`M.tabs.TAB_TYPE`), and a file on `#library` (`M.library.addPdf` takes the role itself). The wheel and the pointer's moves take nothing: a reader can be scrolled and looked at. A role that has not come after `ROLE_SLOW_MS` 600 ms (another window keeps it with `during`) shows the notice "Waiting for another Marginalia window to finish its work…" until it comes. A role that cannot come because the library could not be read again (`acquire` rejected) shows the notice "This window could not read the library again. Close the window, or choose View › Reload."; from then on the window's keys and clicks go through (the user can read and copy; a save is refused) and each of them tries again; the notice goes, and a reader's input is held back again, as soon as the store has answered an ask (with the role, or with "a later ask ended this one"). `prompt:refused` in handover mode (a save that came while another window had the role; rare, since input is not acted on in a reader): the notice "Not saved: another Marginalia window was in use. Save again." stays until closed, the editor keeps the text (`js/margin.js`), and the window does not take the role by itself (the other window is the one in use, and its next key would be lost). No `#tabLock` notice in handover mode. `startReview` holds `M.store.exclusive("review")` from the session's start to its end: one review at a time among the windows; another window gets the notice "A review is open in another window". The hold ends with the session, at its `review:ended` (js/review.js sends it once: when the last card is done, with the end screen still up, or when the review is left). The window that holds it starts its next review as always (`M.review.start` replaces its own session, also one that "Go to source" set aside): the `review:ended` of the session that is replaced during that start does not end the hold, and when the start then brings no session (nothing was due), the hold ends there. `leave()` → Promise<boolean>: the window closes for good (the Mac app calls it): `M.tabs.forget()` at once, and when `M.reader.unsaved()` the window first takes the role, which stores the place.
- **The other modules in a reader**: `M.cache`'s writers answer as in a tab that stepped aside (`savePrompt` rejects "locked"; the quiet writers resolve with the record as it is). `M.reader.snapshot(w, h)` → a canvas of at most w × h CSS pixels with the pages that are in view (cut to the view and to the pages' column), or null (no book, no page drawn yet): the picture of a tab that is dragged. `M.reader`: a scroll that settled in a reader cannot store `lastPosition`; the reader holds it back (`unsaved()` → boolean) and stores it on `store:writer {writer: true}`; the window's stored list of tabs has the place meanwhile. `M.backup`: a reader skips its daily check and keeps its timer (in legacy mode a tab that stepped aside stops for good), and checks when it becomes the writer. `M.library.addPdf`: the whole add runs under `M.store.during` (also its question about a PDF that is in the library already), and it takes the role (`M.app.takeRole()`) before it compares the file with the library; when the role does not come: code "locked" and the notice "Not added: another Marginalia window was in use. Add the PDF again." `M.library.attachPdf` and the restore of PDFs from the backup folder (`M.backup`) run under `during`. `M.exporter.importJSON` takes the role first and runs under `during` (code "locked" when the role does not come). `M.settings.set` and `M.obsidian` refuse in a reader as in a tab that stepped aside.
- **A window's tabs** (`js/tabs.js`): a window's id is the WHOLE query of its address, "?w=<id>" (6 to 16 of a–z and 0–9; `windowId()` → the id, or null for the first window, for any other query, and in a browser), as `desktop/lib/policy.js` `windowOf` reads it. Its list is localStorage "marginalia.tabs" (no id) or "marginalia.tabs.<id>". A reader stores its own list too (it is its own key). At its start the first window takes over the lists of windows that are gone: every key "marginalia.tabs.<id>" whose lock "marginalia.window.<id>" nobody holds; their book and deck tabs come after its own (a library tab holds nothing to bring back), as far as 20 allow; a key whose tabs all came is removed, and what found no room stays stored under its key for a later start (the key is written first: when that fails, the whole list stays and none of its tabs comes in now). A first window that had nothing of its own to show (no stored list, no link) gives up its library tab for them (it does not count against the 20) and shows the first tab that came. `moveToWindow(tabId?, {x, y}?)` → Promise<boolean>: it writes the new window's list (that one tab, with its place and its open editor) under a new id of 10 characters, asks for the window with `window.open("/?w=<id>", "_blank", "popup=yes,width=…,height=…[,left=…,top=…]")` (this window's size; x − 80 and y − 20 of the drop), and takes the tab out of this window (it is no closed tab). False with one tab, while blocked, outside handover mode, with `WINDOWS_MAX` 8 windows open (notice "Close a window first: 8 windows are open"), or when `window.open` throws. When the new window's lock does not show within `WINDOW_WAIT_MS` 4000 ms, the list is taken back and the tab returns, with the notice "The new window did not open: its tab is back in this window" (the new page takes its lock long before it reads its list, so a list that was taken back was not read). `dump()` → `[{screen, collectionId, position, editing}]` (the tab on screen with the place and editor it has now), or null while a review or a dialog is on screen (that window's tabs stay); `take(list)` → the indexes of `list` that came in (after the tab on screen; only tabs of the library as this page knows it, as far as 20 allow); `adopt(list, index?, {active}?)` → how many came in (at `index`; with `active` the first of them is shown); neither takes anything while blocked; `drop(indexes)` → how many tabs are left after those tabs of the last `dump()` went (0: the list is forgotten; −1: no list of indexes, or no dump before); `forget()`: removes this window's key, and nothing is stored after that; `move(id, index)` → boolean. `document.title` = "<the tab on screen> — Marginalia" ("Marginalia" for the library): the title bar and the Window menu show it.
- **A tab that is dragged** (HTML drag and drop; every tab is `draggable`): `dragstart` puts `{w, id, drag, tab}` under the type "application/x-marginalia-tab" (`TAB_TYPE`; `drag` = a number for this drag; `tab` as `dump()` gives it), and a second type of the drag's own, `DRAG_TYPE` "application/x-marginalia-drag-" + that number (a page can read the types while a tab is over it, the data only at the drop: by this type it knows the drag that began in it); nothing starts while blocked, and a drag that cannot take its data is cancelled. The strip `#tabs` takes a tab on `dragover` and `drop` (the mark `.is-drop` on the tab it would stand before, or on "+"). Its own tab: `move`, and the drag is over with the drop (the strip is drawn again there; no `dragend` is needed). A tab of another window (handover mode only) comes in at that place, on screen, and the window says `{type: "took", drag}` on the BroadcastChannel "marginalia.tabs". It comes in AT the drop or not at all (a "took" that came later could leave the tab in two windows): when the page does not know the tab's book (it is a reader and the book is new), the tab stays in its window, the page takes the role (which reads the library again) and says "Drag the tab once more: this window did not know that book yet". The tab's own window takes the tab out ONLY when it hears that "took" (before or after its `dragend`; the drag is then over for it, whether a `dragend` follows or not): what `dragend` reports (`dropEffect`) is no proof, so a drop that took nothing (another program, a window that has no such book) leaves the tab where it was. A strip with 20 tabs takes no tab of another window (the page then says "letgo", below). A window with one tab shows its strip (`#tabs.is-dropzone`) while a tab is dragged over its document; that strip, and a mark that the page set, go `OVER_MS` 700 ms after the last `dragover` (a page hears neither that the tab left it nor that the drag ended elsewhere; the standard lets an engine wait up to 550 ms between two `dragover`s). The page takes a tab too (the document's `dragover` and `drop`, capture phase). The tab of its own window (never behind a dialog): let go within `TEAR_PX` 24 px of the strip, the tab takes its place in the strip; let go anywhere else on the page, it gets a window of its own at that point at once (`moveToWindow`), as a tab that is dragged down in Preview. A tab of another window (handover mode only): within `TEAR_PX` of this strip, when the strip takes tabs (`stripTakes`: room in it, no dialog or review up), the tab comes into the strip as a drop on the strip does ("took"); anywhere else on the window, the page says `{type: "letgo", drag, x, y}` (the point on the screen) on the same channel, and the tab's own window, which hears it before or after its `dragend`, gives the tab a window there (`moveToWindow`; without a point: next to itself). "Anywhere else" includes a strip with 20 tabs and a window with a dialog or a review up: such a window takes no tab, marks no place and shows no strip as a place to drop, but its page says where the tab was let go. So a tab that is let go on any Marginalia window gets its window at once unless a strip takes it (also when that window lies over the tab's own window), and a drop differs from a drag that is cancelled with Escape (no `drop` comes, and nothing happens). A page knows its own drag only by the drag's own type: `w` and a tab's id repeat in other pages (a browser page of Marginalia writes `w` "" as the first window does), so a tab that carries them is still another page's tab. `dragend` with `dropEffect` "none" and the pointer outside the window (the desktop, another program): after `DROP_WAIT_MS` 200 ms without a "took" or a "letgo", `moveToWindow` at the pointer (`screenX`, `screenY`). A drag whose end never came: when the strip is drawn again during a drag, the element that was dragged leaves the page, and an engine may then send no `dragend` (Chromium still sends it to that element, though not to the document: a real drag in a real browser page showed it on 2026-11-15; what follows is the second line of defence). That drag is over for its page (`endDrag`) when a tab of another drag comes over the page or the strip or is dropped there (its types do not carry this drag's type), and at the next `mousemove` or `mousedown` once that element is no longer a tab of the strip (no mouse event comes during a drag; one that an engine sends in the first moment of a drag ends nothing, because the element is still there). So a page never takes another drag's tab for its own. The drag that a page took for over is kept (`st.was`) until its next drag begins: when it goes on all the same (an engine that sends a mouse event during a drag), its events are the page's own again (`ownDrag`), its `dragend` counts, and so does a window's word about it. So its tab never comes into its own window a second time, and never stays in two windows. While a tab is dragged, a small picture of it follows the pointer, also outside the window (`dataTransfer.setDragImage` with a canvas of 220 × 150 CSS pixels that is in the page, off screen, for that moment: a little window with the tab's name on a bar and, under it, what the book shows, `M.reader.snapshot(220, 126)`; for a book tab that is not on screen, the picture taken when it left the screen; else the kind of place, "Library", "Statistics", "Deck", "Book"); without a canvas the system shows the tab itself. A tab's picture goes when the tab's place changes (a route, a link that the tab takes, its book deleted); a tab that leaves its window for good (closed, moved, taken, merged) is not pictured. The tab in the strip looks lifted (`.is-dragging`) until the drag is over. The library takes no tab (`js/library.js` `isTabDrag`). In a browser a tab only moves along its strip (also when it is let go close to the strip), and a page takes no tab of another page.
- **In the Mac app** (`desktop/`): `lib/policy.js` `windowOf(url, origin)` → the id when the URL is the app's own page "/" with exactly the query "?w=<id>" (any hash), else null; `windowOpen` gains `window` (and `load` is false then); `windowFeatures(features)` → `{x, y, width, height}`|null; `MAX_WINDOWS` 8. `main.js` keeps every window (`state.wins`: window → `{w, healthy, healthBefore, closing}`); "the window" for links, the Dock icon and the menu is the one in front, else the one that was in front last (`theWindow()`; never one that is destroyed). `setWindowOpenHandler` never lets Electron make a window: for `window` it calls `openAppWindow(id, features, from)` (`createWindow({w, bounds, from})`: the same webPreferences and preload; at the place asked when it is on a screen, `lib/windowstate.js`, else set off 28 px from the window it came from; on screen at once, `show: true`, in the page's own background colour, while the first window of the app shows when its page has painted; refused with 8 windows, for an id that is open, while the server is down or the app quits). A window never changes its id: a navigation of a window with an id to any other address of the app's page is turned back to its own page, a link that main.js loads into a window keeps that window's "?w", and the first window's page reads no id from an address with anything else in its query. `lib/lifecycle.js` `closeAction({…, windows})` → "close-window" when more than one window is open: `closeForGood(win)` runs the page's `M.app.leave()` (`lib/menu.js` `windowScript("leave")`; at most 1.5 s), destroys the window, and has the window in front take the role (`windowScript("role")`), so the next key there is not lost. The last window hides, as before. A quit closes every window without asking the pages, so every window's tabs are there at the next start. Menu (Window): New Window Cmd+N (first), Move Tab to New Window and Merge All Windows (no keys; after the tab commands); the tab commands go to the window in front. Merge All Windows: for each other window `dump` (nothing while a review or a dialog is on screen there) → `take` in the window in front (→ the indexes that came in; `takenIndexes` checks the answer) → `drop(those indexes)` in the other; a window that gave all its tabs is destroyed; a tab that found no room, or that the window in front does not know, stays in its window. `windowScript(id, arg)`: "moveTab", "dump", "take" (the JSON text of a list, at most 200000 characters, passed as one string that the page parses), "drop" (a list of indexes), "leave", "role"; null for anything else. With two or more windows macOS lists them in the Window menu by their titles; with one, Window › Marginalia shows it. The page-loaded flag, reload, zoom, the right-click menu and the "stopped working" dialog are per window; `window-state.json` keeps the bounds of the window that was moved or closed last (one entry), and at a quit those of the window that was in front, where the one window of the next start then opens.
- **Self-test** (`lib/selftest.js` `windowsProbe`, `windowsVerdict`; check "windows"): a second window "?w=selftest2" (never shown; its page is slowed down as a background page is, `backgroundThrottling` on for it alone) takes the role as it starts, and the first window's page is a reader. A note saved in the second is not in the first one's copy; a save in the first is refused; the first takes the role back, reads the library again and has the note; the second is a reader then, and a tab that comes into it is stored under its own key. The second window closes for good (destroyed, its list forgotten) and the first writes again. Then the first page's own `M.tabs.moveToWindow()` moves a tab out, as a dragged tab does: `window.open` → `lib/policy.js` → a third window with an id that the page made, which holds that one tab and is the writer; it closes for good too, and the first window has its tabs as before.
- **Choices Claude made** (named constants; the user can change them): 8 windows at most; the role follows the focus and the first key or click; 600 ms before the "Waiting…" notice; 1.5 s that a start or a close waits for another page; 4 s before a tab returns from a window that did not open; 200 ms that a tab dropped outside every window waits for a window that took it after all; 24 px around the strip in which a tab that is let go stays in the strip; 700 ms without a `dragover` before a page takes a tab that was held over it for gone; the small picture's size (220 × 150); after a restart one window with the tabs of all; a library tab of a closed window is not brought back.
- **Limits**: a window that is not used shows what another window changed only when it is used (a click, a key, the focus). The first click or key in a reader is not acted on (on macOS the first click on a window in the background only brings it to the front anyway). A place scrolled in a reader goes into the store when that window has the role again or closes; a quit keeps it in the window's stored list of tabs. One review at a time. While a window adds a PDF or imports (also while it asks about a PDF that is in the library already), the other windows wait, with the "Waiting…" notice. When the user goes back to a window whose long work another window waited for, the role still goes to that other window when the work ends, and the next key in the first window brings it back. A drag that is cancelled with Escape while the pointer is outside the tab's window makes a window for the tab, as a drop there does (the page cannot tell the two apart); over the tab's own window nothing happens. A tab that is let go on another program's window that lies over the tab's own window stays where it was (the page cannot tell this from Escape). A tab that is let go on the page of a window that is not in use makes that window take the role, as any drop there does; the new window takes it at its start. A new window needs a moment for its page and its book (about half a second; its frame is on screen at once). A tab that is let go outside every Marginalia window gets its window later than one let go on a page: the page hears of it only at the drag's end, which macOS holds back while it moves the drag's picture back to where the drag began (its default for a drop that nothing took; a page cannot switch that off in Electron as it is), and then `DROP_WAIT_MS` runs (not measured here: no test can do a real drag). The small picture of a book tab that was never on screen in this window shows no page. A reader's refusals keep the words of the legacy rule ("Marginalia is open in another tab") where a module says them itself (`M.obsidian`, `M.settings`); they cannot come from the user's own input, which is not acted on in a reader. The closed tabs (Cmd+Shift+T) are per window and a tab that moved out is none. Not verified on the real Mac: the drag gestures between real windows (the self-test moves a tab through the page's own command, not with the pointer; in a real browser page a real drag along the strip, and one down onto the page, were tried and work, and two real pages of one origin passed a "letgo" with stand-in events), the small picture as the system draws it (no test sees it; the canvas itself was looked at in a real browser page), Cmd+N as macOS gives it to the menu, where `dragend` reports the pointer after a drop outside the window (also with several displays), and a hand-over to a window that macOS has put to sleep for long.
- **Tests**: test/d31.test.js (the role between data-only pages on one fake IndexedDB with shared Web Locks: a reader's start, the hand-over with its reload, a page that always reads again, writes under way, `during` and a page that is gone, three windows, asks in the same instant, 80 random hand-overs with no change lost, a sync that fails or hangs, an ask that was ended while it read, a read that a newer read overtook, a number that cannot win ("held"), the legacy fallbacks, `exclusive`/`hold`/`held`, `reload`; then the real modules as windows of one origin: the start, focus and the input guard, the refused save, views after a hand-over, each window's own list, the lists of windows that are gone (also more tabs than one window holds), the place of the tab on screen, `moveToWindow` and its refusals, a window that never comes, `dump`/`take`/`drop`, a window that is blocked, `leave`, the waiting notice, one review at a time, an import; drag and drop with stand-in events: along the strip, into another window, out of the window, a full strip, a browser page, the library and a file; and the cases the three independent reviews found: the role that comes during a window's first read, a review that ends with its end screen up, a review set aside in the same window, a library that cannot be read again, an address with more in its query, a book the other window does not know yet, a drop that took nothing, a "took" that comes before or after the drag's end, a drag without an end, a list of a closed window that cannot be written; and the user's wishes after 1.5.0: a tab let go on the page of its own window, close to the strip and far from it, with 8 windows open; a tab let go on another window's page ("letgo", before and after the drag's end) and close to that window's strip; a window with one tab, a window behind a dialog and a strip with 20 tabs (both say "letgo"), a browser page; a mark that goes when the tab has left; a drag whose end never came (another drag's tab, the mouse; the same drag going on: its drop, a window's word about it and its end still count, and the tab is not there twice); a tab of another page that carries this window's id and the id of one of its tabs;
