the small picture (also after a route, a link with 20 tabs open, a deleted book), the tabs that are not pictured, and the lifted look), test/reader-dom.test.js (the place held back, `snapshot`), test/library-dom.test.js (a PDF that is added takes and keeps the role), and in desktop/: test/policy.test.js, test/lifecycle.test.js (also: a window that the user asked for is on screen at once, never in the self-test, whose "hidden" check counts a window that was made visible), test/menu.test.js (`windowScript`, `takenIndexes`), test/main.test.js (several windows with Electron mocked: Cmd+N, closing, a quit and the place it keeps, a tab that moves out, 8 windows, a window's id, Move Tab to New Window, Merge All Windows, the window in front, a window that is destroyed, the page-loaded flag per window), test/selftest.test.js (`windowsVerdict`, `windowsProbe`).

## Public release (binding; added 2026-11-17)
The user asked to "make the current app usable so that I can put it on github and people can use it" (DECISIONS D32). The user chose the MIT licence, the name "dshani" on its copyright line, and that other people build the Mac app from the source.
- **No personal data**: no file of the project holds private data of a person: no real name of the author (`LICENSE` carries the public name that the user chose), no e-mail address, no home folder (a home folder is written `~/…`; tests use made-up names such as `/Users/someone`). What belongs to one person's Mac (their vault, what is installed, what they verified) is in `CLAUDE.local.md`, which `.gitignore` leaves out. `desktop/test/docs.test.js` ("no file of the project holds a home folder's path or an e-mail address") reads marginalia/, desktop/, bin/, LICENSES/, .claude/ and the files at the top, never the folder around the project.
- **No copied text**: the text of `test/sample.pdf` (`test/make_sample_pdf.py`, "Open sample PDF" in the library, packed into the Mac app) was written for that file, a short primer on memory and spaced repetition (an independent audit before the first publication asked for that). The tests and the documents hold the project's own text; the research and design notes name their sources and quote no more than a few common words of them (a term that the source video shows highlighted, for example).
- **Licences**: `LICENSE` (MIT), `desktop/package.json` "license": "MIT", `THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES.md` (the scheduler's rules are a port of Orbit's, Apache-2.0, with `LICENSES/Apache-2.0.txt`; PDF.js, KaTeX, the Outfit font and Electron are downloaded, not stored here). The same docs test checks these.
- **What git leaves out**: `.gitignore` at the top (`.DS_Store`, `__pycache__`, `marginalia/dist/`, `marginalia/tools/.cache/`, `.claude/settings.local.json`, `CLAUDE.local.md`) and `desktop/.gitignore` (`node_modules`, `.cache`, `build`, `out`, `sign-identity`). A clone with none of these passes `marginalia test` (one test skips: it needs the download cache of `marginalia build`), and `npm ci` then `npm run app` in `desktop/` build the app and pass its self-test. The build tries a download that fails on the network three times in all (`desktop/scripts/vendor.js` ATTEMPTS; an HTTP error is final) and then says to run it again; the files that came stay in `.cache/cdn`. One trial build of a fresh copy had stopped on a single font file that the network did not deliver.
- **For a new reader**: `README.md` at the top (what it is, what it needs, how to install, where the data is, the limits). The Mac app's welcome card reads "Welcome to Marginalia" and tells a new reader to start with an empty library; the app's own text names no browser (the `marginalia` command names Chrome and Brave, because it opens them).
- **Checked on 2026-11-17**, on the files of this release as git publishes them (177 files): they were cloned into a new folder; there `node --test` (web and desktop), the code checks, the headless boot and the Python tests passed, `npm ci` ran, and `npm run app` built the app with every vendored file downloaded anew and passed the packaged self-test (39 checks). The last wording of four documents (`DECISIONS.md`, `CLAUDE.md`, this section, `desktop/README.md`) was set after that trial; no document goes into the app, and the tests were run again on the final files. Not verified: another Mac, an Intel Mac, Python older than 3.13, Linux, Windows.

## Undo and redo (binding; added 2026-11-18)
The user asked: "Is there a way to add undo/re-do commands?" and chose one undo and redo for cards (DECISIONS D33). A CARD here is a prompt record of any kind: a Q./A. prompt, a cloze prompt, a note, a highlight.

- **A step** is one change that a person made to a card. A view marks it with `opts.step` on the writer of `M.cache`: `savePrompt(p, {content: true, step})`, `removePrompt(id, {step})`, `restorePrompt(id, {step})`. `step` is `true`, or a token of `M.undo.group()` when several writes are ONE step. These calls are steps:
  - js/margin.js: the editor's save (a new prompt or note, an edit, "Write prompt" on a highlight, each half of a Split, "Another angle…"), F (`flag`), "Move to…" (`moveTo`), "Anchor to selection", Backspace (`deleteCard`), and Z (`undoDelete`);
  - js/list.js: the row editor's save and the second half of a Split, the flag, "Move to…", the delete and its Z. `movePrompt` saves a COPY of the record (it changed the cached record itself before). `saveEdit` starts from the record as the cache has it at the save, not from the one of the moment the editor opened (a flag, a move, a send record or an answer that came since stays). A prompt that was deleted meanwhile (another window did it) comes back with the saved text, so no text goes into a deleted record: `restorePrompt` and `savePrompt` carry one token and are ONE step ("Restore"). But never into a book or deck that is deleted too: then nothing is saved, the notice "Not saved: the book or deck of this prompt is deleted. Copy your text before you close the editor." shows, and the editor stays open with the text. The editor takes that text back (`setText`), so it keeps a draft of it again (an editor drops its draft at every save) and a second Save reaches the list at once;
  - js/reader.js: H (`saveHighlight`);
  - js/library.js: the deck page's "Add" (`saveBatch`): every prompt of one Add carries the same token, so they are one step. Its notice "Added n prompts" has the button Undo, which calls `M.undo.undo({group: token})`: after an Add the focus stays in the add box, where Cmd+Z works on the box's text.
- **Never steps** (no `opts.step`): everything in a review (an answer, F, Backspace: U and the review's own notice take those back), the Obsidian send record (`patchPrompts`), a found position and a lost mark (js/reader.js `refindPrompt`, js/library.js `markAnchorsLost`), `recontext`, an import, and every writer of a book, a deck or a folder (the library keeps its own "Undo" notices).
- **`M.cache`** (additions to SPEC §17.2):
  - A writer that gets `opts.step` hands `M.undo.note({id, before, after}, step)` a copy of the record as it was and as it is now (`before` null: a new prompt), at once, before the bus event. `savePrompt` takes "as it was" from the cached record, so a step save must hand in ANOTHER object than the cached one: a save of the cached record itself hands in two equal states, which is no step. A new prompt whose first store write fails is dropped again, and its step with it (`retract()`: the redo list that the step had emptied is there again, unless another step or an undo came since); an edit whose write fails stays in the session, and so does its step. Without js/undo.js `opts.step` does nothing.
  - `stateKey(p)` → string: is the prompt live, then kind, text, tags, flagged, collectionId, the passage (the quote with prefix and suffix, or page and y of a free note) and the cards. `"-"` for no record; null for data that cannot be written down (it equals nothing). The send record, a found position, the context snapshot and every time stamp are NOT in it.
  - `revertPrompts(changes, {now})` → `Promise<Array<Prompt|null>>`: the one writer of `M.undo`, never a step itself. `changes` = `[{id, from, to}]`: for each prompt the two snapshots of a step (null: there was no record). ALL the prompts go from `from` to `to`, or none does:
    1. Nothing is touched unless every prompt can go. It rejects code "changed" when a prompt is not in its state `from` (`stateKey`; for `from` null the prompt must be missing or deleted, whatever a deleted record holds), and code "gone" when a `to` is a live prompt whose collection is deleted or whose record is missing. `err.ids` names the prompts that are in the way. Code "bad_revert": an entry without an id, or a prompt twice. Code "locked" in a tab that stepped aside.
    2. Then every record changes in one synchronous run, and after that the writers' bus events go out (`prompt:deleted`, `prompt:restored`, `prompt:saved`). A prompt takes the state of its `to`:
       - `to` null: the prompt is deleted as `removePrompt` does (`deletedAt` and `updatedAt` are `now`); its fields stay.
       - The fields of `stateKey` come from `to` when they differ: kind, text, tags, flagged, collectionId and the cards (each card with its schedule and its history, also a card that the edit had dropped), and `revisedAt` is the one of `to` (absent when `to` has none), so the struggling count goes on as before the step. The anchor and the context come from `to` only when the collection or the passage differ; for a live prompt the context is then computed again (`contextFor`). In every other case the record keeps its own anchor and context, which may hold a newer found position. Every other field stays as the record has it at that moment: `obsidianSentAt`, `obsidianFile`, `obsidianPendingFile`, `createdAt`.
       - Then the prompt is live or deleted as `to` is. A deleted `to` with other fields than the deleted record has comes back with those fields, so the way forward of that step still fits.
       - `updatedAt` is `now`. For a prompt that is live afterwards `contentUpdatedAt` is `now` too (also when only the cards differ): the change wins an import merge, and `M.obsidian` counts the card as edited since its last send ("Update in Obsidian" replaces the copy; "Send all" never adds a second one).
    3. Then ONE store write (`prompts.putMany`, one transaction) stores them all: the store never holds half of a step. When it fails, the records keep their state in the session (as after an edit that could not be saved) and it rejects code "store" after the toast "Could not save" (no toast when the lock refused the write: that page says so itself). Code "locked" comes only from part 1, when nothing was changed.
- **`M.undo`** (`js/undo.js`, a module beside SPEC §17.2; no DOM and no storage, it loads in Node; loaded right after js/cache.js, 31 namespaces):
  - `note(change, step)` → `{retract()}` | null (for `M.cache` only); `group()` → token; `undo({group}?)` / `redo()` → `Promise<{ok, action, reason?, label?, ids?}>`; `run("undo" | "redo")` → boolean (for `M.app`'s key routing); `canUndo()`, `canRedo()`, `size()` → `{undo, redo}`, `peek("undo" | "redo")` → `{label, excerpt, where}` | null, `texts()` → the text of every snapshot, `clear()`, `excerpt(record)`, `idle()` → a Promise that resolves when no undo or redo is under way (the keys start them and do not wait); `LIMIT` 100, `TEXT`, `NOTICE_ID` "undo".
  - Two lists of ENTRIES live in the page: the steps to take back and the steps to make again. An entry holds one change, or the changes of one group. A new step empties the redo list. At most `LIMIT` entries wait for undo; the oldest goes first. A reload empties both lists.
  - No step: a change whose two states are the same (`stateKey`), and a change with no live card before and none after. In an entry one card is one change: a second step of a group on the same card goes on from the first (its `before` stays, its `after` is the new one), and a change that ends where it began leaves the entry. An ungrouped step that takes the newest entry back by hand (its states are that entry's states, the other way round: Z after a delete, F after F) is not a new entry: that entry moves to the redo list.
  - `undo()` and `redo()` run one after the other, in the order of the calls. Each one, in this order:
    1. answers "unavailable" without a loaded cache that has `revertPrompts`;
    2. answers "importing" while `M.exporter.isImporting()`;
    3. answers "review" while a review is on screen (`M.review.isActive()` and not `isSuspended()`; a review that waits behind the reader does not stop it);
    4. answers "modal" while a question, a dialog, the settings or the ? sheet is up (`M.app.modal()`; a notice's button can still be clicked then), and says nothing;
    5. takes the writer role (`M.app.takeRole()`; "role" when it does not come), so a window of the Mac app reads the library again before anything is compared. Everything after this is ONE piece of work inside `M.store.during()`: this window keeps the role until the entry is stored, and a window that asks for the role meanwhile waits;
    6. for `undo({group})`: takes that group's entry only (the newest one of it, when another step cut the group in two). It answers "moved" when that entry is in the list and is not the newest one. When no entry of the group is in the list, it answers "already" if the group was taken back (and not made again since), else "late"; nothing is written, and the entries stay;
    7. answers "empty" with nothing to take;
    8. answers "editor" when an editor of this window holds a prompt of the entry: nothing is written and the entry stays for later. The notice says so, and says "It waits in another tab." when no editor on screen holds the card. An editor holds the prompt that it edits (`promptId`), and the second editor of a Split holds the first half too (its draft key is "split:<the first half's id>"). The editors asked are the margin's (`M.margin.editing()`), those that wait with tabs not on screen (`M.tabs.heldEditors()`) and the prompt list's (`M.list.editingId()`). An editor for a new prompt, the deck page's add box too, holds no card and stops nothing;
    9. hands the whole entry to `M.cache.revertPrompts` (undo: each prompt from its `after` to its `before`; redo: the other way round), which checks all, changes all and stores all in one write (above). A rejection with code "store" counts as taken (the write failed, also when the lock refused it: the session holds the change, and the entry moves with it). Code "locked" (nothing was changed) answers "locked", says nothing and keeps the entry. Every other code refuses the entry ("changed", "gone");
    10. moves the entry to the other list.
  - A refused entry goes away together with every other entry, of both lists, that holds one of the prompts that were in the way (`err.ids`): they all build on a state that is gone.
  - Notices (`M.app.toast`, id "undo", one at a time, 6 s): "<Action> undone · “<first line>”" with the button Redo, "<Action> redone · “…”" with the button Undo, and the detail line "<book or deck> · p. <page>"; "Nothing to undo" / "Nothing to redo", "That is undone already." and "That step cannot be undone any more." (2.5 s); "Not undone: the card changed after that step." ("one of its cards" for a group), "… its book or deck is deleted.", "… Marginalia could not save the change." (detail: the action and the first line), "Not redone: …" alike; "Wait until the import finishes, then try again."; "Save or cancel the editor of that card first." (with "It waits in another tab." after it); "Not undone: you made other changes after that one."; "Not during a review: the review has its own undo." (the review's key can be changed, so the notice names none). The actions: New prompt, New note, New highlight, Edit, Delete, Restore, Move, Flag, Unflag, Anchor change, "<n> new prompts", "<n> changes". The first line is the card as the person sees it after the command (48 characters at most; a leading "Q." is left out, a picture reads "[image]", a highlight shows its passage). A "locked", "role" or "modal" answer says nothing (the page says or shows that itself).
- **Keys** (`M.keys.UNDO_MAP`, "Key routing"): `M.app`'s keydown listener asks the map right after `M.tabs.handleKey` and before the focus owner's map. The map is `inField: false`: inside a text field the two keys are never taken, so they stay the undo and redo of the text. Outside one: while the settings are open or a dialog is up, the keys are left alone (no `preventDefault`); in a review Cmd+Z calls `M.review.undo()` and Shift+Cmd+Z does nothing; in every other place `M.undo.run(id)`, also while an editor is open (the key then comes from one of its buttons or from the page; `M.undo` leaves the card of that editor alone). A held key acts once (the repeats are taken and do nothing). The ? sheet has the section "Undo and redo" with the two keys. READER_MAP's `undoDelete` "z", the review's "u" and the "Undo" notices are unchanged.
- **Pictures** (js/attachments.js): the ids that `M.undo.texts()` names count as references (`referencedIds`), so `gc`, `discard` and `sweep` keep a picture that an undo or a redo of THIS page can bring back. The steps of another window of the Mac app are that page's own, so the sweep at the start of a page runs only in a window that starts alone (`M.app`: no other Web Lock "marginalia.window.<id>" is held, `M.store.held()`; when the locks cannot be asked in handover mode, the window does not sweep). A second window never sweeps. After a reload no step names a picture, and the next sweep of a window that starts alone takes it as before.
- **The views** follow through the bus events of the writers. Two additions: (a) the margin's and the list's own "Prompt deleted · Undo (Z)" notice goes, with its offer, when `prompt:restored` names its prompt (the prompt is back by Cmd+Z); (b) a card that holds the focus when a bus event takes it away (`prompt:deleted`, or a `prompt:saved` that moves it out of the book: Cmd+Z, Shift+Cmd+Z, the prompt list, another window) hands the focus to the reader (js/margin.js `dropCard`), while the margin's own delete still gives it to the next card.
- **`M.list.editingId()`** → the id of the prompt whose row holds the open editor (for the second half of a Split: the first half), or null. **`M.tabs.heldEditors()`** → the options of the prompt editors that wait with tabs not on screen.
- **The Mac app**: the page takes the two keys itself, as in a browser. The Edit menu of the Mac app is unchanged: Undo and Redo keep Electron's roles and their shortcuts, and act on the text field that has the focus. This is the second exception to "no menu shortcut takes a key the web app uses" (the tab commands are the first): outside a text field the page stops the key's default, so the menu's Undo does not run; inside one the page leaves the key alone, so the menu's Undo acts on the text. A click on Edit › Undo with the pointer acts on text only. Each window is a page with its own entries (D31).
- **The packaged self-test** (`desktop/lib/selftest.js`, check `undo-redo`): in its import phase the page of the built app edits one imported prompt as a step, takes the edit back (the cache and the stored record hold the old text), makes it again and takes it back once more, all through `M.cache` and `M.undo`; the prompt ends as the import left it.
- **Editors of other windows** are not asked (each window is a page of its own): a card that is open in an editor of another window can be taken back here, and that editor's later save starts from the record as it is then.
- **Not covered**: a book, a deck or a folder (their own notices stay), Settings, an import, an export, a review answer after its session, a copy in Obsidian (it changes only with "Update in Obsidian").
- **Tests**: marginalia/test/undo.test.js (the writers' steps, `stateKey`, `revertPrompts` and its one store write, groups, the limit, the refusals, what an editor holds, the notices and their buttons, the order of the commands, `idle()`), test/undo-dom.test.js (the 31 real modules: the keys, the deck page's Add as one step and the Undo of its notice, a question that is up, the ? sheet, a picture kept for a step), test/d31.test.js "undo and redo among the windows" (two pages of one origin: each its own steps, the role first, only a window that starts alone sweeps), test/d30.test.js (an editor that waits with its tab), test/margin-dom.test.js, test/list-dom.test.js, test/reader-dom.test.js and test/app.test.js (the views' steps, the focus, the old notices, the key routing), test/keys.test.js (`UNDO_MAP`), test/smoke.test.js (the module and its functions), desktop/test/menu.test.js (the Edit menu's roles and keys), desktop/test/selftest.test.js (the check `undo-redo`).
- **Checked on 2026-11-18**: `node --test` (web and desktop), the code checks, the headless boot and the Python tests pass. Each of 171 deliberate faults (one changed line of the code each) makes a test fail. A real page of the final code (a test server with a throwaway vault and backup folder) took an edit, a flag, a delete and an Add of two prompts back and made them again with the keys: in the prompt list, on a deck page and in the reader's margin. The files as git publishes them, without `.claude/launch.json`, were cloned into a new folder: there the same tests passed, `npm ci` ran, and `npm run app` built the app with every vendored file downloaded anew and passed the packaged self-test (40 checks, `undo-redo` among them).
- **Not verified on the real Mac**: Cmd+Z and Shift+Cmd+Z reaching the page of the installed app when no text field has the focus (Electron gives a key to the page first, and to the menu only when the page leaves it alone).
